# Publication

## Purpose

The publication feature turns approved revisions into immutable, cumulative static releases. It activates a release atomically. A separate static file server reads only published files.

## Decisions

- Only the owner publishes or activates a release. One exception exists: the worker stage `publishing` publishes as the code subject.
- A record is published at its current revision, and only when that revision is approved and has no unresolved concerns. Code can approve an action, a vote, or an executive action. People approve the other kinds. The worker and the owner publish all kinds.
- The `publishing` stage publishes every approved record of every kind, whoever approved it. A person approval needs no owner step. A person approval that replaces a published record needs a correction note. The editor writes the note when the person approves, and the approval decision keeps it. The worker leaves the record out and logs it in two cases. The approval has no note, or the record already has 20 corrections with a note. The other records still publish. A note on a first publication is dropped. A relationship or a brief lists only when its citations are current in the active release. A later wake publishes it when its citations are current.
- The stage wakes when the worker starts, about 60 seconds after an approval, and at each full hour. It also wakes when the gap of 10 minutes after the newest release ends. A wake makes at most one release. It publishes up to 64 ready records. Each of the seven kinds gets an equal share. A kind with fewer ready records gives its unused share to the others. A wake with nothing ready makes no release, except a status-only release on a wake that passed a full hour. A wake inside the gap makes no release. A kind alternates published replacements and new records, a replacement first, and replacements fill at most half of the kind while new records wait. A correction reaches readers soon. At the record cap, the batch shrinks to the free slots; see the record cap invariant.
- A read of one kind can hold rows that are not ready. One example is a brief with changed citations. The tick reads that kind again without those rows. It stops when the kind has enough ready records or no row is left. A not ready record may become ready after the next release, so only that listing leaves it out. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords.ListKindAsync` and `PublishedRecordLoader.ListReadyAsync`, which reports the IDs of the rows that it dropped. Test: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.ManyRowsThatAreNotReadyDoNotHideAReadyRecordBecauseTheReadRepeatsWithoutThem`.
- A publication conflict ends the tick. A race with an owner publish is normal, and the next tick builds again from the latest release. Any other failure is reported and counted as a stage failure.
- The stage waits while the active release is not the latest release. This is the state after `releases activate` rolls back. It resumes when the owner activates the latest release or publishes from the CLI.
- A record that fails validation while the stage loads it is left out of the batch and reported with its ID and message. The other records publish in one release. The next tick tries the record again.
- The action and vote pages say "From the official record, checked" with the approval time. They do not claim that a person approved the record. When `textOrigin` is `model`, they add "An AI summary of the official record". When it is `code`, they add "Wording: Written automatically from Congress.gov data, with no AI". An action page with tracked issues then also says "Issues: Chosen by an AI model from the bill title and subjects". Text that a person wrote gets no label. `ApprovedAtUtc` holds the time of the approval, by code or by a person. Enforced by `PublishedActionView` and `PublishedVoteView`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.ActionAndVotePagesSayThatTheRecordComesFromTheOfficialRecordInsteadOfClaimingAPersonApprovedIt`, `ReleaseRendererTests.ActionAndVotePagesLabelAnAiSummaryOnlyWhenAModelWroteTheText`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.AnActionPageLabelsCodeWrittenWordingWithItsSourceAndNeverCallsItAnAiSummary`.
- A newer draft never replaces published content automatically.
- A release is cumulative. It holds the base release records plus explicit additions or replacements.
- Each vote kind, executive action type, and cast word that has a definition maps to its term. `PublishedRecordLabels` enforces the map. `EveryVoteKindHasItsTermKeyOrNoneForOtherAndEveryKeyIsInTheGlossary`, `TheTypeOfAnExecutiveActionIsATermAndANoticeUsesThePresidentialNoticeDefinitionNotTheAgencyOne`, and `EveryCastWordMapsToItsTermAndACandidateNameHasNone` protect it.
- Carried records are hard links to the immutable files of the base release. Disk therefore grows with published records, not with the number of releases.
- Each publish rebuilds `release.json`, `officials.json`, `catalog.json`, and the home page. It rebuilds the all-records pages and the weekly briefs pages. It rebuilds `about.html`, the issue and policy area pages, the four board pages, and `search.html`. It also rebuilds the officials pages, `report.html`, `method.html`, `glossary.html`, every withdrawal notice, and the assets. It also writes again the page of each record that lists related records, when the new release holds a link that cites that record. Its record file stays the same hard link; see [related records](#related-records). It reads the previous manifest once, under the 64 MiB manifest cap, `PublicationProtocol.MaximumManifestBytes`. It reads the previous `officials.json` once, under `PublishedOfficials.MaximumBytes`. It rebuilds the files of an official only when the official is touched; see the invariants. `PublicationProtocol.MaximumRecordsPerRelease` is the one owner of the record limit, which is 40,000. The database has no count check, because C# owns the rule.
- Postgres records which release is active. The `current` link is only a copy for the static server, so a failed link swap never drops committed records.
- Every publication write takes all seven review locks in one fixed order, which prevents a deadlock. The order is in [advisory locks](../architecture.md#advisory-locks).
- A release has no status column. `is_active` marks the one active release: a commit sets it on the new row and clears the old one, and rollback moves it.
- Rollback is a CLI command only. The workspace has no rollback or activate control.
- The host keeps the files of the 30 highest release numbers and the active release. It also keeps the newest release of each of the last 7 UTC days. The database keeps every release row. Briefs, the release list, and the stale brief check read the rows. The [search](search.md) service reads release files over HTTP, so a pruned release on the host does not break it.
- At launch, the Jetson serves releases through Caddy and the Cloudflare tunnel on civic-lens.info. See [Web server](../operations.md#web-server).
- Publish stages and checks a release on the server. The active release changes only after the checks pass.
- Locally, a Caddy container serves the release root with `file_server { precompressed br gzip }`.
- Host renders the shared Razor components to static HTML, so publication needs no new project.
- Each manifest entry carries filters, so the facet pages and the search filters need no record file reads. Each published record type owns the mapping in its `ToEntry` method. A filter that the kind has no fact for stays empty; publish never guesses it.
- One loader owns each record kind: its ID shape, its ready list, and how publish loads and builds it. `PublishedRecordLoaders` lists them, and the publish and ready-list handlers go through that list. One loader owns the Application side of a kind. The other per-kind places are the `PublicationProtocol.IsRecordId` arm, the store state check, `PublishedRecordLabels.Kind`, the review link, and the `IReleaseRenderer` method.
- The loader base class gives each kind one owner for its Application rules. A non-generic base exists only so `PublishedRecordLoaders` can hold all record kinds in one list. The seven kinds are real variation, with different IDs, stores, and records. The precedents are `PostgresAnalysisStore<TRun>` and `ReviewRecordPage`.
- `PublishedRecordLoader<TCandidate, TAddition>` owns the ready-list and load steps once. A kind supplies only its store reads, its ready row, and how it builds and renders one record. `PublishedRecordRules.Cite` is the one owner of a published citation for every kind.
- Official names in every record kind come from the current `PublicationCatalog`, not from stored revisions. The action `policyAreaIds` filter comes from the policy area ID that the draft stored. Drafting resolved it through `IssueConfiguration.PolicyAreaIdFor`, and the reviewer approved it with the revision. Publish never reads the issue configuration. A label that the configuration lacked at draft time gives an empty filter.
- A record ID is unique across all kinds, because all kinds share the flat `records/{id}` paths.
- A brief record embeds no source text. It holds the wording that a person approved and a list of citations. Each citation names a published record by kind, ID, and revision, and carries a copy of the cited headline. A brief is a weekly summary over other records, so it publishes only when the new release holds every record that it cites; see [briefs](briefs.md#publication).
- An action record embeds the full text of each cited extraction once. Each citation names its extraction, and the contract checks the quote against that source.
- The latest action of the bill of an action record lives in `bill-status.json`, not in the record file or the manifest. A bill moves after approval, and a record revision stays frozen. The file holds one row for each action record of the release that has a stored status. A row has the record ID, the text, the date, and the time that Civic Lens read it. An action with no stored status has no row, and its page shows no status line. An older reader ignores the file. See [bill status line](#bill-status-line).
- A relationship record links one statement to one action or vote. It embeds no source text. It holds the wording that a person approved, the quote of the statement, and the fact of the target. Both are text. It also holds a citation of each side. A citation names a record by kind, ID, and revision, and carries a copy of the headline. A relationship publishes only when the new release holds both cited revisions and the latest decision on each is an approval. A link that a release already holds stays in later releases, whatever happens to the records that it cites. See [relationship record](#relationship-record).
- A vote record embeds the full text of each cited extraction once, as an action record does. Its citations are the vote day, the kind, the result facts, and the cast of each tracked member. The kind can be nomination, cloture, or motion to proceed. Only Senate votes use these three. They are new enum names, so they need no version bump. `PublishedEvidenceRules` owns the source and citation checks for actions and votes. A vote record also holds the raw result wording of the source, the totals of the whole roll call, and the majority of each party. See [Vote record](#vote-record).
- The manifest schema version is 1. A new record kind needs no version bump, because every reader ships with or after the release it reads. The publisher reads only releases of the current schema version. `app.js` and `public.js` ship inside each release. An older publisher fails loudly on the unknown kind.
- Release JSON uses relaxed escaping (`JavaScriptEncoder.UnsafeRelaxedJsonEscaping`), so non-ASCII letters and punctuation take UTF-8 bytes, not 6-byte `\uXXXX` escapes. This is safe because release JSON is fetched as JSON and never inlined in HTML or a script tag. Readers do not change.
- Unreviewed proposals, internal reviewer notes, operational job details, and mutation controls stay private.
- Public serving reads release contracts only. It never reads operational storage or private notes.
- The public UI is static pages that Caddy serves. Publish renders every public page, including the official, issue, policy area, and briefing pages. No server is on the public page path. Search and reader reports go to the search service.
- Search goes to the [search](search.md) service, which is also the public read API. Each release holds `search.html`, which calls it. The browser no longer searches the manifest.
- A release holds the member photos that its officials show. A photo file is named by the SHA-256 hash of the capture, not of the decoded image bytes: `photos/{hash}.jpg`. A name therefore never changes its bytes, and a changed photo gets a new name. App assets keep fixed names, because the renderer owns them. A hash name lets a CDN keep a photo for a long time.
- A publish writes the newest stored photo row of each official without `withholdPhoto`. It reads a capture only for a hash that the previous release lacks, and it links every other photo. Every publish path gets the artifact directory for this read: the worker, `releases publish`, and the review workspace. The directory is required: `releases publish` and the review workspace refuse to start without `CIVIC_LENS_ARTIFACT_DIRECTORY`.
- A new photo that cannot be read does not fail the publish. The profile keeps the photo of the previous release, and the file is linked. With no previous photo the official has no photo. Both cases log an error with the person and the attempt. A cancelled token, a database error, and a missing file in the previous release still fail the publish.
- A photo is only for the portrait on the official page and on the officials list. Photos are never used in ads.
- Each public page has one stable URL, so search engines can find it.
- Only a person withdraws a record. The owner gives `--withdraw <kind>:<id> <note>` to `releases publish`, or uses Take off site on the record editor. A request can have withdrawals and no records. The code subject withdraws nothing.
- A record can leave the release only when review blocked it. Its status is `ApprovalWithdrawn`, `ChangesRequested`, `AwaitingReviewWithConcerns`, or `Rejected`. The commit checks this under the review locks.
- A withdrawal removes the record from the manifest `records` list and adds a `PublishedWithdrawal` to the manifest `withdrawals` list. The release keeps no record file. The record page becomes a notice with no headline, body, or officials.
- A withdrawal never leaves a link to nothing. The publish refuses to withdraw a record while a relationship or a brief that stays in the release cites it. The owner withdraws the citing records in the same request.
- A person brings a withdrawn record back. The worker returns it when a person approved a higher revision with a correction note. A record whose newest approval has no note, or was made by code, stays withdrawn. The worker logs it and skips it. The owner can also run `releases publish <key> <kind>:<id>`, but only for a person approval. A code approval never returns a record, on any path. To free the record, a person withdraws the code approval and approves the revision again with a note.
- Every record page links to `report.html` with the record ID and revision in the query. The link shows once, below the record, and `PublishedRecordLabels.ReportRecordLink` owns its text. One static report page serves all records. The page posts to the [search](search.md) service, which stores the report. The reader sends no contact data, and the page stores nothing about the reader.
- The entries backfill takes the entry of a revision from the oldest kept folder whose release lies in the range of the state row. It compares the entry in every later kept folder of that range, and a difference blocks the row with the reason `foldersDiffer`. A kept folder of that range that omits the revision or lists another revision is a difference too. A kept folder holds `release.json`. A missing manifest file means the folder was retired.
- A mismatch skips the row with a reason and never fixes a value. The run goes on. The command exits with 1 when a row was skipped or an open range has no row. It also exits with 1 when the limit left a checked range without a row.
- The entries backfill takes no advisory lock. It relies on `PublishedEntries.InsertAsync`: `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and `RequireSameAsync` make a rerun and a race with a commit safe.
- One class, `ReleaseEntryComparison`, owns the rule that two entries agree outside the six new fields. `ReleaseEntryRewrite` and the entries backfill both call it.
- The entries backfill runs now, before the other backfills. It reports the counts that it cannot prove, and it does not wait for the other backfills.

## Invariants

Each line gives the rule, what enforces it, and the test that protects it. Cross-feature invariants are in [architecture](../architecture.md#invariants).

- Only an approved record with no open concern publishes. Enforced by `PublishedRecordRules.IsPublishable`, which each loader applies to its publish and its ready list. Tests: `PublishReleaseTests.RejectsDraftsThatAreNotCleanlyApproved`, `PublishActionTests.ActionThatIsNotApprovedIsRefusedBecauseNoModelOrDraftMaySpeakForTheSite`, `PublishVoteTests.AVoteWhoseRevisionNobodyApprovedIsRefusedBecauseAModelRevisionNeverPublishesAlone`, `PublishVoteTests.AVoteWithAnOpenConcernIsRefusedAndIsNotListed`, and `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AnActionThatReviewDoesNotApproveIsNotPublishedByATickBecauseOnlyAnApprovedRecordIsReady`.
- A release holds at most `PublicationProtocol.MaximumRecordsPerRelease` records, and the manifest stays under the manifest cap at that count. Enforced by the `PublishRelease` record count check and manifest size check. Test: `PublishReleaseTests.RejectsMoreRecordsThanTheCapBeforeAnythingIsBuilt`, `PublishReleaseTests.AReleaseAtTheRecordLimitOfFilledStatementEntriesKeepsTheManifestUnderThreeQuartersOfTheManifestCap`, `PublishReleaseTests.AReleaseAtTheRecordLimitOfFilledActionEntriesKeepsTheManifestUnderThreeQuartersOfTheManifestCap`, and `PublishReleaseTests.RefusesAManifestOverTheManifestCapAndStagesNothing`.
- Manifest filters hold at most `PublishedRecordEntry.MaximumFilterValues` distinct, nonblank values, each at most `PublishedRecordEntry.MaximumFilterValueLength` characters. Enforced by `PublishedRecordEntry.Validate`, which `PublicationRelease.Validate` calls. Test: `PublicationReleaseTests.FiltersAreBoundedAndDistinctSoOneEntryCannotSwellTheManifest`.
- An entry's filters come from the revision that the release publishes. Enforced by `PublishedDocumentChange.ToEntry`, `PublishedStatement.ToEntry`, and `PublishedAction.ToEntry`. Tests: `PublishStatementTests.EachKindFillsItsSearchFiltersFromTheRevisionItPublishes`, `PublishStatementTests.ARepublishedStatementReplacesItsCarriedEntryInsteadOfDuplicatingIt`, `PublishActionTests.AnActionFillsItsSearchFiltersFromTheRevisionItPublishesAndNamesOnlyItsSponsor`, and `PublishActionTests.ARepublishedActionReplacesItsCarriedEntryInsteadOfDuplicatingIt`.
- The table `publication_record_states` holds, for every release number, exactly the records that the release lists. Each row is a range of releases that hold one revision of one record. A commit compares its records with the state of the highest release, never the active release, because a rollback makes them differ. Enforced by `PublicationRecordStates.RecordAsync`, which `PostgresPublicationStore.CommitAsync` calls in the commit transaction, and by the migration `PublicationRecordStates` for earlier releases. The migration `DropPublicationRecordsJson` compares the table with `records_json` and aborts the drop on any difference. Prune and activate write nothing to the table. After it writes the rows, one count query reads the rows that cover the new release number, carried rows included. The commit rolls back when the count differs from the number of records that the release lists. The commit throws `PublicationStateMismatchException`, which is not a publication conflict, so the worker reports it as a stage failure. `PublishRelease` deletes the staged folder, and `releases publish` prints the counts. The check guards the writer and compares counts only, so it does not catch a wrong revision or drift in earlier releases. The column `publication_releases.records_json` no longer exists. The table is the only source of the records of a release. Readers use only the table: `ReviewQueries.ReadActiveReleaseRecordsAsync`, `ReviewQueries.ReadReleaseRecordsAsync`, and `BriefFirstRelease`. Tests: `PostgresPublicationRecordStateTests.AStateTableThatHoldsAnotherRowCountThanTheReleaseListsStopsThePublishAndKeepsTheActiveRelease`, `PublishReleaseTests.AStateMismatchDeletesTheBuiltDirectoryBecauseNoReleaseWasCommitted`, `ReleaseCommandTests.AStateMismatchExitsWithOneAndPrintsItsCountsInsteadOfTheGenericFailure`, `PostgresPublicationRecordStateTests.TheTableMatchesTheRecordsOfEveryReleaseAfterWithdrawalReturnRevisionChangeAndRollback`, `PostgresPublicationRecordStateTests.AReplayWritesNoRowsBecauseTheReleaseAlreadyExists`, `PostgresPublicationRecordStateTests.AFailureInsideTheStateWritesLeavesNoReleaseAndNoClosedRangeBecauseTheyShareOneTransaction`, `PostgresPublicationRecordStateTests.PruneLeavesEveryReleaseRowAndStateRowAloneBecauseOldStatesStayForReaders`, `PublishedRecordReferenceTests.TheKindSpellingMatchesTheWireSpellingOfPublishedRecords`, `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.TheStateTableMatchesEveryReleaseForTheCamelCaseKindsOfADocumentChangeAndAnExecutiveAction`, `PublicationRecordStatesMigrationTests.TheBackfillRebuildsEveryReleaseFromRecordsJsonWithLegacyEntriesGapsAndReturnedRecords`, `PublicationRecordStatesMigrationTests.TheDropRemovesRecordsJsonAndKeepsEveryStateRowBecauseTheTableIsTheOnlySourceAfterIt`, `PublicationRecordStatesMigrationTests.TheRollbackRebuildsRecordsJsonOfEveryReleaseFromTheStateTableAndTheDropRunsAgain`, `PublicationRecordStatesMigrationTests.TheDropFailsAndKeepsRecordsJsonWhenAStateRowDiffersFromTheReleasesBecauseTheDataCannotComeBack`, and `BriefStalenessTests.ACommitAfterARollbackLeavesEveryReleaseAnsweringItsOwnRevisionBecauseTheDiffIsAgainstTheHighestRelease`.
- The table `published_records` is the only owner of the first publication time of a record. A record has one first time, also after a rollback. Enforced by `PublishedRecords`, the primary key on kind and record ID, and `PublishRelease`, which reads the time before it builds. The commit also calls `PublishedRecords.RequireStoredTimesAsync`. It throws `PublicationConflictException` when a stored time differs from the time in the release. Tests: `PublishFirstPublishedTests.ARecordPublishedAgainAfterARollbackKeepsItsFirstTimeInItsFileItsManifestEntryAndTheTable`, `PostgresPublishedRecordsTests.ARollbackAndAPublishOfTheRecordAgainKeepTheFirstTimeBecauseTheTableHasInsertsOnly`, and `PostgresPublishedRecordsTests.ACommitWhoseTimeDiffersFromTheStoredTimeConflictsAndWritesNoReleaseBecauseTheReleaseFileWouldSplitTheFirstTime`.
- No code updates or deletes a row of `published_records`. The commit inserts a row for each added record with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, in the commit transaction. Enforced by `PublishedRecords`, the one writer. Test: `PublishedRecordsInsertOnlyTests.NoProductionCodeUpdatesOrDeletesTheFirstPublicationTimesAndOnlyTheInsertTouchesTheTable`. It scans the production source, because no other test fits a write-only table.
- A publish that adds a record of the active release, and finds no stored time for it, stops with an error. The error tells the operator to run `releases first-published backfill`. Enforced by `PublishRelease.RequireFirstPublished`. Test: `PublishFirstPublishedTests.ARecordThatTheActiveReleaseHoldsButTheTableLacksRefusesThePublishUntilTheBackfillRuns`.
- A row of `published_records` has a known kind, a record ID of 32 or 64 lower hex characters, and a time inside the tick range. Enforced by the checks `ck_published_records_kind`, `ck_published_records_record_id`, and `ck_published_records_first_published_at_utc_ticks`. Test: `PostgresPublishedRecordsTests.TheTableRefusesAnUnknownKindABadRecordIdAndATimeOutsideTheTickRange`.
- An entry row holds only the facts that its published revision fixes. Views compute the derived values. Another owner keeps each value that the row leaves out. The configuration owns the name of an official. The member list and the party data give the yea count, the nay count, and the alignment of a vote. A view reads the cosponsors from the sponsorships of the action revision that the row names. The commit writes one row of `published_entries` for each record revision that it adds, in one statement. It also writes child rows for the official IDs, issues, and policy areas. All rows belong to the commit transaction. The manifest is built and written separately, and no reader uses the rows yet. A commit of an earlier release wrote no rows. `releases entries backfill` fills them; see the entry backfill invariants below. Enforced by `PublishedEntries.InsertAsync`, which `PostgresPublicationStore.CommitAsync` calls after `PublishedRecords`. Tests: `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.ACommitWritesTheEntryAndKeepsTheManifestOrderOfEveryArrayBecauseTheReleaseFileMustBeRebuiltByteForByte`, `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.AVoteEntryAndAnExecutiveActionEntryWriteTheirKindAndTheirEnumColumns`, and `PublishedReleaseOutlivesDatabaseTests.PublishedReleaseServesFromFilesAloneAfterTheDatabaseIsGone`, which compares the rows with the manifest entry except the names.
- No code updates or deletes a row of the entry tables. The insert uses `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. When the row exists, `PublishedEntries.RequireSameAsync` reads the stored facts and compares them with the facts of the release entry. A difference throws `PublicationConflictException` and the release is not written. One revision therefore has one entry, also after a rollback. A change of a configured name, or `--rewrite-entries`, cannot conflict, because the rows hold no name and no vote figure. Enforced by `PublishedEntries`, the one writer. Tests: `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.APublishOfTheSameRevisionWithADifferentEntryConflictsAndWritesNoReleaseBecauseOneRevisionHasOneEntry`, `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.APublishOfTheSameRevisionWithADifferentOfficialNameIsAcceptedBecauseTheTableHoldsNoNames`, `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.ARollbackAndAPublishOfTheSameRevisionAgainKeepTheOneRowBecauseTheEntryIsInsertOnly`, `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.APublishOfAnOlderActionRevisionAgainWithABillLineSucceedsBecauseTheRowHoldsNoLine`, and `PublishedEntriesInsertOnlyTests.NoProductionCodeUpdatesOrDeletesAnEntryRowAndOnlyTheWriterTouchesTheTables`. The last test scans the production source.
- An entry row names exactly one revision row of its own kind, and that revision exists. Enforced by the check `ck_published_entries_revision_key` and the seven foreign keys to the revision tables. Tests: `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.TheTableRefusesARowThatBreaksOneRule` and `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.ARevisionWithNoRevisionRowIsRefusedByTheKeyAndTheReleaseIsNotWritten`.
- An entry row mirrors `PublishedRecordEntry.Validate` for the columns that it holds. Only an action holds a bill title. Only an executive action holds a document type. Only a relationship holds the four link columns. Enforced by the checks `ck_published_entries_bill_title`, `ck_published_entries_document_type`, and `ck_published_entries_link`, and by the kind, ID, headline, and host checks. Test: `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.TheTableRefusesARowThatBreaksOneRule`. The limits on the number of values stay in C#.
- A relationship entry has exactly one matched official, and only a relationship entry has one. The matched official is the row of `published_entry_officials` with `is_matched` set. Enforced by the partial unique index `ux_published_entry_officials_matched`, which allows at most one. The check `ck_published_entry_officials_matched_kind` allows a matched row for a relationship only. `PublishedRecordEntry.Validate` requires the matched ID to be one of the officials, so at least one row is set. The statement and target are published records, enforced by two foreign keys to `published_records`. The commit checks them first and throws `FirstPublishedMissingException` with the backfill message when one has no row. Tests: `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.ASecondMatchedOfficialInOneEntryIsRefusedBecauseARelationshipMatchesOneOfficial`, `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.AMatchedOfficialOfAnEntryThatIsNotARelationshipIsRefused`, `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.ARelationshipEntryWritesOneMatchedFlagForEachOfficialBecauseOnlyTheSecondOfThreeMatched`, `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.ARelationshipWhoseTargetHasNoFirstPublicationTimeIsRefusedWithTheBackfillMessageAndNoReleaseIsWritten`, `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.ARelationshipEntryThatNamesARecordNobodyPublishedIsRefusedByTheRecordKey`, and `PublishedRelationshipTests.ARelationshipEntryRefusesALinkThatIsNotOneStatementAndOneBillOrVote`, which refuses a matched ID outside the officials.
- The values of one array of an entry are distinct, have no blank value, and keep the manifest order in the column `position`. Enforced by the keys and checks of the three child tables. Tests: `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.TheChildTablesRefuseARowThatBreaksOneRule` and `PostgresPublishedEntriesTests.TwoChildRowsWithTheSameValueInOneEntryAreRefusedBecauseTheArraysHoldDistinctValues`.
- The backfill inserts the first time of each record and withdrawal of the active manifest, and it never changes a row. Enforced by `BackfillFirstPublished` and `PublishedRecords.InsertAsync`. Tests: `PublishFirstPublishedTests.TheBackfillCopiesEveryRecordOfTheActiveManifestOnceAndCountsWhatWasAlreadyThere` and `PostgresPublishedRecordsTests.TheBackfillInsertsMissingRowsCountsThemAndLeavesAnExistingRowAlone`.
- The entries backfill writes a row only after two checks. The record file of the oldest kept folder of the range must agree with the manifest entry outside the six new fields. The entry in every later kept folder of the range must agree too, and each of those folders must list the revision. A failed check skips the row with the reason `recordFileInvalid`, `recordFileDiffers`, `foldersDiffer`, or `manifestInvalid`. A record or a linked record with no `published_records` row gets the reason `firstPublishedMissing`. Enforced by `BackfillPublishedEntries` and `ReleaseEntryComparison`. Tests: `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ABackfillFillsAnEarlierRevisionFromTheOldestKeptFolderAndWritesTheFactsThatACommitWritesIntoTheTables`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AManifestEntryThatDiffersFromItsRecordFileOrWhoseFileIsInvalidOrMissingIsSkippedWithItsReasonAndNoRowIsWritten`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.TwoKeptFoldersThatHoldOneRevisionWithDifferentFactsBlockTheRowBecauseTheBackfillNeverPicksAValue`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AKeptFolderInTheRangeThatOmitsTheRevisionOrListsAnotherOneBlocksTheRowEvenWhenALaterFolderHoldsIt`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ARecordWithNoFirstPublicationTimeIsSkippedWithItsReasonBecauseTheEntryRowNeedsThatTime`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AFolderThatListsARevisionAfterTheFirstFolderSetTheEntryButOmitsItLaterStillBlocksTheRowEvenWhenTheNewestFolderHoldsItAgain`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ARelationshipWhoseTargetHasNoFirstPublicationTimeIsSkippedWithItsReasonAndIsWrittenOnceTheTimeExists`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AReleaseThatACommitAddsBetweenTheTwoReadsNeverMakesTheOlderRevisionSkipAsFoldersDiffer`.
- The entries backfill reads the release list before the candidates. A commit between the reads then leaves a range that no listed folder covers, so it never causes a false `foldersDiffer`. It reads each kept manifest at most once for a run, from the oldest release to the newest, and one at a time. It writes only through `PublishedEntries.InsertAsync`, in batches of 25 rows. Each batch is one transaction, and the limit counts inserted rows. A rerun adds nothing. A failure in a later batch keeps the earlier batches. Enforced by `BackfillPublishedEntries`, `PostgresPublicationStore.AddEntriesAsync`, and `PublishedEntriesInsertOnlyTests`. Tests: `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ARerunAddsNothingReadsNoManifestAndCountsTheRowsAlreadyPresent`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ALimitBoundsTheInsertedRowsAndTheNextRunContinuesWhereItStopped`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AFailureInALaterBatchKeepsTheRowsOfTheEarlierBatchOf25`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AReleaseThatACommitAddsBetweenTheTwoReadsNeverMakesTheOlderRevisionSkipAsFoldersDiffer`, and `PublishedEntriesInsertOnlyTests.NoProductionCodeUpdatesOrDeletesAnEntryRowAndOnlyTheWriterTouchesTheTables`.
- A commit that inserts the same row between the candidate read and the batch insert does not break the run. The insert counts the row as present. A commit that inserts a row with other facts stops the run with `PublicationConflictException` and keeps the commit row. The backfill message tells the operator to compare the row with the kept folders, and the commit message says to rebuild the release. Enforced by `PublishedEntries.InsertAsync`. Tests: `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ARowThatACommitInsertsBetweenTheCandidateReadAndTheInsertIsCountedAsPresentAndNotWrittenTwice` and `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ARowThatACommitInsertsWithOtherFactsStopsTheRunAndStaysAsTheCommitWroteIt`, and `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AConflictWithARowThatACommitWroteTellsTheOperatorToCompareTheRowAndNotToRebuildTheRelease`.
- A withdrawal never yields an entry row. A returned revision has its own range and gets its row from its own folder. A closed range that no kept folder lists gets no row and counts in `closedRangesWithoutKeptFolder`. An open range without a row counts in `openRangesWithoutRow`. A range that passed every check but got no row, open or closed, counts in `readyRangesWithoutRow`. The command exits with 1 while either count is above 0. Enforced by `BackfillPublishedEntries` and `ReleaseCommand`. Tests: `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AWithdrawalNeverYieldsAnEntryRowAndAReturnedRevisionIsFilledFromItsFolder`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ARevisionThatOnlyARetiredFolderHeldGetsNoRowAndCountsAsAClosedRangeWithoutAKeptFolderWithoutFailingTheCommand`, `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.AnOpenRangeThatNoKeptFolderHoldsIsReportedAndTheCommandExitsWithOne`, and `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ALimitThatStopsBeforeAReadyClosedRangeLeavesItCountedAndTheCommandExitsWithOneUntilARerunWritesIt`, and `BackfillPublishedEntriesTests.ASkippedRowMakesTheCommandExitWithOneAndTheOtherRowsAreStillWritten`.
- Only the owner runs the entries backfill. Enforced by `ReviewAuthorization.RequireOwner` in `BackfillPublishedEntries`. Test: `ReleaseCommandTests.OnlyTheOwnerMayRunTheEntriesBackfillBecauseItWritesPublishedFactsAndTheCommandRefusesAReviewer`.
- `releases entries verify` rebuilds `release.json` of every kept folder from the tables and compares the bytes with the file. The state rows give the records. The entry rows, their child rows, and `published_records` give the facts and the first times. The withdrawal rows give the withdrawals. The file gives only what the tables leave out. These are the official names, the six optional entry fields, `publishedAtUtc`, and the withdrawals of a release that no withdrawal row covers. The command orders the records and withdrawals with `ReleaseRecordOrder` and writes them with `PublicationProtocol.JsonOptions`. Enforced by `VerifyPublishedEntries` and `ReleaseSplice`. Tests: `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AReleaseThatTheTablesRebuildByteForByteWithTheOrderAndTheNamesOfItsFilePassesAndTheRunWritesNothing`, `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AnEntryValueThatChangedInTheRowFailsTheReleaseBecauseTheFileNoLongerMatchesTheTablesAndNamesTheRecord`, and `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AWithdrawalThatItsRowsRebuildPassesAndAChangedNoteFailsBecauseTheRowsAreThenTheOwnerOfTheWithdrawals`. The bill line comes from the file, as `chamber` does. Test: `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AnActionEntryWithABillLineRebuildsByteForByteBecauseTheSpliceTakesTheLineFromTheFile`.
- The verify command first reads the file and writes it again with no change. A file that the serializer writes in other bytes fails with `manifestNotReproducible` before any table is read. A serializer change is then never blamed on the tables. Enforced by `VerifyPublishedEntries`. Test: `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AFileThatTheSerializerWouldWriteDifferentlyFailsBeforeAnyTableIsComparedSoASerializerChangeIsNeverBlamedOnTheTables`.
- The state rows of a release and the entries of its manifest name the same revisions. Each state row has its entry row and its first publication time. A break fails the release and names the record. The reasons are `entryRowMissing`, `firstPublishedMissing`, `stateRowNotListed`, and `entryOutsideRange`. Enforced by `ReleaseMembership`. Tests: `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AStateRowWithNoEntryRowFailsTheReleaseBecauseTheFutureForeignKeyNeedsEveryRangeToHaveItsRow`, `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AnEntryOutsideItsReleaseRangeFailsTheReleaseBecauseTheStateTableWouldHideARecordThatTheFileServes`, and `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AStateRowThatTheFileDoesNotListFailsTheReleaseBecauseTheTablesWouldServeARecordThatWasNeverPublished`.
- The verify command skips and reports, and it never fixes a value. It exits with 1 when any release fails. A folder without a manifest counts as retired, and an unreadable manifest fails with `manifestInvalid`. A release that lists withdrawals without withdrawal rows is legacy when it is older than the first release with a withdrawal row. It is also legacy when no withdrawal row exists. A newer release with such withdrawals fails with `bytesDiffer`. Legacy releases are counted apart from the passed releases and do not change the exit code. Enforced by `VerifyPublishedEntries` and `ReleaseCommand`. Tests: `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.ARetiredFolderIsCountedApartAndAnUnreadableManifestFailsBecauseNeitherCanBeProvedFromTheTables`, `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AReleaseWhoseWithdrawalsOnlyTheFileHoldsIsCountedAsLegacyAndDoesNotFailTheRunBecauseEarlyWithdrawalsHaveNoRows`, and `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AReleaseAfterTheFirstWithdrawalRowThatListsWithdrawalsWithoutRowsFailsBecauseOnlyOlderReleasesMayLackThem`.
- The verify command writes nothing and takes no advisory lock. It reads the rows of one release in one repeatable read transaction, so the state, entry, and withdrawal rows come from one snapshot. It reads each manifest once, one at a time. Enforced by `PostgresPublicationStore.ReadReleaseAsync`. Test: `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.AReleaseThatTheTablesRebuildByteForByteWithTheOrderAndTheNamesOfItsFilePassesAndTheRunWritesNothing`.
- Only the owner runs the verify command. Enforced by `ReviewAuthorization.RequireOwner` in `VerifyPublishedEntries`. Test: `VerifyPublishedEntriesTests.OnlyTheOwnerMayVerifyBecauseTheReportNamesUnpublishedRecordsAndTheCommandRefusesAReviewer`.
- The tables `publication_withdrawals` and `publication_withdrawal_corrections` hold, for every release committed after the migration `PublicationWithdrawals`, exactly the withdrawals that the manifest of the release lists. Each withdrawal row is a range of releases, like a state row. A commit compares its withdrawals with the open rows, never with the active release, because a rollback makes them differ. A row opens when the withdrawal appears. It closes when the record returns or a rollback drops it. A second withdrawal of the same record opens a new row. The corrections of a withdrawal keep the order of the manifest in the column `position`. After it writes the rows, the commit counts the rows that cover the new release. It throws `PublicationStateMismatchException` when the count differs from the withdrawals that the release lists. Enforced by `PublicationWithdrawalRows.RecordAsync`, which `PostgresPublicationStore.CommitAsync` calls in the commit transaction. The release file still comes from the staged manifest. No reader uses the tables yet. Withdrawals made earlier have no rows; see the first Limits line on the withdrawal tables. Tests: `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.TheRowsOfTheActiveReleaseRebuildTheManifestWithdrawalsWithTheirNotesAndCorrectionOrder`, `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.ACarriedWithdrawalKeepsItsOneRowAndALaterReleaseAddsNoRow`, `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.AReturnClosesTheRangeAndASecondWithdrawalOpensANewRow`, `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.AWithdrawalOfARolledBackReleaseClosesAtTheHighestReleaseSoItNeverCoversTheNextRelease`, `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.AReplayWritesNoSecondRowBecauseTheReleaseAlreadyExists`, and `PublishWithdrawalsTests.EveryCommitCarriesTheWithdrawalsOfItsManifestSoTheStoreRowsMatchTheFile`.
- No code deletes a row of the withdrawal tables or updates a correction row. The one update sets `last_release` of an open withdrawal row. Enforced by `PublicationWithdrawalRows`, the only writer. Test: `PublicationWithdrawalsInsertOnlyTests.OnlyOneClassWritesTheWithdrawalTablesAndTheOnlyUpdateClosesARange`. It scans the production source.
- A withdrawal row names a record that `published_records` holds and a release that exists. Its range ends at or after it starts. Its revision is at least 1. Its note has 1 to `PublishedCorrection.MaximumNoteLength` characters and is not only whitespace. A correction row has a position of 0 or more and a replaced revision of at least 1. Its note is null, or it has 1 to the same limit and is not only whitespace. Only a row with no note can say that the facts did not change. Postgres text cannot hold NUL, so the database needs no NUL check. Enforced by the checks `ck_publication_withdrawals_range`, `ck_publication_withdrawals_revision`, `ck_publication_withdrawals_note`, `ck_publication_withdrawal_corrections_position`, `ck_publication_withdrawal_corrections_revision`, `ck_publication_withdrawal_corrections_note`, and `ck_publication_withdrawal_corrections_facts_unchanged`, the tick checks, and the foreign keys. C# keeps the rules that need two tables. The withdrawal time is not before the first publication time. Each replaced revision is older than the withdrawn revision. `PublishedWithdrawal.Validate` owns them. `PublishedCorrection.ValidateAll` owns the other list rules: the order, at most 20 noted entries, and one code update of each kind in a run. Tests: `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.TheWithdrawalTableRefusesEachBrokenRule`, `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.TheWithdrawalNoteLimitIsTheContractLimit`, `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.TheWithdrawalTableRefusesARecordThatWasNeverPublishedAndAnUnknownRelease`, `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.TheCorrectionTableRefusesEachBrokenRule`, and `PostgresPublicationWithdrawalTests.TheCorrectionTableRefusesAWithdrawalThatDoesNotExistAndADuplicatePosition`.
- A record ID is unique across all kinds in a manifest. Enforced by `PublicationRelease.Validate`. Tests: `PublicationReleaseTests.ARecordIdIsUniqueAcrossKindsBecauseAllKindsShareOneRecordFilePath` and `PublishActionTests.AnActionAndAStatementCannotShareOneIdBecauseTheyWouldShareOneRecordFile`.
- A record ID has the shape of its kind. Enforced by each loader's `ValidateId` for requests and by `PublicationProtocol.IsRecordId` for files. Test: `PublishStatementTests.RecordIdMustMatchTheIdShapeOfItsKindSoAKindCannotBeMislabelled`.
- Each action citation quotes the embedded source that it names, and each embedded source is cited. Enforced by `PublishedAction.Validate`. Tests: `PublishedActionTests.RejectsACitationWhoseQuoteMatchesAnotherSourceButNotTheOneItNames`, `PublishedActionTests.RejectsACitationThatNamesNoEmbeddedSource`, and `PublishedActionTests.RejectsASourceThatNoCitationNamesBecauseTheFileEmbedsOnlyCitedEvidence`.
- An action file holds 1 to `PublishedAction.MaximumSources` sources and 1 to `PublishedAction.MaximumSponsorships` sponsorships. Enforced by `PublishedAction.Validate`. Tests: `PublishedActionTests.RequiresOneToEightSourcesSoTheRecordFileStaysBounded` and `PublishedActionTests.RequiresOneToSixHundredSponsorships`.
- The contract limits for actions equal the Core limits. No enforcer; the constants are separate copies. Test: `PublishedActionTests.ContractLimitsEqualTheCoreLimits`.
- An action sponsorship is never withdrawn before it began. Enforced by `ActionDraftRevision.ValidateShape` and, at the trust boundary, `PublishedAction.Validate`. Tests: `ActionDraftRevisionTests.ASponsorshipCannotBeWithdrawnBeforeItBegan` and `PublishedActionTests.ASponsorshipCannotBeWithdrawnBeforeItBegan`.
- Each vote citation quotes the embedded source that it names, and each embedded source is cited. Enforced by `PublishedVote.Validate` through `PublishedEvidenceRules`. Tests: `PublishedVoteTests.RejectsACitationWhoseQuoteMatchesAnotherSourceButNotTheOneItNames`, `PublishedVoteTests.RejectsACitationThatDoesNotQuoteItsSourceExactly`, `PublishedVoteTests.RejectsACitationThatNamesNoEmbeddedSource`, and `PublishedVoteTests.RejectsASourceThatNoCitationNamesBecauseTheFileEmbedsOnlyCitedEvidence`.
- A published fact equals its cited quote, and a cast is the last field of its cited member line. Enforced by `PublishedVote.Validate`. Tests: `PublishedVoteTests.AFactMustEqualItsCitedQuoteSoNoFactStatesMoreThanTheSourceSays` and `PublishedVoteTests.ACastMustBeTheWordsItsCitationShowsSoNoVoteIsPublishedThatTheLineDoesNotState`.
- A published vote result equals the text of a cited result line. The totals are non-negative. The party majorities name distinct parties, and their Yea and Nay counts add up to the totals. Each member names a party that has a majority entry. A vote with candidate total facts and no party total facts, such as the election of the Speaker, has no totals and no party majorities. `PublishedVote.IsCandidateVote` owns this test. Its casts are candidate names. Its members still name a bounded party. `PublishedVote.ResultPrefix`, `PublishedVote.PartyTotalPrefix`, and `PublishedVote.CandidateTotalPrefix` own the fact wording, and `VoteFactPrefixes` refers to them. Enforced by `PublishedVote.Validate`. Tests: `PublishedVoteTests.RejectsAResultThatNoCitedResultLineHolds`, `PublishedVoteTests.RejectsPartyMajoritiesThatDoNotAddUpToTheTotals`, `PublishedVoteTests.RejectsAMemberWhoseNamedPartyHasNoMajorityEntry`, `PublishedVoteTests.ACandidateTotalVoteHasNoTotalsAndNoPartyMajorities`, `PublishedVoteTests.ACandidateTotalVoteWithTotalsIsRefusedBecauseItsCastsAreNotYeaOrNay`, `PublishedVoteTests.ACandidateTotalVoteWithPartyMajoritiesIsRefused`, `PublishedVoteTests.AVoteWithNoCandidateTotalsRequiresTotals`, `PublishedVoteTests.ACandidateTotalVoteStillNamesABoundedPartyForEachMember`, `PublishedVoteTests.AVoteWithPartyTotalsAndACandidateLineStillRequiresTotals`.
- A party majority is the cast of more members of the party. Only Yea and Nay count, and Aye counts as Yea and No as Nay. Present and Not Voting stay out. Equal counts give no majority. `PublishedPartyMajority.Of` and `Validate` own the majority. `PublishedOfficialVote.SideOf` owns the mapping of Aye to Yea and of No to Nay. `VoteMemberLine.CastOf` and the vote rows both call it. Tests: `PublishedVoteTests.ATiedPartyHasNoMajoritySoNeitherCastCountsAsFollowingIt`, `PublishedVoteTests.RejectsAMajorityThatDoesNotMatchItsCounts`, `PublishVoteTests.PresentAndNotVotingStayOutOfTheMajority`.
- The totals come from the saved lines: the House party total lines, or the Senate count line. The saved member lines must add up to them, in total and for each source party letter of the House. A vote does not publish when it fails this check. It also does not publish when it has no result line, no party total lines, or a party total line with no counts. A House vote with candidate total lines and no party total lines is the exception: it publishes with no totals and no majorities. `VoteTally` throws an `ArgumentException`, which the loader tags with the record, as for a member without a configured name. Tests: `PublishVoteTests.AMemberLineThatDisagreesWithTheTotalLinesRefusesTheVoteAndStagesNothing`, `PublishVoteTests.AHouseVoteWithNoPartyTotalLinesRefusesBecauseItHasNoTotalsToShow`, `PublishVoteTests.APartyTotalLineWithNoCountsRefusesTheVoteBySayingWhichVote`, `PublishVoteTests.ASpeakerElectionPublishesItsCandidateTotalsWithNoTotalsAndNoPartyMajorities`, `PublishVoteTests.AVoteWithPartyTotalsAndACandidateLineKeepsEveryCheckAndRefusesAMismatch`, `PublishVoteTests.VoteviewSenatorsTakeTheRosterPartyByBioguideIdEvenWhenTheMemberLineNamesAnotherParty`, `PublishVoteTests.HouseLinesFromTheRealRenderersPublishWithTheirTotalsAndParties`, `PublishVoteTests.VoteviewLinesFromTheRealRendererPublishWithTheirTotalsAndParties`, `PublishVoteTests.EachHouseCountLabelFromTheRealRendererPublishesIntoItsOwnTotal`, `PublishVoteTests.EachVoteviewCountLabelFromTheRealRendererPublishesIntoItsOwnTotal`.
- The party of a voter comes from three steps, in this order. First, the caucus, else the party, of the roster term of the vote chamber that covers the vote day. Second, the party that the letter on the member line of the vote names: R Republican, D Democrat, I Independent. Third, `Unknown`. A member is never dropped. When two terms of the chamber cover the day, the term with the later start wins. A term that starts on the day another ends takes that day. The roster holds current members only, so a member who left uses the letter. `VoteParties` owns the order and `VoteMemberLine.SourcePartyName` owns the letter mapping. Tests: `PublishVoteTests.AnIndependentWhoCaucusesWithDemocratsCountsAsDemocrat`, `PublishVoteTests.AMemberWhoSwitchedPartyCountsWithTheTermThatCoversTheVoteDay`, `PublishVoteTests.OnTheDayOneTermEndsAndTheNextStartsTheNewTermWins`, `PublishVoteTests.WhenTermsOverlapTheOneThatStartsLatestWins`, `PublishVoteTests.AHouseVoteUsesTheHouseTermOfAMemberWhoHoldsASenateTermOnTheSameDay`, `PublishVoteTests.AMemberWhoLeftTheRosterTakesThePartyOfTheSourceLetter`, `PublishVoteTests.AMemberWhoLeftTheRosterAndHasAnUnknownLetterCountsUnderUnknown`.
- A published vote names its data source, and a source that is not defined is refused. A vote file with no `source` reads as `congressGov`, because every vote before the field was a House vote. The record page says the source in plain words. It links the official roll call page of the chamber. A Voteview vote says that Voteview stores each vote as a number code and that Civic Lens shows the code as a word. It also says that Voteview lists no Vice President tie-break vote. Enforced by `PublishedVote.Validate`, `PublishedVoteBuilder`, `PublishedRecordLabels.VoteSource`, and `PublishedVoteView`. Tests: `PublishedVoteTests.AVoteFileFromBeforeTheSourceFieldReadsAsCongressGovBecauseEveryOlderVoteWasAHouseVote`, `PublishedVoteTests.ARecordWithAnUnknownDataSourceIsRefusedSoThePageNeverNamesASourceThatDoesNotExist`, `PublishVoteTests.TheRecordNamesTheDataSourceOfItsRevisionSoAReaderKnowsWhoseDataTheCastsRestOn`, `ReleaseRendererTests.TheVotePageNamesItsDataSourceAndLinksTheOfficialRollCallPageSoAReaderCanCheckItAtTheChamber`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.AVoteviewVoteSaysThatVoteviewListsNoVicePresidentTieBreakVoteSoAFiftyFiftyResultIsNotMisread`.
- A published vote kind other than Other has a citation. Enforced by `PublishedVote.Validate`. Test: `PublishedVoteTests.AKindOtherThanOtherNeedsItsCitationAndOtherNeedsNone`.
- A vote file holds 1 to `PublishedVote.MaximumSources` sources and 1 to `PublishedVote.MaximumMembers` members, each official once. A headline, a bill type, and a cast stay within `PublishedVote.MaximumHeadlineLength`, `MaximumBillTypeLength`, and `MaximumCastLength`, which `PublishedOfficialVote` also uses. Enforced by `PublishedVote.Validate`. Tests: `PublishedVoteTests.ACastOverTheLimitIsRefusedSoNoOfficialVoteRowCanFailLaterAtStaging`, `PublishedVoteTests.ABillTypeOrHeadlineOverTheLimitIsRefusedBecauseTheOfficialVoteRowCapsThem`, `PublishedVoteTests.RequiresOneToThreeSourcesSoTheRecordFileStaysBounded`, `PublishedVoteTests.RequiresOneToSixHundredMembers`, and `PublishedVoteTests.ARecordListsEachOfficialOnceSoTheFilterNeverRepeatsAnId`.
- The contract limits for votes equal the Core limits. No enforcer; the constants are separate copies. Test: `PublishedVoteTests.ContractLimitsEqualTheCoreLimits`.
- The vote day is the date of the cited start line, which keeps the offset of the API. Enforced by `PublishedVote.Validate`. Test: `PublishedVoteTests.TheVoteDayIsTheDateOfTheCitedStartLineSoALateVoteKeepsItsHouseDayAcrossUtcMidnight`.
- The vote page links to the action record of the bill only when the caller passes its ID. Otherwise it shows the bill as text. The loader passes an ID only when the release holds that action record, and the action loader runs before the vote loader. Enforced by `PublishedVotePage`, `VoteRecordLoader.BillActionRecordIn`, and the loader order in `PublishedRecordLoaders`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.TheVotePageLinksToTheBillActionOnlyWhenTheReleaseHoldsThatAction`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AVoteWithoutABillShowsNoBillPanel`, `PublishVoteTests.TheBillLinksToTheActionRecordOfTheReleaseWhenTheActionIsPublishedEarlier`, `PublishVoteTests.TheBillLinksToAnActionAddedInTheSameReleaseBecauseActionsLoadBeforeVotes`, `PublishVoteTests.TheBillShowsAsTextWhenTheActionRecordIsNotInTheRelease`, `PublishVoteTests.TheLinkUsesTheActionThatTheReleaseHoldsWhenSeveralSourcesDraftedTheSameBill`, and `PostgresHouseVoteTests.PublicationReadReturnsTheApprovedVoteWithItsExtractionsAndTheActionRecordsOfTheSameBillOnly`.
- The vote entry names no official, has no policy area filter, and dates the vote by its day. Enforced by `PublishedVote.ToEntry`. Test: `PublishedVoteTests.TheEntryNamesNoOfficialUntilAnOfficialPageOrSearchServiceIndexesVotesAndDatesTheVoteByItsDay`.
- The action `policyAreaIds` filter holds the policy area ID that the approved revision stored. Enforced by `PublishedActionBuilder` and `PublishedAction.ToEntry`. Tests: `PublishActionTests.AnActionFillsItsSearchFiltersFromTheRevisionItPublishesAndNamesOnlyItsSponsor` and `PublishActionTests.AnActionWithoutAStoredAreaIDPublishesNoAreaFilterBecauseTheReviewerApprovedNone`.
- A brief publishes only when every citation is current in the new release. Current means that the release holds the cited kind, record, and revision, and that the latest decision on that revision is an approval. The loader checks this at build and in the ready list. The store checks it again at commit. Enforced by `BriefRecordLoader` and `PostgresPublicationStore.RequireCurrentCitationsAsync`, both through `BriefRecordReader.FindChangedAsync`. Tests: `PublishBriefTests.ABriefIsRefusedWhenTheSamePublishReplacesItsCitedRecordWithANewerRevision` and `PostgresBriefPublicationTests.TheCommitRefusesABriefWhenACitedRecordChangedBetweenTheBuildAndTheCommit`, `PublishBriefTests.ABriefIsRefusedWhenTheNewReleaseDoesNotHoldTheCitedRecordBecauseThePageWouldLinkToNothing`, `PublishBriefTests.ABriefThatIsNotApprovedIsRefusedBecauseNoModelOrDraftMaySpeakForTheSite`, and `PostgresBriefPublicationTests.APublishRefusesABriefWhoseCitedRevisionWasWithdrawnAfterTheBriefWasApproved`.
- A brief entry fills only `eventDate`, with the Monday of its week. Enforced by `PublishedBrief.ToEntry`. Tests: `PublishedBriefTests.TheEntryFillsOnlyTheWeekStartAsTheEventDate` and `PublishBriefTests.ABriefFillsOnlyItsWeekAsTheEventDateAndCopiesTheHeadlineOfEachCitedRecord`.
- A brief file holds 1 to `PublishedBrief.MaximumParagraphs` paragraphs and 1 to `PublishedBrief.MaximumCitations` citations. Each paragraph cites listed IDs, and each listed ID is cited. Enforced by `PublishedBrief.Validate`. Tests: `PublishedBriefTests.RequiresOneToTwelveParagraphsSoThePageStaysBounded`, `PublishedBriefTests.RejectsAParagraphThatCitesAnIdTheBriefDoesNotListBecauseTheLinkWouldHaveNoTarget`, and `PublishedBriefTests.RejectsAListedCitationThatNoParagraphUsesBecauseThePageWouldListARecordTheTextNeverCites`.
- The contract limits for briefs equal the Core limits. No enforcer; the constants are separate copies. Test: `PublishedBriefTests.ContractLimitsEqualTheCoreLimits`.
- A commit waits for the action review lock and the vote review lock. Enforced by `PostgresAdvisoryLock.ReviewKeysBeforeBriefs`. Tests: `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.CommitWaitsForTheActionLockBecauseAnActionDecisionMustNotSlipInDuringThePublish` and `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.CommitWaitsForTheVoteLockBecauseAVoteDecisionMustNotSlipInDuringThePublish`.
- The manifest `publishedAtUtc` and each `firstPublishedAtUtc` come from the application clock at build time. They can be earlier than the stored release time by the time that the commit waits. Only the stored release time places a release in a brief week. No enforcer. No test.
- A commit waits for the relationship review lock. It takes that lock after the vote lock and before the brief lock. Enforced by `PostgresAdvisoryLock.ReviewKeysBeforeBriefs`. Tests: `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.CommitWaitsForTheRelationshipLockBecauseARelationshipDecisionMustNotSlipInDuringThePublish`, `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.TheCommitHoldsTheVoteLockWhileItWaitsForTheRelationshipLockSoTheRelationshipLockComesAfterTheRecordLocks`, `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.ServingWaitsForTheRelationshipLockToo`, and `BriefStalenessTests.ThePublishDoesNotHoldTheBriefLockWhileItWaitsForTheRelationshipLockSoTheRelationshipLockComesBeforeIt`.
- A relationship publishes only when every cited revision is current in the new release. Current means that the release holds the cited kind, record, and revision, and that the latest decision on that revision is an approval. A withdrawal therefore counts as a change. The loader checks this at build and in the ready list. The store checks it again at commit. Enforced by `RelationshipRecordLoader` and `PostgresPublicationStore.RequireCurrentCitationsAsync`, both through `CitedRevisions.FindNotCurrentAsync`. Tests: `PublishRelationshipTests.ARelationshipIsRefusedWhenTheNewReleaseDoesNotHoldACitedRecordBecauseTheLinkWouldPointAtNothing`, `PublishRelationshipTests.ARelationshipIsRefusedWhenTheSamePublishReplacesACitedRecordWithANewerRevision`, `PublishRelationshipTests.ARelationshipThatIsNotApprovedIsRefusedBecauseNoModelOrDraftMaySpeakForTheSite`, `PostgresRelationshipPublicationTests.TheCitedRecordsAreCurrentOnlyWhenTheGivenReleaseHoldsTheirExactRevisionsAndTheirLatestDecisionIsAnApproval`, `PostgresRelationshipPublicationTests.TheCommitRefusesARelationshipWhenACitedRecordWasWithdrawnBetweenTheBuildAndTheCommit`, `PostgresRelationshipPublicationTests.TheCommitRefusesARelationshipWhoseCitedRecordIsMissingFromTheNewReleaseSoThePageNeverLinksToNothing`, and `PostgresRelationshipPublicationTests.TheReadyListHoldsAnApprovedRelationshipUntilACitedRecordLosesItsApprovalBecausePublishWouldRefuseIt`.
- A relationship that a release holds is carried into later releases as it is. Its record file and its page are always hard links when carried. Only the pages of the cited statement, action, and vote are written again. Enforced by `PublishRelease`. Test: `PublishRelationshipTests.AReplacedRecordPageListsTheLinksThatAnEarlierReleaseCarriesAndTheLinkFilesAreCarriedAsTheyAre`.
- A relationship entry takes its officials from the statement, its issues and policy areas from both sides, and its event date from the statement date. It has no source host. Enforced by `PublishedRelationship.ToEntry` and `PublishedRelationshipBuilder`. Tests: `PublishedRelationshipTests.TheEntryTakesTheOfficialsFromTheStatementBothSidesIssuesAndTheStatementDateAndNoSourceHost` and `PublishRelationshipTests.ARelationshipEntryTakesTheOfficialsFromTheStatementTheIssuesAndAreasFromBothSidesAndTheStatementDate`.
- A relationship entry holds the link type, the statement record ID, the target kind and record ID, and the matched official ID. Other entries hold none of them. The matched official is one of the officials of the entry. The target kind is action or vote, and the type fits it. Each ID has the form of its kind. Enforced by `PublishedRelationship.ToEntry`, `PublishedRecordEntry.Validate`, and `PublishedRelationship.TypeFits`, which the file check also uses. Tests: `PublishedRelationshipTests.TheEntryTakesTheOfficialsFromTheStatementBothSidesIssuesAndTheStatementDateAndNoSourceHost`, `PublishedRelationshipTests.ARelationshipEntryRequiresItsLinkTypeStatementAndTargetBecauseTheOfficialPageListsThePairFromTheManifestAlone`, `PublishedRelationshipTests.ARelationshipEntryRefusesALinkThatIsNotOneStatementAndOneBillOrVote` (it covers the matched official too), `PublishedRelationshipTests.AnEntryOfAnotherKindRefusesEveryLinkFieldBecauseOnlyARelationshipHasAPair`, `PublishRelationshipTests.ARelationshipEntryTakesTheOfficialsFromTheStatementTheIssuesAndAreasFromBothSidesAndTheStatementDate`.
- An action entry holds the official bill title of the action record in `billTitle`. Every other kind holds none. The title has 1 to `PublishedAction.MaximumBillTitleLength` characters, the limit of the review. The record check and the entry check use the same constant. Enforced by `PublishedAction.ToEntry`, `PublishedAction.Validate`, and `PublishedRecordEntry.Validate`. Tests: `PublishedActionTests.TheBillTitleIsBoundedInTheRecordAndInItsEntryAtTheLimitOfTheReviewSoTheTwoNeverDisagree`, `PublishedActionTests.AnActionEntryRequiresABillTitleAndEveryOtherKindRefusesOneBecauseOnlyAnActionHasAnOfficialTitle`, `PublishRelationshipTests.TheActionEntryCarriesTheOfficialBillTitleSoALinkedBillShowsItWithoutReadingTheActionFile`.
- A relationship file names the cited revision of each side, and its type fits the kind of its target. Enforced by `PublishedRelationship.Validate`. Tests: `PublishedRelationshipTests.RejectsATypeThatDoesNotFitTheKindOfTheTargetBecauseAVoteHasOneType`, `PublishedRelationshipTests.RejectsASideOfTheWrongKindBecauseALinkJoinsOneStatementToOneActionOrVote`, and `PublishRelationshipTests.TheRecordFileNamesTheCitedRevisionsAndCopiesTheWordingThatAPersonApproved`.
- The contract limits for relationships equal the Core limits. No enforcer; the constants are separate copies. Test: `PublishedRelationshipTests.ContractLimitsEqualTheCoreLimits`.
- A page that lists related records shows the links of the new release that cite its record. They follow the statement date, and an undated statement comes last. Enforced by `ReleaseLinks.Citing` and `PublishedRelatedPanel`. Tests: `PublishRelationshipTests.TheRelatedListFollowsTheStatementDateAndPutsAnUndatedStatementLast`, `ReleaseRendererTests.EachKindOfCitedRecordListsItsRelatedRecordsAsLinksWithTheLinkTypeAndTheStatementDate`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.APageWithoutRelatedRecordsShowsNoRelatedPanel`.
- A publish that adds or replaces a link writes again the page of each statement, action, or vote that the link cites. A publish that adds or replaces such a record lists the links that cite it. Enforced by `ReleasePages`. Tests: `PublishRelationshipTests.APublishThatAddsALinkWritesAgainThePageOfEachCitedRecordAndKeepsItsRecordFileAsTheSameFile`, `PublishRelationshipTests.ARelationshipPublishesWithTheCitedRecordsThatTheSamePublishAddsAndTheirPagesListIt`, and `PublishRelationshipTests.AReplacedLinkIsListedOnceBecauseTheNewRevisionTakesThePlaceOfTheCarriedOne`.
- A page that is written again keeps its record file as the same file on disk. Enforced by `PublishRelease`, which links the record file and writes only the page. Test: `PublishedRelatedRecordsTests.ALinkMakesTheCitedPagesListEachOtherWhileTheirRecordFilesStayTheSameFilesOnDisk`.
- The quote of a relationship lies on the extraction of its statement and inside one passage of the statement. Enforced by `PublishedRelationshipBuilder` through `ApprovedStatement.HasPassageCovering`, which the draft resolver also uses. Test: `PublishRelationshipTests.ARelationshipWhoseQuoteLiesOutsideEveryPassageOfItsStatementIsRefusedBecauseThePageWouldQuoteTextThatNoOneApproved`.
- `releases publish <key> --rewrite-pages` renders every carried record page again from its record file, for every record kind. A layout change then reaches pages that a plain publish only links. The record file stays the same file on disk. A page keeps its bill link, its related records, and its corrections. A withdrawn record is not carried, so it stays withdrawn. An invalid carried record file or relationship file stops the publish with `ReleaseRewriteException`. The message names the kind and ID. `ReleaseCommand` prints it and exits with 1. A plain publish still links every carried page that no link or fact change touches. The option needs an active release and counts as a change, so it can publish with no record. The option is in the payload hash only when set, so the hash of every earlier request holds. A page reads and renders when the staging writes it. A render failure stops the publish before the commit, and `PublishRelease` discards the staged directory. Enforced by `ReleasePages`, `PublishRelease`, `PublishReleaseRequest`, and `ReleaseCommand`. Tests: `PublishPageRewriteTests.ALayoutChangeReachesEveryKindOfCarriedPageAndEachRecordFileStaysTheSameFile`, `PublishPageRewriteTests.ARewrittenPageKeepsItsBillLinkItsRelatedRecordsItsCorrectionsAndItsBillStatusBecauseTheLayoutChangesNoFact`, `PublishPageRewriteTests.AWithdrawnRecordStaysWithdrawnAfterTheRewriteBecauseOnlyCarriedRecordsAreRendered`, `PublishPageRewriteTests.APlainPublishStillHardLinksEveryCarriedPageBecauseOnlyTheOptionRendersThemAgain`, `PublishPageRewriteTests.TheRewriteReadsAndRendersOneRecordBeforeItReadsTheNextSoPeakMemoryStaysAtAboutOnePage`, `PublishPageRewriteTests.EveryPublishAsksTheDatabaseForTheBillActionsOfAllReleaseVotesOnceBecauseOneQueryForEachVoteWouldCostTooMuch`, `PublishPageRewriteTests.ARenderFailureWhileStagingLeavesNoReleaseAndDiscardsTheStagedDirectory`, `PublishPageRewriteTests.TheRewritePagesOptionIsInThePayloadHashSoTheSameKeyIsAMismatchWithoutIt`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.TheStatusOnlyFlagStaysOutOfTheRequestHashWhenItIsOffSoTheKeyOfAnEarlierRequestStillReplays`, `PostgresHouseVoteTests.FindBillActionRecordIdsOfVotesUsesTheBillOfThePublishedRevisionAndNotTheBillOfTheCurrentCandidate`, `PostgresHouseVoteTests.FindBillActionRecordIdsOfVotesLeavesOutAVoteWithNoBillAndStillAnswersTheVoteWithABill`, `PostgresHouseVoteTests.FindBillActionRecordIdsOfVotesThrowsForAnUnstoredRevisionBecauseAReleaseNeverNamesOne`, `ReleaseCommandTests.TheRewritePagesOptionPublishesWithNoRecordAndRendersTheCarriedPageAgain`, `ReleaseCommandTests.ARewriteOfPagesThatFindsAnInvalidRecordFilePrintsTheKindAndIdAndExitsWithOneSoTheOperatorKnowsWhichFileToCheck`, `ReleaseCommandTests.ARewriteOfPagesThatFindsAnInvalidRelationshipFilePrintsTheRelationshipIdAndExitsWithOne`.
- A carried vote page keeps the link to the action of its bill when it is written again. Enforced by `VoteRecordLoader.RenderCarriedAsync`. Test: `PublishRelationshipTests.ACarriedVoteKeepsTheLinkToItsBillActionWhenALinkMakesItsPageRenderAgain`.
- A publish that adds no statement, action, vote, or relationship reads no link file. A rewrite of the pages, or a bill status change, reads the link files that cite a record it renders. Enforced by `ReleaseLinks.LoadAsync`. Tests: `PublishRelationshipTests.APublishThatTouchesNoRecordThatListsRelatedRecordsReadsNoLinkFile` and, for the rewrite, `PublishPageRewriteTests.ARewrittenPageKeepsItsBillLinkItsRelatedRecordsItsCorrectionsAndItsBillStatusBecauseTheLayoutChangesNoFact`. No test covers the bill status case.
- A publish reads only the carried relationship files that cite a record it renders. A relationship in the release without a pair row fails the read, so no link is left out. Enforced by `PostgresRelationshipReviewStore.FindCitingAsync` and `ReleaseLinks.LoadAsync`. Tests: `PublishRelationshipTests.OnlyTheCarriedLinksThatCiteAnAddedRecordAreReadFromDiskSoThePublishCostFollowsTheLinksOfThatRecord` and `PostgresRelationshipPublicationTests.FindCitingNamesOnlyTheGivenRelationshipsThatCiteAGivenRecordOfTheRightKind`.
- The read caps hold the largest file that the write side can produce: `PublishedRecordFiles.MaximumCitedRecordBytes` for a cited record and `PublishedRelationship.MaximumFileBytes` for a relationship. Enforced by the constants and by `ReleasePages`, which reads one cited record at a time. Tests: `PublishedRecordFilesTests.TheCitedRecordCapHoldsTheLargestRecordThatTheWriteSideCanProduceBecauseTheLimitsHaveOneOwner`, `PublishedRelationshipTests.AFileWithEveryFieldAtItsLimitAndEscapedStaysUnderTheFileCapSoTheReadCapNeverRefusesAPublishedLink`, and `PublishRelationshipTests.EachCarriedRecordIsReadAndRenderedBeforeTheNextIsReadSoPeakMemoryStaysAtAboutOneRecord`.
- A commit also waits for the brief review lock. Enforced by `BriefStaleness.TakeLocksAsync`. Test: `BriefStalenessTests.ThePublishWaitsForTheBriefLockLastInTheOrderBecauseAnAppendOfABriefMustNotSlipInDuringIt`.
- The stored release time is the database time of the commit, read after the commit holds the brief lock. Enforced by `PostgresPublicationStore.CommitAsync`. Tests: `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.TheReleaseTimeIsTheDatabaseTimeOfTheCommitSoABriefWeekNeverMissesARelease` and `BriefStalenessTests.TheReleaseTimeIsStampedAfterTheBriefLockSoABriefReadThatWaitedForTheCommitFindsTheRelease`.
- The precompressed manifests hold exactly the manifest bytes. Enforced by `PublishRelease`. Test: `PublishReleaseTests.PrecompressedManifestsHoldExactlyTheManifestBytesSoTheServerNeverSendsADifferentRelease`.
- A commit holds the exact revision and review state version that the build read, in the draft table of the record's kind. Enforced by `PostgresPublicationStore`. Tests: `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.ADecisionAfterTheBuildBlocksTheCommitBecauseApprovalMayHaveChanged`, `PostgresBillStreamTests.AnActionDecisionAfterTheBuildBlocksTheCommitBecauseApprovalMayHaveChanged`, `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.AStatementIsNeverCheckedAgainstTheActionTableBecauseTheKindPicksTheDraftTable`, `PostgresBriefPublicationTests.TheCommitChecksTheBriefTableForABriefAndRefusesAChangedReviewState`, and `PostgresHouseVoteTests.AVoteCommitWithAStaleReviewStateVersionIsAConflictBecauseTheKindPicksTheVoteTable`.
- The kinds share the batch limit equally, and unused share goes to the other kinds. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords.ShareLimitBetweenKinds`. Test: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.EveryKindKeepsItsShareOfABatchSoOneLargeBacklogDoesNotCrowdOutTheOthers`.
- One release is active. Enforced by `IX_publication_releases_is_active`. Test: `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.EachCommitBecomesTheActiveReleaseSoAFailedLinkSwapCannotHideIt`.
- A release stores the git commit SHA of the build that committed it in `publication_releases.code_version`. Releases from before the column existed hold null, and nothing fills them. A build with no valid SHA cannot commit a release. The worker, `releases publish`, and the review workspace refuse to start without a valid SHA. So Take off site never stops after it withdraws the approval. Enforced by `PostgresPublicationStore.CommitAsync`, `CodeVersion`, `BuildCodeVersion`, and `ck_publication_releases_code_version`. Tests: `CodeVersionTests`, `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.AReleaseStoresTheCodeVersionOfTheBuildThatCommittedIt`, `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.ABuildWithoutACodeVersionCannotCommitAReleaseAndStoresNothing`, and `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.TheDatabaseRefusesAMalformedCodeVersionOnARelease`.
- The swap renames a new link over `current`, so the name always exists and names a complete release. Caddy serves `{root}/current` through the link in production. On Linux a read that follows the link at the swap moment can rarely fail. A reader who retries gets the page. Enforced by `FileReleaseDirectory.ActivateAsync`. Test: `FileReleaseDirectoryTests.SwappingTheActiveReleaseAlwaysLeavesCurrentNamingACompleteRelease`.
- Release numbers have no gaps. Enforced by `PostgresPublicationStore`. Test: `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.ReleaseNumbersMustFollowTheLatestCommittedOneWithoutGaps`.
- A replay with another payload fails. Enforced by `PostgresPublicationStore`. Test: `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.ReplayReturnsTheOriginalOutcomeAndRejectsADifferentPayloadForTheSameKey`.
- A published release serves without the database. Enforced by the static release files. Test: `PublishedReleaseOutlivesDatabaseTests.PublishedReleaseServesFromFilesAloneAfterTheDatabaseIsGone`.
- `releases publish` fails without the collection configuration and the issues configuration. Enforced by `Program.cs`. No test.
- A person who replaces a record of the active release gives a note. Enforced by `PublicationCorrections.For`. Test: `PublishCorrectionsTests.APersonReplacementWithoutANoteIsRefusedBecauseAReaderMustSeeWhyTheRecordChanged`.
- A replacement writes an entry that names the replaced revision and keeps the earlier entries below it. A person publish fails when the record already has 20 entries with a note. Enforced by `PublicationCorrections.For`. Tests: `PublishCorrectionsTests.APersonReplacementWithANoteWritesAnEntryThatNamesTheReplacedRevision`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.ASecondCorrectionKeepsTheFirstEntryBelowTheNewOne`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.APersonPublishOverTheNotedCapFailsLoudInsteadOfDroppingTheOldestEntry`.
- A code approval needs no note, whoever publishes it. It writes an entry without a note. A code update replaces the code entry of its own kind in the newest run. So a run holds at most one entry of each kind. A vote update sets `factsUnchanged` when the headline or the summary changed. Every other published field must stay the same, also the issues and the computed party data. `VoteRecordLoader` compares the new vote with the vote in the record file of the active release (`PublishedVote.IsTextRewriteOf`). Review keeps no history of published revisions, so only that file can serve. A code update never hits the cap. A person approval that replaces a published record needs a note, also when the code subject publishes it. Without it the publish fails with `MissingCorrectionNoteException`. Enforced by `PublicationCorrections.For`, `PublishedRecordRules.IsApprovedByCode`, and `PublishedCorrection.Prepend`. Tests: `PublishCorrectionsTests.ARunOfCodeUpdatesStaysOneEntryThatNamesTheNewestReplacedRevision`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.ACodeRevisionThatOnlyRewritesTheTextOfThePublishedVoteIsMarkedAsFactsUnchanged`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.ACodeRevisionThatAlsoChangesTheIssuesStaysAnUpdateFromTheSource`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.TheLabelComparesWithThePublishedRevisionAndNotWithAnUnpublishedOneBetween`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.TheLabelFiresWhenAnUnpublishedRevisionChangedFactsAndTheNewRevisionRestoresThePublishedFacts`, `PublishedVoteTests.ComputedPartyDataAndEveryOtherVoteFieldCountAsFactsSoTheLabelNeverHidesAChange`, `PostgresHouseVoteTests.PublicationReadHoldsOnlyTheLastApprovedAndTheCurrentRevisionSoTheReplacedPublishedRevisionComesFromTheReleaseFile`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.ACodeRevisionThatChangesAVoteFactStaysAnUpdateFromTheSource`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.AFactChangeAfterATextOnlyUpdateAddsAnEntryInsteadOfHidingTheTextOnlyOne`, `PublishedCorrectionTests.PrependKeepsOneCodeUpdateOfEachKindInTheNewestRun`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.ACodeUpdateAfterAPersonCorrectionAddsOneEntryAndKeepsTheCorrection`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.ACodeUpdateOfARecordAtTheNotedCapStillPublishesSoCodeFactsNeverStall`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.ATickPublishesAnUpdateOfARecordThatHasTheMostPersonCorrectionsInsteadOfSkippingIt`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.ACodeApprovalWritesAnEntryWithoutANoteBecauseItOnlyRefreshesFromTheSource`, `PublishCorrectionsTests.APersonApprovalThatReplacesAPublishedRecordNeedsANoteEvenWhenTheCodeSubjectPublishesIt`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.ATickThatReplacesAPublishedRecordWritesAnUpdatedFromTheSourceEntryWithoutANote`.
- The list runs from the newest to the oldest replaced revision, each below the record revision. It has at most 20 entries with a note. It has at most one code entry of each kind between two entries with a note, before the first, and after the last. Only a code entry can set `factsUnchanged`. Every note is valid. The list holds at most 62 entries. These are 20 with a note and 2 in each of the 21 gaps. Enforced by `PublishedCorrection.ValidateAll`, which the seven record contracts call. The `corrections` field is required. Tests: `PublishedCorrectionTests` (also `AnOlderRecordFileWithoutTheFieldReadsAsASourceUpdateAndAPlainEntryWritesNoField`), `PublishedActionTests.CorrectionsRoundTripInTheRecordFileSoAReaderShowsWhatThePublishWrote`, `PublishedActionTests.ARecordThatWasNeverReplacedListsNoCorrections`.
- A rollback to a release whose files are gone is refused, and the active release does not change. The check runs in the activation transaction, under the same advisory lock as the link switch. Enforced by `PostgresPublicationStore.ActivateAsync` and `ActivatePublicationRelease.RequireFilesAsync`. Tests: `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.ActivationRefusedForMissingFilesLeavesThePreviousActiveReleaseUnchangedBecauseTheLinkStillServesIt`, `PublicationReleaseHandlersTests.ActivatingAReleaseWhoseFilesAreGoneIsRefusedBecauseTheLinkCouldNotServeItAndLaterPublishesWouldFail`.
- A prune keeps the 30 highest release numbers. It also keeps the highest number created on each of the 7 most recent UTC days, with today as the first day. A day with no release keeps nothing extra. A prune removes any other folder at or below the highest committed number. It never removes the active row or the folder that the `current` link serves. A burst of publishes cannot fill the disk. `ReleaseRetention.Plan` owns the rule and takes the release numbers with their creation times and the current time. The creation time is `published_at_utc_ticks` of the release row, and the store reads the database clock. Enforced by `ReleaseRetention`, `PostgresPublicationStore.PruneAsync`, `PruneReleases`, and `FileReleaseDirectory.RetireAsync`. Tests: `ReleaseRetentionTests.ABurstOfFiveHundredReleasesInOneDayKeepsThirtyPlusOlderDailyReleasesSoTheDiskStaysBounded`, `ReleaseRetentionTests.ADayWithNoReleaseIsSkippedSoOnlyDaysThatHaveAReleaseInTheWindowAddNumbers`, `ReleaseRetentionTests.ADayThatIsSevenDaysBackKeepsNothingBecauseOnlySixPastDaysAndTodayCount`, `ReleaseRetentionTests.TheUtcMidnightSplitsTwoDaysEvenWhenTheReleasesAreMinutesApart`, `ReleaseRetentionTests.AnOffsetTimestampCountsOnItsUtcDayNotItsLocalDay`, `ReleaseRetentionTests.TheDailyWindowStartsAtUtcMidnightSixDaysBackSoSevenDaysCountTheCurrentOne`, `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.PruneKeepsTheNewestThirtyAndOneReleasePerDayAfterABurstBecauseADrainMustNotFillTheDisk`, `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.PruneOffersThePlanAndTheActiveFolderAndLeavesEveryRowSoARolledBackReleaseSurvives`, `PruneReleasesTests.RetiresReleasesBelowTheKeptNumbersButNotTheActiveOneSoARollbackTargetSurvives`, `PruneReleasesTests.AnOlderDailyReleaseSurvivesAndTheRestOfThatDayIsRetired`, `PruneReleasesTests.NeverRetiresTheReleaseTheCurrentLinkServesEvenWhenItIsOldSoVisitorsKeepTheirSite`, `FileReleaseDirectoryTests.RetiringKeepsAFolderThePlanKeepsEvenWhenItIsOlderThanOneThatIsRetiredBecauseDailyReleasesSitBelowTheNewest`, `FileReleaseDirectoryTests.RetiringSkipsTheNamedFoldersEvenWhenThePlanRetiresThem`.
- Candidates come from the disk. A folder with no row is retired, and a folder that is not a release name is not. A folder with a number above the highest committed release is never retired, because a release in progress may own it. Enforced by `ReleaseRetentionPlan.Retires` and `FileReleaseDirectory.RetireAsync`.
