A kept board says that the newest Congress has no member with 25 split votes yet, and it names the Congress that it keeps. The line of the newest roll call names the Congress. The count of officials with no figures in the Congress of the board is per chamber. So is the count with no party. A name on the board links to the official page of the board Congress when it is older than the current page of the official. A board never mixes parties or chambers. An independent who caucuses with a party counts with that party, because the vote rows carry the caucus party. Enforced by `OfficialBoardPage`, `OfficialBoard`, and `ReleaseOfficialPages`. Tests: `OfficialBoardPageTests.ABoardHoldsOnlyMembersOfItsChamberAndPartyAndNeverMixesTwoParties`, `OfficialBoardPageTests.ABoardKeepsTheEarlierCongressWhileNoMemberOfTheNewCongressHasEnoughSplitVotes`, `OfficialBoardPageTests.ABoardMovesToTheNewCongressWhenOneMemberReachesTheMinimumSplitVotes`, `OfficialBoardPageTests.AMemberNewToTheChamberHasNoFiguresInTheKeptCongressAndIsCountedAsLeftOut`, `OfficialBoardPageTests.AnOfficialWithOnlyAttendanceInANewerCongressNeverMovesTheBoardBecauseNoMemberThereHasEnoughSplitVotes`, `OfficialBoardPageTests.AMemberOfTheOtherPartyReachingTheMinimumMovesBothPagesOfTheChamberToTheNewCongress`, `OfficialBoardPageTests.WhenOnlyANewcomerHoldsTheNewCongressAndNoRowHasPreviousFiguresTheBoardKeepsTheCongressOfTheOthers`, `PublishedBoardPageTests.AKeptBoardSaysWhyItShowsTheEarlierCongressNamesTheCongressOfTheRollCallAndLinksThePagesOfThatCongress`, `PublishedBoardPageTests.ABoardAtTheNewestCongressHasNoKeptNoticeAndLinksTheCurrentPage`, `HomeHighlightsTests.TheLeaderKeepsTheCongressOfItsBoardAndLinksThePageOfThatCongressWhenTheBoardKeepsAnEarlierOne`, `PublishedHomePageTests.ABreakLineFromAKeptBoardNamesItsEarlierCongressAndLinksThePageOfThatCongressSoTheCountsCanBeChecked`, `OfficialBoardPageTests.EachChamberUsesItsOwnNewestCongressSoAHouseAtOneCongressNeverHidesASenateAtAnother`, and `PublishOfficialPagesTests.ACarriedSummaryRowReachesTheBoardOfItsChamberAndPartyBecauseAnUntouchedOfficialStaysOnItsBoard`.
- A percent never reads 0% for a nonzero count or 100% for a count below its total. It reads "under 1%" or "over 99%" instead. The official page and the boards share the rule. Enforced by `PublishedOfficialView.Percent`. Tests: `PublishedBoardPageTests.ASplitVoteCountNeverRoundsANonzeroCountToZeroOrANonTotalToAHundredPercent` and `PublishedBoardPageTests.AMissedShareBelowOneHalfPercentReadsUnderOnePercentSoANonzeroCountNeverReadsZero`.
- A board has three lists. The first two rank the share of split votes where the member voted against, or with, most of the party. They rank only members with at least `OfficialBoardPage.MinimumSplitVotes` (25) split votes cast with Yea or Nay. A member below the minimum appears under "Too few split votes yet" with the count and no rank. The third list orders the members by missed votes. It shows the roll calls held and the dates. It states that the page gives no cause. Every count shows its total. The wording of a split vote count comes from `PublishedOfficialView.SplitVoteCount`, which the official page also calls. The wording of a missed vote comes from `PublishedOfficialView.MissedVotes`. Enforced by `OfficialBoardPage`, `PublishedBoardView`, and `PublishedBoardPage`. Tests: `OfficialBoardPageTests.AListRanksOnlyMembersWithEnoughSplitVotesAndNamesTheOthersSoAShortRecordNeverWinsByChance`, `OfficialBoardPageTests.TheMissedListOrdersByMissedVotesAndCarriesTheHeldCountAndDatesOfTheMember`, `PublishedBoardPageTests.EveryCountNamesWhoAndWhatAndShowsItsTotalSoNoNumberStandsAlone`, `PublishedBoardPageTests.AMemberBelowTheMinimumIsNamedWithTheCountAndGetsNoRank`, and `PublishedBoardPageTests.TheSplitVoteTablesSortByTheShareBehindTheCellAndNameTheirDefaultOrder`.
- A board page names what it leaves out. These are the officials with no Congress in their summary row and the officials who have no vote figures in the Congress of the board. They also include the officials with votes but no Democrat or Republican party. A row with no Congress is never read as zero. The summary row holds no count of Present or Not Voting casts. A board shows only the Yea and Nay casts as its total. Enforced by `OfficialBoardPage.All` and `PublishedBoardView.LeftOut`. Tests: `OfficialBoardPageTests.AnOfficialWithNoCongressOrAnEarlierCongressIsOnNoBoardAndIsCountedAsLeftOut` and `PublishedBoardPageTests.APageWithNoCongressSaysSoAndStillListsWhatItLeavesOut`.
- A vote row shows a pill when the entry `alignment` is `split` or `partiesAgree`. This holds in the record lists of the all-records pages and the facet pages. The pill reads "Split vote" or "Both parties agreed". The line under the title reads the kind and, when the entry holds both counts, "Yea N, Nay N". The list says the meaning of each pill and of the counts once, above the rows, in `PublishedVoteView.ListNotes`. The meaning says that the vote is substantive. The counts note says that Yea and Nay counts are casts of all members of the chamber who voted Yea or Nay. On the first page of an issue the summary panel already defines the pills, so the list leaves the two pill definitions out there. A policy area page defines the pills on every page. The counts note shows on every page. A vote with `mixed`, no `alignment`, or no counts gets no pill. A Speaker election or another candidate vote has no totals, so it gets no pill and no counts. The list does not show the chamber. The search index does not hold these fields. A row that the browser builds after a sort has no pill, and the sort status line says so. The method page says when a row has no pill. Enforced by `PublishedVoteView.ListBadge`, `PublishedVoteView.ListYeaNay`, `PublishedVoteView.ListNotes`, and `PublishedRecordTable`. The board page uses `PublishedVoteListFacts`, which keeps the meaning beside its one row. Tests: `PublishedHomePageTests.AVoteInAListShowsItsPartyPillAndCountsAndTheListSaysTheirMeaningOnceAboveTheRows`. Also `PublishedHomePageTests.ACandidateVoteAndAMixedVoteGetNoPartyPillAndACandidateVoteShowsNoCounts`. Also `ReleaseRendererTests.AllRecordsPageDefinesTheVoteBadgesAndTheCountsOnceSoNoRowRepeatsAParagraph`. Also `ReleaseRendererTests.AnIssuePageDefinesTheVoteBadgesOnlyWhereItsSummaryDoesNotButAlwaysSaysTheCountsMeaningSoEachPageSaysItOnce`. Also `ReleaseRendererTests.APolicyAreaPageDefinesTheVoteBadgesOnItsFirstPageBecauseItsSummaryDoesNotDefineThem`. The sort status line text is checked by `ReleaseRendererTests.TheSortStatusLineTemplateEndsWithTheNoticeThatSortedRowsLeaveOutTheBadgeAndTheCounts`. No test runs the script.
- A record list row is one link. It shows the kind icon, the title, a kind line, the vote pill when there is one, and the date column. The row link covers the whole row. The issue page shows the icon of the issue beside its title. A record page puts its panels in one `record-panels` list, with flat headings and no frames, and its lede uses the sans face. Enforced by `PublishedRecordTable`, `PublishedRecordFrame`, `PublishedFacetPage`, and `public.css`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryPanelOfARecordPageSitsInOneSectionListSoTheRecordPagesShareOneFlatLook` and `ReleaseRendererTests.AnIssuePageShowsItsIconBesideTheTitleAndAPolicyAreaPageShowsNone`. No test checks the CSS.
- Code picks the home page items from the manifest entries and the summary rows of one publish. The rules are fixed and end in a tie-break by record ID or official name. The same records on the same release day give the same items. The vote list holds the `HomeHighlights.VotesPerChamber` (2) newest substantive votes of the House and of the Senate. The other list holds the `HomeHighlights.ExecutiveActionCount` (3) newest executive actions. Both follow the order of `ReleaseRecordOrder`. A substantive vote here is an entry with an `alignment`: a substantive vote in which both the Democrats and the Republicans voted. The four votes show newest first in one list. A chamber with fewer such votes shows fewer rows, and the other chamber does not fill them. A column with no entry is hidden, and the section is hidden when both columns are empty. The vote list is titled "Votes", and each row names its chamber before the date, as in "Senate, 5 May 2026". An entry with no chamber, from a release before the field, is left out until `--rewrite-entries` fills it. The break card names one official for each chamber and party. That official has the highest share of split votes cast against most of the party, in the Congress of the board for the chamber. The home page needs the boards, not the summary rows, and `ReleaseOfficialPages` passes them on. The card names the first member of the first board list of the chamber and party. So it counts only officials in the board Congress with one break and at least `OfficialBoardPage.MinimumSplitVotes` split votes cast. A tie goes to the first name, as on the board. A row shows the name as a link, the party letter, and the state name. It also shows "N of M votes" with the break count and the cast count, and a bar of that share. The rows come in the order of the share, highest first. A tie goes to more breaks, then to the lower official ID. `BreakLeader.All` owns this order. The lede names the Congress of the rows: one Congress, or the two newest when the chambers keep different boards. The party letter and the state name come from the newest started term of the official that the counts use. That term is in the office of the chamber, and its caucus, or else its party, is the party of the board. A term in another office or party never counts. A member who changed party shows the party of the counted votes. With no such term, the row has no party and state. A missing party or state is left out. `PartyLetters` owns the party letter, and `UsStates` owns the state names. The name links to the official page of that Congress when that page is not the current one. A chamber and party with no leader gets no row. The card is hidden when no chamber and party has a leader. An info button on the card opens the fixed rule text and a link to the method page. The closest vote card is on each of the four board pages, and not on the home page. `ClosestVote` picks it from the votes with an `alignment`. These are substantive votes where both major parties voted. A vote must be dated in the 7 days that end on the release day. Both end days count. It picks the smallest gap between the Yea count and the Nay count, then the lower record ID. Both chambers count. The gap ignores thresholds such as 60 votes and two thirds, and the card says so. The card names the dates and the number of votes it chose from. With no vote in the window, the card is hidden. It does not name the result, because an entry holds no result. `PublishRelease` writes the home page after `ReleaseOfficialPages`, because the break card needs the summary rows. It also passes the count of published officials for the link "browse all N officials". Enforced by `HomeHighlights`, `BreakLeader`, `PartyLetters`, `OfficialBoardPage`, `PublishRelease`, `ClosestVote`, `PublishedBreakCard`, and `PublishedHomeLatest`. Tests: `PublishedBoardPageTests.TheClosestVoteCardNamesTheDatesTheGapAndTheSizeOfThePoolAndLinksTheVote`, `PublishedBoardPageTests.ATiedClosestVoteSaysTheCountsAreEqualAndNoVoteInTheWindowHidesTheCard`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheOldPanelsButtonsTablesAndReleaseLineAreGoneFromHomeAndTheHeroHoldsTheHeadingAndTheLede`, `HomeHighlightsTests.TheTwoNewestSubstantiveVotesOfEachChamberComeNewestFirstSoNeitherChamberCrowdsOutTheOther`, `HomeHighlightsTests.AProceduralVoteAndAnEntryWithNoChamberAreLeftOutAndAChamberWithFewerVotesShowsFewerRows`, `HomeHighlightsTests.TheNewestExecutiveActionsComeNewestFirstAndOnlyTheThreeNewestStay`, `HomeHighlightsTests.TheOfficialCountIsTheCountThatThePublishGives`, `HomeHighlightsTests.TheBreakLeaderHasTheHighestShareNotTheHighestCountAndNeedsTheMinimumOfSplitVotes`, `HomeHighlightsTests.AMemberWithExactlyTheMinimumOfSplitVotesCanLead`, `HomeHighlightsTests.BreakLeadersComeHighestShareFirstThenMoreBreaksThenLowerOfficialIdSoTheCardOrderNeverDependsOnBoardOrder`. Closest vote tests: `ClosestVoteTests.TheClosestVoteHasTheSmallestGapBetweenItsYeaAndNayCountsAndATieGoesToTheLowerRecordId`, `ClosestVoteTests.OnlyVotesDatedInTheSevenDaysThatEndOnTheReleaseDayCountSoARecessGivesNoStaleCard`, `ClosestVoteTests.AVoteDatedOnTheReleaseDayCountsAndOneDatedTheDayAfterDoesNot`, `ClosestVoteTests.AVoteWithNoAlignmentIsNeverTheClosestVoteBecauseProceduralVotesAndSpeakerElectionsAreOut`, `ClosestVoteTests.WithNoVoteInTheWindowThereIsNoClosestVote`. More tests: `HomeHighlightsTests.ATieOnShareAndOnBreaksGoesToTheFirstOfficialNameLikeTheBoardListNotTheLowerId`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheBreakCardListsOneRowForEachLeaderWithTheHighestShareFirstAndALinkToTheBoards`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheBreakCardNamesTheCongressOfItsRowsSoAKeptEarlierBoardIsNotPassedOffAsTheCurrentOne`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheInfoButtonOfTheBreakCardOpensTheFixedRuleAndSaysWhenAChamberAndPartyHasNoLine`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheBreakCardShowsOnlyThePartyLetterAndStateNameThatTheTermHolds`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheBreakCardUsesThePartyOfTheCountedChamberSoAMemberWhoChangedPartyIsNotLabeledWithTheNewParty`, `PublishedHomePageTests.ALaterTermInAnotherOfficeOrChamberDoesNotChangeThePartyOrStateOfAMemberOfTheHouse`, `PublishedHomePageTests.ASenateLeaderTakesTheStateOfTheSenateTermNotOfALaterHouseTerm`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheBreakCardLeavesOutThePartyAndStateItDoesNotHold`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheLatestListHoldsTheNewestVotesOfEachChamberAndTheThreeNewestActionsNewestFirstWithTheirDateAndALink`, `PublishedHomePageTests.TheLatestListAndTheBreakCardAreHiddenWhenTheyHaveNothingToShowInsteadOfAnEmptyBlock`, `PublishedHomePageTests.AColumnWithNoEntryIsHiddenSoTheOtherColumnStandsAlone`, `PublishedHomePageTests.HomeLinksTheOfficialsListWithTheRealCountInPlaceOfTheZipForm`, and `PublishEntryRewriteTests.ThePublishHandsTheHomeCardsTheVoteCountsAndTheLeaderOfTheCarriedSummaryAndARewriteOfAllOfficialsRecomputesThem`.
- One rule orders dated rows: the event day, else the first publication day, newest first. Records of one day follow the first publication time, newest first, then the record ID. A vote row on an official page has no publication time, so it follows the records of its day. The publish writes the manifest records in this order, so the home page and the all-records pages show the same order. The home page and the all-records pages read the manifest order. Other readers sort or match for themselves. The readers that keep the first name of an official, `ReleaseDataFiles` and `PublishedSearchPage`, now take the name from the record with the newest event day. The manifest order is no contract guarantee. Enforced by `ReleaseRecordOrder`, which `PublishRelease`, `ReleaseFacets`, and `PublishedOfficialView` call. Tests: `PublishEntryRewriteTests.TheHomeAndAllRecordsPagesFollowTheEventDayThenTheFirstPublicationDay`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialPageAndAFacetPageOrderTheSameEntriesTheSameWayBecauseOneRuleOwnsTheDateOrder`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.FacetPageShowsTheEventDateAndOfficialsOfEachEntryAndFallsBackToTheFirstPublicationDayWhenThereIsNoEventDateAndSameDayEntriesFollowTheLaterPublicationFirst`.
- Every record page shows a timeline of the dated events that its own record file holds, in date order. Events on one day follow their clock time. An event with only a date comes first on its day. Approval and first publication carry a time. Enforced by `PublishedTimelineView` and `PublishedTimeline`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryRecordKindListsItsDatedEventsInDateOrderOnATimeline` and `ReleaseRendererTests.TimesOnOneDayKeepTheirClockOrderSoTheShownTimesNeverContradictTheOrder`.
- A public record page omits every fact row that has no value, such as tracked issues, policy areas, bill numbers, or other people quoted. The review editors keep their "None" rows. A vote of kind Other reads "Other question". With a question quote, a Question row shows the question text. Without one, the kind value says the source gives no question. A statement quote reads "Quote 1" with a badge that says whether it is inside quotation marks. Enforced by `PublishedRecordView.Values` and `PublishedRecordView.Links`, which every public list row uses, and by `PublishedRecordLabels`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.APublicPageOmitsFactRowsThatHaveNoValueInsteadOfPrintingNone`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AnOtherKindVoteWithAQuestionQuoteIsCalledAnOtherQuestionAndShowsTheQuestion`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AnOtherKindVoteWithNoQuestionQuoteSaysTheSourceGivesNoQuestionSoTheReaderIsNotSentToANonexistentQuote`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.StatementQuotesAreNumberedAndSayWhetherTheyLieInsideQuotationMarks`.
- An official page orders its rows by date, newest first, and gives each table a caption. The record page and every section use the same rows. Enforced by `PublishedOfficialView` and `PublishedOfficialPage`. Tests: `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheCurrentPageListsStatementsActionsAndVotesNewestFirstWithTheirOwnDates`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheCurrentPageShowsTheNameTheOfficesAndTheDatedTermsWithTableCaptionsAndAnOngoingTermAsPresent`.
- A table gets a sort header only when its page asks for one. A paged table asks only when the page has the API address. A table that asks for none, such as a review table, has no sort attribute. Sorting reads `data-sort-value`, so a change of the date display never changes a sort. Enforced by `DataTable`, `TableCell`, `PublishedRecordTable`, and `PublishedOfficialPage`. Tests: `DataTableTests.ATableThatAsksForNoSortRendersNoSortAttributeSoTheReviewTablesStayAsTheyWere`, `DataTableTests.ASortableTableMarksItsHeadersTheValueBehindACellAndTheDefaultOrderInTheCaption`, `ReleaseRendererTests.WithoutAnApiAddressNoPagedTableHasASortHeaderSoTheStaticPageStaysNewestFirstWithNoDeadControl`, `ReleaseRendererTests.WithAnApiAddressTheAllRecordsHeadersNameTheSearchSortKeyAndTheStaticRowsStayInManifestOrder`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AFacetPageSortsOnlyByRecordAndDateAndFiltersTheSearchByItsOwnIssueOrPolicyArea`, `ReleaseRendererTests.TablesThatDoNotPassASortStayPlainEvenWhenTheReleaseHasAnApiAddress`, `ReleaseRendererTests.EachSortedTableNamesItsColumnsInTheOrderOfItsHeadersBecauseTheScriptBuildsRowsFromThatList`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheCastCellsOfTheBreaksTableSortByTheCastWordNeverByThePopoverText`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.EveryWholeTableOnTheOfficialPageSortsInTheBrowserByTheValueBehindACellNeverByItsDisplayedText`, and `PublishedOfficialPageTests.EachSortableTableNamesItsDefaultOrderInItsCaptionSoTheOrderWithoutScriptsIsStated`. No test for the script, because the repository has no JavaScript test harness. No test for these browser rules. A row with an empty sort value stays last. A failed sort restores the previous state. A built row has the title of a static row.
- Every term has a `Kind` (`PublishedOfficeKind`): Senator, Representative, Executive, Judicial, or Other. `PublicationOfficials` is the only place that sets it. A member term is a Senator term in the Senate and a Representative term in the House. An executive roster term is Executive. A configured office takes its configured kind, else Other. The Host reads the `Kind` and never decides it from the office text. A release with an undefined `Kind` is refused. Enforced by `PublicationOfficials` and `PublishedOfficeTerm.Validate`. Tests: `PublishOfficialPagesTests.AMemberTermIsKindSenatorOrRepresentativeByItsChamberSoTheListGroupsASenatorWhoWasARepresentative`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.AnExecutiveRosterTermIsKindExecutiveSoThePresidentIsNotInOtherOfficials`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.AConfiguredOfficeTakesItsConfiguredKindElseOtherSoOnlyAnOfficeWithAKindLeavesTheOtherOfficialsTab`, `PublishedOfficialsTests.ATermWithAnUndefinedKindIsRefusedBecauseTheListCouldNotPlaceTheOfficial`.
- A person takes member terms by bioguide ID, else executive terms by Wikidata ID, else configured offices. A configured office is the term of a person with no roster terms, including a person absent from the member snapshot or the executive snapshot. Roster terms win when both exist, and a person with a bioguide ID may have offices. Enforced by `PublicationOfficials`. Tests: `PublishOfficialPagesTests.AConfiguredOfficeIsTheTermOfAPersonWithNoRosterTerms`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.APersonWithABioguideIdAndNoMemberTermShowsTheConfiguredOffice`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.ExecutiveTermsWinOverConfiguredOfficesForAFormerMemberAbsentFromTheMemberSnapshot`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.RosterTermsWinOverConfiguredOfficesWhenBothExistForAPerson`.
- Every record page links to `report.html` with its own record ID and revision, so a report binds to the version that the reader saw. Enforced by `PublishedRecordFrame` and `PublicationProtocol.ReportPageLink`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryRecordPageLinksToTheReportPageWithItsRecordAndRevisionSoAReportBindsToTheVersionTheReaderSaw` and `ReleaseRendererTests.ARecordPageOffersTheReportLinkAboveAndBelowTheRecordSoAReaderFindsItWithoutScrolling`. The second test checks that one link comes before the heading and one comes after the record facts.
- The current page of an official with a vote file shows the issue panel. A statement or a sponsorship counts in the Congress of its day. The day is the event day of the entry, else its first publication day. `CongressCalendar` maps the day to a Congress. The panel uses the same Congress as the page, so its three counts share one window. The votes count takes the rows of the vote file of that Congress. Enforced by `TalkAndActionProfile` for statements and sponsorships, and by `ReleaseOfficialVotes.ReadAsync` for votes. Tests: `TalkAndActionProfileTests.AStatementOrASponsorshipOutsideTheCurrentCongressDoesNotCountButTheEdgeDaysDo`, `TalkAndActionProfileTests.AnEntryWithNoEventDateCountsInTheCongressOfItsFirstPublicationDay`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.OnlyTheCurrentPageOfAnOfficialWithAVoteCarriesAProfile`, `PublishOfficialVotesTests.AVoteFileHoldingAVoteOfAnotherCongressIsRefusedSoAMisplacedFileCannotHideARow`.
- The profile counts a statement entry as a statement and an action entry as a sponsored bill. It counts a vote row of a substantive kind as a vote. A cosponsor never counts, because an action entry names only its sponsors. Other entry kinds never count. Enforced by `TalkAndActionProfile` and `PublishedAction.ToEntry`. Tests: `TalkAndActionProfileTests.OnlyStatementsAndActionsCountAsTalkAndSponsorshipSoOtherEntriesNamingTheOfficialAreIgnored`, `PublishedActionTests.TheEntryNamesOnlyTheSponsorAndDatesTheLatestSponsorshipEvent`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.TheCurrentPageGetsTheProfileOfItsCongressWithTheStatementTheSponsoredBillAndTheVoteAndACosponsorGetsNoPage`.
- The count "Votes they cast" counts a substantive roll call only when the official cast a vote. A cast of `PublishedOfficialVote.NotVoting` ("Not Voting") leaves the count. Enforced by `PublishedOfficialVote.IsCast` and `TalkAndActionProfile`. Test: `TalkAndActionProfileTests.ANotVotingCastLeavesTheVotesColumnWhilePresentAndACandidateNameStayIn`.
- The first key figure of each chamber with vote rows states the share of roll calls that the official voted on. The vote file owns the Congress window: `ReleaseOfficialVotes.ReadAsync` refuses a file with a vote of another Congress. A roll call is missed when the cast is "Not Voting". No other cast is missed. The held count is the number of vote rows of the chamber. These rows are the roll calls on this site while the official served. The label reads "of roll calls voted on: 580 of 580". It names the chamber when the official has votes in two chambers. The note of the figure keeps the approved meaning of a missed vote. Procedural votes count. The count can be fewer than the chamber held. The value shows a percent only when the held count is at least `TalkAndActionProfile.MinimumRecordsForShare`. Below it the value is the plain count, such as "3 of 4". A chamber with no rows has no figure. Enforced by `MissedVotes.Of`, `TalkAndActionProfile.Of`, `PublishedKeyFigure`, `CongressCalendar.Ordinal`, and `ReleaseOfficialVotes.ReadAsync`. Tests: `MissedVotesTests`, `PublishOfficialVotesTests.AVoteFileHoldingAVoteOfAnotherCongressIsRefusedSoAMisplacedFileCannotHideARow`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheVotedFigureOfEachChamberNamesTheChamberAndTheCountsAndItsNoteKeepsTheApprovedMeaningOfAMissedVote`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheVotedFigureShowsAShareOnlyAtTheThresholdAndAnOfficialWithNoRowsOfAChamberGetsNoFigureForIt`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialWhoMissedEveryRollCallOfAChamberShowsZeroPercentVotedSoTheFigureNeverHidesAFullAbsence`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.TheProfileOfThePageCountsTheMissedVotesOfItsOwnVoteRows`, `CongressCalendarTests.AnOrdinalTakesThNotStNdOrRdForTheElevenToThirteenFamily`.
- `PublishedVoteKindExtensions.IsSubstantive` is the one owner of the substantive vote kinds: passage, suspension, amendment, conference report, veto override, and nomination. The count "Votes they cast" leaves out every other kind, which is a procedural vote. The method note lists the substantive kinds from the same member. Enforced by `PublishedVoteKindExtensions` and `TalkAndActionProfile`. Tests: `TalkAndActionProfileTests.OnlySubstantiveVoteKindsCountSoAProceduralVoteNeverRaisesTheVotesTheyCast`, `ReleaseRendererTests.TheMethodPageNamesTheSubstantiveVoteKindsAndTheThreeIssueCountsFromTheirOwners`.
- The tab "Issues they work on" of the record page opens a pane. The pane has one row for each tracked issue that a counted statement, sponsored bill, or vote of the Congress holds. Rows sort by issue ID. Each row shows three plain counts: "Their statements", "Bills they sponsored", and "Votes they cast". It shows the official title of each sponsored bill, linked to the action page. The pane shows no share, percent, average, or color, because a count without a position says nothing about the official. A record with several issues counts once for each issue. A record with no tracked issue is in no row. With no issue it shows "No tracked issue" and a plain sentence. Enforced by `TalkAndActionColumn`, `TalkAndActionProfile`, and `PublishedRecordList.razor`. Tests: `TalkAndActionProfileTests.ARecordWithSeveralIssuesCountsOncePerIssueAndARecordWithNoIssueCountsOnNoIssue`, `TalkAndActionProfileTests.ASponsoredBillIsListedOnEachOfItsIssuesWithItsBillTitleInTheCurrentCongressOnly`, `TalkAndActionProfileTests.TheIssuesOfThePanelAreTheSortedUnionOfTheIssuesOfAllThreeColumns`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheIssuePanelShowsPlainCountsAndTheTitlesOfTheSponsoredBillsAndLeavesOutProceduralVotes`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialWithNoRecordOnATrackedIssueGetsAPlainSentenceInPlaceOfTheIssueTable`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheIssuesTabIsLastAndOpensAPaneOutsideTheRecordListSoTheListKeepsOnlyRecordRows`.
- A vote is split when it is substantive. The majorities of the Democrats and of the Republicans must both be set and differ. A tie in either party gives no majority, so the vote is not split. A vote with candidate totals has no party majorities, so it is never split. The party names are the roster names `PublishedPartyMajority.Democrat` and `PublishedPartyMajority.Republican`. Enforced by `PublishedVote.IsSplit` and `PublishedVoteKindExtensions.IsSubstantive`. Tests: `PublishedVoteTests.AProceduralVoteWithOppositeMajoritiesIsNotSplitSoARuleVoteCannotMoveTheShare`, `PublishedVoteTests.AVoteIsSplitOnlyWhenMostDemocratsAndMostRepublicansHaveOppositeMajorities`, `PublishedVoteTests.AVoteWithOnlyOneMajorPartyOrCandidateTotalsIsNeverSplit`.
- Publish stores the split flag on each vote row. The page reads the flag and does not compute it again. A row with the flag set must have a substantive kind and totals. A Democrat or Republican row must also have a party majority. A row of another party can have none, because its group can have no Yea or Nay or a tie. Such a row is never a break. Old vote files have no flag and do not read. Enforced by `PublishedOfficialVote.Of` and `PublishedOfficialVote.Validate`. Tests: `PublishVoteTests.TheOfficialRowIsFlaggedSplitWhenMostDemocratsAndMostRepublicansVotedOnOppositeSides`, `PublishVoteTests.ATiedPartyHasNoMajorityOnItsOfficialRow`, `PublishVoteTests.ASpeakerElectionPublishesItsCandidateTotalsWithNoTotalsAndNoPartyMajorities`, `PublishVoteTests.AMemberOfAGroupWithNoYeaOrNayVotesStillPublishesARowOnASplitVote`, `PublishVoteTests.ATiedIndependentGroupStillPublishesARowOnASplitVote`, `PublishedOfficialVotesTests.ASplitVoteRowThatIsProceduralOrHasNoTotalsIsRefusedSoThePageNeverCountsIt`, `PublishedOfficialVotesTests.OnlyADemocratOrRepublicanRowOfASplitVoteMustHaveAPartyMajority`.
- The votes with their party count only split votes, which are always substantive, where the official cast Yea or Nay. They count each chamber and each party shown on the rows. A cast equal to the party majority is a match. A cast against it is a break. Present, Not Voting, and a candidate name are left out of both numbers and stated as a count. The share shows only when the cast count is at least `TalkAndActionProfile.MinimumRecordsForShare`. The party is the caucus, else the party, of the official on the day of each vote; see the voter party rule under [Invariants](#invariants). A member who left the roster takes the party of the source letter, so an Independent who left counts under no major party. The method page states both rules. The page has one sentence for each chamber and party with rows. Each sentence names the Congress. It omits a party that is neither Democrat nor Republican. Enforced by `VotesWithParty.Of`, `TalkAndActionProfile.Of`, `TalkAndActionProfile.ShareOf`, and `PublishedOfficialView.WithParty`. Tests: `VotesWithPartyTests`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.ThePercentOfVotesWithTheirPartyShowsOnlyAtTheThreshold`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialWhoseRowsNameNeitherMajorPartyGetsNoVotesWithTheirPartyPanel`, `PublishVoteTests.AnIndependentWhoCaucusesWithDemocratsCountsAsDemocrat`, `PublishVoteTests.AMemberWhoLeftTheRosterTakesThePartyOfTheSourceLetter`, `ReleaseRendererTests.TheMethodPageNamesTheSubstantiveVoteKindsAndTheThreeIssueCountsFromTheirOwners`.
- The panel "What they said and what they did" comes after the sections and before the offices on the current Congress page only. It is one short panel. It has one row for each approved relationship whose pair matched the official. A co-speaker with no match gets no row. A pair belongs to the Congress of its statement day. Rows are newest first by statement day, with no cap. A statement with no day belongs to the Congress of its bill or vote, shows "No date", and comes last, in the order of `ReleaseRecordOrder.NewestFirstUndatedLast`. The date column is "Statement date". The panel states that a reviewer approved each link and that a link does not mean a match. It makes no judgment. With no pair it shows one line and keeps the panel. Enforced by `LinkedPair.In`, `PublishedOfficialPage.razor`, and `PublishedOfficialView`. Tests: `LinkedPairTests.PairsFollowTheStatementDayNewestFirstWithTheTitleAndTheCastOfTheOfficial`, `LinkedPairTests.AJointStatementShowsItsPairOnlyOnThePageOfTheOfficialThatThePairMatchedSoACoSpeakerWhoSponsorsNothingGetsNoRow`, `LinkedPairTests.AStatementOfAnotherCongressIsNotInThePairsOfThisCongress`, `LinkedPairTests.AnUndatedStatementBelongsToTheCongressOfItsTargetAndItsRowComesLast`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnUndatedStatementShowsNoDateInsteadOfAnInventedDay`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheLinkedPairsPanelComesAfterTheRecordListAndListsEachPairWithTheLinkWordsTheTitleAndTheCast`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialWithNoPairKeepsThePanelWithOneLineThatSaysThereIsNoLinkYet`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnEarlierCongressPageHasNoLinkedPairsPanel`.
- A pair reads the manifest in memory and no relationship file. The statement and target come from their own manifest entries. A linked bill shows `billTitle`, else its headline. Publish forbids a release that holds a link without both cited records; see the relationship invariants above. A pair whose record is missing is left out, so the page never fails. Enforced by `LinkedPair.In`, `ReleaseOfficialPages`, and the commit check `CitedRevisions.FindNotCurrentAsync`. Tests: `LinkedPairTests.ALinkWhoseRecordIsNotInTheReleaseIsLeftOutSoThePageNeverCrashes`, `LinkedPairTests.AVoteTargetShowsItsHeadlineBecauseOnlyAnActionEntryHoldsAnOfficialTitle`, `PublishRelationshipTests.TheOfficialPageListsEachLinkOfAStatementOfTheOfficialWithTheBillTitleAndTheCastOfTheOfficial`.
- The cast of a vote pair is the cast in the vote row of the official for that vote ID, shown as it is. The row comes from the vote file of the page Congress. A vote with no row shows "No vote on record". A bill pair shows "Not a vote". Enforced by `LinkedPair.In` and `PublishedOfficialView.TheirVote`. Tests: `LinkedPairTests.AVoteWithoutACastRowOfTheOfficialHasNoCast`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AVoteLinkWithNoCastRowOfTheOfficialSaysSoInsteadOfLeavingTheCellEmpty`.
- A change to a bill or a vote touches the matched official of each link that cites it, and no other speaker. The pair on that page then shows the new title. Enforced by `ReleaseOfficialPages`. Tests: `PublishRelationshipTests.ABillThatTheOfficialDoesNotSponsorStillTouchesTheOfficialPageWhenItIsReplacedBecauseThePairShowsItsTitle`, `PublishRelationshipTests.AVoteThatIsReplacedTouchesTheOfficialPageOfASpeakerWhoIsNotAMemberOfItsChamber`, and `PublishRelationshipTests.AJointStatementPutsItsPairOnTheMatchedOfficialAndAReplacedBillTouchesOnlyThatOfficial`.
- The method page explains a link, the four link words, and that a link is not a verdict. The words come from `PublishedRecordLabels.RelationshipType`. Enforced by `PublishedMethodPage`. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.TheMethodPageExplainsALinkWithTheFourPublicLinkWordsAndSaysThatALinkIsNotAVerdict`.
- The next key figure of each chamber and major party states how often the official voted with most of that party. It needs a split vote cast. The label names who and how many, such as "with most House Republicans: 203 of 206 split votes". The note keeps the definition of a split vote. When the official did not cast Yea or Nay on some split votes, the note says how many. The value shows a percent only at the share threshold. Below it the value is the plain count, such as "3 of 4". An official with no split vote cast has no such figure. The votes against the party are the rows of the tab "Against their party". Enforced by `PublishedKeyFigure`, `VotesWithParty`, and `PublishedOfficialView`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.TheMethodPageNamesTheSubstantiveVoteKindsAndTheThreeIssueCountsFromTheirOwners` (the method page text), `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheWithPartyFigureOfEachChamberNamesWhoAndHowManySplitVotesAndTheNoteSaysHowManyWereNotCast`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialWhoNeverBrokeWithThePartyShowsAFullShareAndNoMarkAgainstItAndAnOfficialWithNoSplitVoteGetsNoPartyFigure`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialWhoseRowsNameNeitherMajorPartyGetsNoWithPartyFigure`.
- The key figures row has two more figures: the bills and resolutions that the official sponsored in the Congress, and the statements of the Congress. `TalkAndActionProfile.SponsoredBills` and `TalkAndActionProfile.StatementsInCongress` own the counts, so a statement with no issue counts once and a statement with two issues counts once. A zero statement figure says that Civic Lens has not yet published a statement, and that this does not mean the official made none. Each figure has an info button with the accessible name "What this means: " and the label. The button opens the note with the native popover, so no script is needed. The row has no figure without a profile. Enforced by `PublishedKeyFigure`, `PublishedKeyFigures.razor`, `InfoPopover.razor`, and `TalkAndActionProfile.Of`. Tests: `TalkAndActionProfileTests.TheStatementCountCountsEachStatementOfTheCongressOnceWhateverItsIssuesSoAKeyFigureNeverOvercountsOrDropsOne`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.EachKeyFigureHasAnInfoButtonThatNamesItAndOpensItsNoteSoNoNumberStandsWithoutItsMeaning`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheBillAndStatementFiguresCountTheCongressOnlyAndOnlyAZeroStatementFigureSaysNothingWasPublishedYet`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.ThePageHasNoKeyFiguresWithoutAProfileBecauseTheirCountsComeFromIt`.
- The vote strip "Every vote in this Congress" has one mark for each vote row of the page, oldest first. A mark is tall for Yea or Aye, short for Nay or No, and very short for Present or Not Voting. A vote for a candidate, such as a vote for Speaker, has its own gray mark. A vote against most of the party has its own color. The legend names the marks "Yes", "No", "Against most of their party", and "Did not vote Yes or No". It names "Voted for a candidate" only when the strip has such a mark. `PublishedOfficialVote.IsCandidate` owns the candidate rule, for the strip and for the "Voted for" heading of the vote page. The server renders the strip from the vote rows of the page, so it needs no script. The text above the strip names the number of roll calls and the first and last day. The strip has a text alternative that gives the number of votes and the number of marks against the party. `VotesWithParty.IsBreak` owns the break rule. A page with no vote row has no strip. Enforced by `PublishedVoteStrip.razor`, `PublishedVoteStripView`, `PublishedOfficialVote.IsCandidate`, and `VotesWithParty.IsBreak`. Tests: `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheVoteStripHasOneMarkForEachRollCallOldestFirstAndMarksASplitVoteAgainstThePartyAtTheHeightOfItsCastBecauseBothAreFactsTheStripShows`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialWithNoVoteHasNoVoteStripBecauseItWouldDrawNothing`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AVoteForACandidateHasItsOwnMarkAndLegendEntrySoAReaderDoesNotTakeItForAMissedVote`. No test checks the mark heights.
- The record page of an official has a filter row of tabs. The tabs "All", one tab for each kind, "Against their party", and "Issues they work on" are the possible tabs. Each tab except the last shows the count of its rows. A list of two or more kinds has one tab for each kind. A kind tab reads such as "Statements", "Bills", or "Votes". A list of one kind has no kind tab, because the tab would repeat "All". The counts on the tabs stay fixed for the whole list. They do not narrow when another filter is on. A kind with no rows has no tab. The tab "Against their party" shows only when the list holds a break on a page with a profile. The tab "Issues they work on" shows on every page with a profile. It swaps the list for the issue pane, and any other tab swaps it back. `PublishedOfficialView.RowClass` puts a class `kind-<kind>` on each row, and the class `against-party` on a break row. The tab value is that class. `VotesWithParty.IsBreak` owns the break rule. The tabs need JavaScript. With JavaScript off, the tabs stay hidden, every row shows, and the issue pane shows below the list. Enforced by `VotesWithParty.IsBreak`, `PublishedOfficialView.RecordTabs`, `PublishedOfficialView.RowClass`, `PublishedRecordList.razor`, and `public.js`. Tests: `VotesWithPartyTests.OnlyAYeaOrNayAgainstTheMajorityOfASplitVoteIsABreak`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.OnlyARowOfASplitVoteCastAgainstMostOfTheirPartyIsMarkedAsAgainstTheParty`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheKindTabsCountEveryRowOfTheListSoATabNeverPromisesMoreThanItShows`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheKindTabsAreHiddenWithoutScriptAndNoRowIsHiddenSoTheListStaysWhole`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.ARecordsListOfOneKindHasNoKindTabsBecauseTheyCouldNotNarrowItAndANoProfilePageHasNoTabAtAll`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheAgainstPartyTabShowsOnlyWhenTheOfficialHasAVoteAgainstTheirPartyAndCountsThoseVotes`, `VotesWithPartyTests.AVoteOfAPartyThatIsNeitherDemocratNorRepublicanIsNeverABreak`, `VotesWithPartyTests.ARowWithNoPartyMajorityIsNeverABreak`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnEarlierCongressPageHasNoAgainstPartyTabEvenWithAVoteAgainstTheirParty`. No test covers the script.
- A record row shows an icon of its kind and the title as the one link of the row. It shows a line "Kind, date". It shows the cast of a vote as a pill. The link covers the whole row. A bill reads "Bill or resolution", and an executive action reads its own type. A Yea or Aye cast has a filled pill. Every other cast has a pale pill. So a color never says more than the word. A row keeps its detail line under the kind line. It is the roll call and the result of a vote, or the status line of a bill. The list shows 15 rows a page with "Previous" and "Next" when `public.js` runs. The record list has no sort header, because the list is newest first. Enforced by `PublishedRecordRows.razor`, `PublishedRecordLabels.Badge`, `PublishedOfficialView.CastClass`, and the `.record-list` rules of `public.css`. Tests: `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheCurrentPageListsStatementsActionsAndVotesNewestFirstWithTheirOwnDates`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnExecutiveActionRowShowsItsSpecificTypeAndOtherRowsShowTheirKind`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.OnlyAYesCastGetsTheFilledPillSoTheColorNeverClaimsMoreThanTheWordSays`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.ARecordRowHasOneLinkOnItsTitleThatCoversTheWholeRowAndAnIconThatIsHiddenFromReaders`. No test checks the layout.
- The current page of an official shows sections that each answer one question. The sections are "Where they break from their party", "Bills they introduced", "Issues they work on", "How they voted recently", and "What they said". A section shows the newest 5 items at most. It ends with a link "See all N ..." to the record page. The link opens the tab that holds the same items. `PublishedOfficialSectionsView.Of` owns the items, the texts, and the links. Without a profile the page shows only "What they said", and "Bills they introduced" only when the official has a bill. A section with no item shows one plain line. The lines are "No votes against most of their party.", "No bills on record.", "No votes on record.", and "No statements on record.". The section "Where they break from their party" shows only when the official cast a vote on a split vote. The page ends with a link "See all N records". A row of a section is the row of the record page, from `PublishedRecordRows`. A row of a past Congress page stays in the list on that page. Enforced by `PublishedOfficialSectionsView`, `PublishedOfficialSections.razor`, `PublishedRecordRows.razor`, and `PublishedOfficialPage.razor`. Tests: `PublishedOfficialPageTests.EachSectionShowsAtMostFiveItemsAndEndsWithASeeAllLinkToTheRecordPageSoThePageStaysShort`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.ASectionListsTheNewestItemsFirstSoTheLatestVoteAgainstThePartyComesBeforeTheOlderOnes`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.ASectionWithNothingToShowSaysSoInPlainWordsInsteadOfDisappearingSoAReaderKnowsTheAbsenceIsReal`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnOfficialWithNoProfileShowsOnlyTheStatementsSectionAndABillSectionOnlyWhenTheyHaveBills`.
- A row of links under the key figures leads to each shown section by its anchor. The section "Issues they work on" shows one chip for each tracked issue with its count of records. The count is the number of statements, sponsored bills, and substantive votes on the issue, from `TalkAndActionProfile.RecordsOn`. The chip opens the record page with `?issue=` and the issue ID, and the page shows the rows of that issue. Enforced by `PublishedOfficialSections.razor`, `TalkAndActionProfile.RecordsOn`, and `public.js`. Tests: `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheJumpRowLinksEachShownSectionByItsAnchorAndNoOtherSoNoLinkLeadsNowhere`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnIssueChipLinksTheRecordPageFilteredToItsIssueAndItsCountEqualsTheRowsThatFilterShows`. No test covers the script.
- Each official has a record page at `officials/{id}/record.html`, `PublicationProtocol.OfficialRecordPagePath`. It holds every record of the official in one list with the tabs, the pager, and the issue pane. It reads two query values: `tab`, the value of a tab, and `issue`, an issue ID. The same code of `public.js` that runs the tabs applies both, so the page has one tab mechanism. A past Congress page keeps its list on the page. A publish writes the record page with the official page. A carried official links both. An untouched official whose previous release has no record page counts as touched, so a link of a section never leads nowhere. The sitemap lists the record page, because the release site remembers each page that it writes or links. Enforced by `ReleaseOfficialPages`, `ReleaseOfficialVotes.ListAsync`, `ReleaseSite`, `PublishedOfficialRecordPage.razor`, `PublishedRecordList.razor`, and `public.js`. Tests: `PublishedOfficialPageTests.EverySeeAllLinkNamesATabOrIssueThatTheRecordPageHasSoTheLinkOpensTheListItPromises`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheRecordPageHoldsEveryRecordWithTheFullTabsAndThePagerWhileTheOfficialPageHoldsNoFullList`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheRecordPageSitsTwoFoldersDeepSoItsAssetsAndLinksResolveFromTheReleaseRootAndItLinksBackToTheOfficialPage`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.EveryPublishWritesTheRecordPageBesideTheOfficialPageAndLinksItWhenTheOfficialIsUntouchedSoTheSeeAllLinksNeverBreak`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.AnUntouchedOfficialWhosePreviousReleaseHasNoRecordPageGetsOneBecauseALinkedPageThatIsMissingWouldBeADeadLink`, `PublishSiteFilesTests.TheSitemapListsEveryPageOfTheReleaseSoASearchEngineFindsPagesNoLinkReaches`.
- Each nonzero count in the issue pane is a button that filters the record list to the rows the count counted. Without JavaScript the counts stay plain text and every row shows. `TalkAndActionColumn` keeps the record IDs behind each count, and `TalkAndActionProfile.CountKeysByRecord` gives each row the keys, such as `vote:housing`, from those same IDs. A count and its filter cannot differ, because the count is the number of IDs. The votes key follows the votes count: substantive votes with a cast other than Not Voting. The keys of a row show in its `data-filter-keys` attribute, joined by spaces. This needs an issue ID with no space, which the slug rule of `IssueConfiguration.ValidateId` gives. The script hides every row without the key. A count filter and a kind tab combine with AND: a row shows only when it passes both. The script hides a row with the `hidden` attribute on the row itself. The sort code reorders the same row elements, so a sort never shows a hidden row, and clearing a filter keeps the sort. The record list holds an empty status line from page load. The line stays empty until a filter is on, and goes empty again when the filters clear. With a filter on, the script writes "4 of 52 records match" there, so a screen reader announces each change. When no row passes both filters, the line adds that no record matches both filters. The script also shows the sentence "Filtered to their statements on Health in the 119th Congress" and a button "Show all records". The button resets the kind tab and the count filter. A click on a count also closes the issue pane and shows the list. A second click on the count removes only the count filter. A count of 0 is plain text. Each count prints its unit, such as "1 bill", "0 bills", or "3 statements", from `PublishedOfficialView.Counted`. The accessible name of the button starts with that same text, so a voice control user can say what the button shows. The issue table clamps each bill title to 2 lines with the `clamp-2` CSS rule. The link keeps the full title in its `title` attribute. Enforced by `TalkAndActionColumn`, `TalkAndActionProfile.CountKeysByRecord`, `PublishedOfficialView.Rows`, `PublishedOfficialView.CountFilterNotice`, `PublishedOfficialView.CountFilterLabel`, `PublishedOfficialView.Counted`, `CountFilter.razor`, `DataTable.razor`, the `clamp-2` rule in `app.css`, and `public.js`. Tests: `PublishedOfficialPageTests.ABillTitleIsClampedToTwoLinesAndItsLinkKeepsTheFullTitle`, `TalkAndActionProfileTests.TheCountKeysOfTheRecordsMatchTheCountsSoAFilteredListCannotDifferFromItsCount`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.EachCountButtonNamesTheRowsItFiltersAndTheNumberShownEqualsTheCountClicked`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.OnlyARowInTheCurrentCongressCarriesAStatementOrBillMarkerAndOnlyASubstantiveCastVoteCarriesAVoteMarker`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheServerHtmlHasPlainCountsWithAZeroNeverAButtonAndEveryRowVisible`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheFilterNoticeStatesWhatIsShownInPlainWordsAndOffersToShowAllRecords`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.AnEmptyListWithNoProfileHasNoRecordsFilterMarkup`, and `PublishedOfficialPageTests.APageWithNoProfileStillFiltersByKindButHasNoIssueNotice`. No test covers the script.
- The vote page shows a badge only for a vote that is split or where both parties agreed. A split vote is the split vote of the profile, and its line names the side of each party. Both parties agreed when the vote is substantive and the Democrats and the Republicans have the same majority. A tie gives no majority, so a tie gives no badge. A procedural vote and a vote with candidate totals get no badge. Each badge line says "who cast Yea or Nay", because the majority ignores Present and Not voting. The badge panel also gives the Yea and Nay count of each party, with every member counted, tracked or not. It says that these counts can differ from the source totals, and that Aye counts as Yea. On a split vote only, a panel lists the tracked members who cast Yea or Nay against the majority of their own party. It uses the break rule of the profile. A current independent counts with the caucus. A former independent counts with no party. A member with another party or an unknown party is never listed. A break row shows the name and the cast, with the word of the source such as Aye or No, and no source line. A party with no break shows one line and no heading. Enforced by `PublishedVote.IsSplit`, `PublishedVote.PartiesAgree`, `VotesWithParty.IsAgainstPartyMajority`, `PublishedVoteView.Badge`, and `PublishedVoteView.Breakers`. Tests: `VotesWithPartyTests.ASplitVoteRowWithoutAPartyMajorityIsNeverABreak`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AFormerIndependentIsInNeitherPartyCountNorTheBreakList`, `PublishedVoteTests.PartiesAgreeOnlyWhenBothHaveTheSameMajoritySoATieIsNeitherAgreementNorSplit`, `PublishedVoteTests.AProceduralVoteAndACandidateVoteNeverHavePartiesThatAgree`, `ReleaseRendererTests.ASplitVoteNamesEachPartySideAndListsOnlyTheMembersWhoBrokeWithTheirPartyByParty`, `ReleaseRendererTests.ASplitVoteWhereNoMemberBrokeSaysSoInsteadOfShowingAnEmptyList`, `ReleaseRendererTests.ASenateSplitVoteNamesTheSenateInEveryPartyLine`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AnAyeOrNoCastBreaksFromThePartyLikeAYeaOrNayAndTheRowKeepsTheWordOfTheSource`, `ReleaseRendererTests.ASplitVoteSaysWhoCastYeaOrNayBecausePresentMembersDoNotSetTheMajority`, `ReleaseRendererTests.APairOfPartiesThatAgreeByOneMemberShowsTheCountsSoAThinMarginIsNotHidden`, `ReleaseRendererTests.ASpeakerElectionGetsNoBadgeAndNoBreakPanelBecauseItHasNoYeaOrNayTotals`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.ATiedPartyAndAProceduralVoteGetNoBadgeBecauseNeitherCountsAsASplitVote`.
- The share threshold has one owner, `TalkAndActionProfile.ShareOf`. The missed votes and the votes with their party use it, and show a percent only at `TalkAndActionProfile.MinimumRecordsForShare` records (5). Enforced by `TalkAndActionProfile.ShareOf`, `MissedVotes`, and `VotesWithParty`. Tests: `MissedVotesTests`, `VotesWithPartyTests`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.ThePercentOfVotesWithTheirPartyShowsOnlyAtTheThreshold`.
- A withdrawn record leaves the manifest and the vote files, so it leaves the profile. The profile adds no touch rule, but the pairs do; see above. A publish builds the profile only for a touched official. Enforced by `ReleaseOfficialPages`. Tests: `PublishWithdrawalsTests.AWithdrawnStatementNoLongerCountsInTheTalkAndActionProfile`, `PublishOfficialPagesTests.APublishRewritesOnlyThePagesOfTheOfficialsItTouchesAndLinksEveryOtherPage`.
- A vote row holds the kind and the tracked issue IDs of its vote. It also holds the result, the totals, the party of the official, and the majority cast of that party. The majority is empty when the party tied or had no Yea or Nay cast. The totals and the majority are empty for a vote with candidate totals. The row also holds the required flag `splitVote`; see the split vote invariants below. The issue IDs follow the rules of the manifest entry filters. Enforced by `PublishedOfficialVote.Validate` and `PublishedRecordEntry.AreFilterValues`. Tests: `PublishOfficialVotesTests.AVoteRowCarriesTheRollCallBillCastHeadlineKindAndIssuesSoTheProfilePageNeedsNoRecordFile`, `PublishedOfficialVotesTests.IssueIdsThatTheManifestEntryWouldRefuseAreRefusedOnTheRow`, `PublishedOfficialVotesTests.AnUndefinedKindIsRefused`, `PublishedOfficialVotesTests.ARowRefusesAPartyMajorityThatIsNeitherYeaNorNay`, `PublishVoteTests.TheRecordCarriesTheResultTheTotalsAndEachPartyMajorityFromTheSavedLines`.
- Every release holds `method.html`, written before the manifest. `PublishedMethodPage` is the one owner of the text, and the issue panel links to it. Enforced by `ReleaseIndexPages` and `PublishedOfficialPage`. Tests: `PublishReleaseTests.EveryIndexPageIsWrittenBeforeTheManifestSoALongReleaseIsNeverTruncated`, `PublishedOfficialPageTests.TheIssuePanelShowsPlainCountsAndTheTitlesOfTheSponsoredBillsAndLeavesOutProceduralVotes`, `ReleaseRendererTests.TheMethodPageNamesTheSubstantiveVoteKindsAndTheThreeIssueCountsFromTheirOwners`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.TheMethodPageSaysWhereSenateVotesComeFromAndThatNoVicePresidentTieBreakVoteIsListed`.
- Every release holds `glossary.html`, written before the manifest. `Glossary` is the one owner of each term, its meaning, why it matters, and its source. The page lists every term from that list. A `Term` component shows the same definition as a browser popover. Only the first use of a term on a page is a popover. Later uses are plain text. `GlossaryPageUse` tracks the terms that `PublicPage` has shown. A term that is not in the list fails the render. The approved wording changes only with the approval of the owner. Enforced by `Glossary`, `Term`, `GlossaryPageUse`, `PublishedGlossaryPage`, and `ReleaseIndexPages`. Tests: `GlossaryTests.TheApprovedWordingIsUnchangedBecauseOnlyTheOwnerMayApproveANewWord`, `GlossaryTests.TheGlossaryPageListsEveryTermWithItsMeaningReasonAndSourceAndEveryPartyCountRule`, `GlossaryTests.ATermIsAPopoverOnItsFirstUseOnlyAndPlainTextAfterThat`, `GlossaryTests.ATermIsAButtonWithAPopoverHoldingTheApprovedMeaningAndTheFooterLinksToTheGlossaryPage`, `ReleaseRendererTests.EachCastWordOnTheVotePageKeepsItsTermSoAReaderCanLookUpYeaNayPresentAndNotVoting`, `ReleaseRendererTests.TheKindOfAVoteIsATermAndKeepsItsVisibleLabel`, `PublishReleaseTests.EveryIndexPageIsWrittenBeforeTheManifestSoALongReleaseIsNeverTruncated`.
- Every release holds `report.html`, written before the manifest. Without the service address or the Turnstile site key, it says that reporting is not available. It then holds no form, no key, and no Turnstile script. Enforced by `ReleaseIndexPages` and `PublishedReportPage`. Tests: `PublishReleaseTests.EveryIndexPageIsWrittenBeforeTheManifestSoALongReleaseIsNeverTruncated` and `ReleaseRendererTests.ReportPageWithoutTheServiceAddressOrTheSiteKeySaysThatReportingIsNotAvailableAndShowsNoFormOrKey`.
- The report page lists every category of `ReaderReportProtocol` with a label, so a new category reaches the page. `PublishedRecordLabels.ReportCategory` throws for a category with no label. The Turnstile script loads on this page only. Enforced by `PublishedReportPage` and `PublicPage`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.ReportPageHoldsEveryProtocolCategoryWithALabelAndPostsToTheConfiguredServiceWithTheSiteKey` and `ReleaseRendererTests.OnlyTheReportPageLoadsTheTurnstileScript`.
- The report script checks the record ID and revision from the query with the rules of the service before it shows the form. It posts a form-encoded body, shows one message for each status, and resets the Turnstile token after a failed send. Enforced by `public.js`. No test, because the repository has no JavaScript test harness. The service refuses a bad form with 400 whatever the script does.
- Each page this renderer writes has a description of at most 160 characters, and `og:description` repeats it. Enforced by `PublicHead`, which `PublicPage` applies to every page. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryPageTypeHasABoundedDescriptionThatOpenGraphRepeats`.
- The site navigation holds Officials, Issues, and Votes and bills. Votes and bills leads to the all-records list. Only the home page has a search form in the header, with the placeholder "Search bills, votes, people". It sends its query to `search.html`. The footer holds the name Civic Lens and a navigation with How we check records, Glossary, About, and Report a problem. How we check records leads to the method page. The footer has no link to the weekly briefs; the home page links to them when a brief exists. A page can mark a footer link as current. Every page has a site navigation and a footer navigation. Each link in them leads to a page that every release writes, and a page in a subfolder reaches it through `RootPath`. The site navigation marks at most one link with `aria-current="page"`, the section of the page. `PublicSections` owns the link lists. `PublicPage` renders them and shows the search form when a page sets `HasSearch`. Each page passes its `PublicSection`. Tests: `PublicFoundationTests.TheHeaderHoldsThreeSectionsAndTheFooterHoldsTheOtherPagesSoThePhoneHeaderStaysShort`, `ReleaseRendererTests.OnlyTheHomePageHasASearchBoxSoOtherPagesStayFocusedOnTheirOwnContent`, `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryPageTypeMarksItsSectionInTheNavigationOrNoneWhenItBelongsToNoSection` and `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryNavigationAndFooterLinkOfEveryPageTypeLeadsToAPageTheReleaseWritesSoNoReaderReachesA404`.
- The theme control of the public header is one icon button, `PublicThemeMenu`. It opens a popover with three choices: Light, Match system, and Dark. The choices use `data-theme-choice`, so `app.js` keeps the saved choice in the `civic-lens-theme` key and `theme.js` applies it before paint. The button shows only with scripts on. Enforced by `PublicThemeMenu` and `app.js`. Test: `PublicFoundationTests.TheThemeMenuOffersLightSystemAndDarkAndKeepsTheStoredChoiceKeys`.
- The public frame has one width rule. `body.public` sets `--content` to 1280 pixels and `--gutter` to 32 pixels, or 16 pixels at 760 pixels width and below. `--frame-pad` turns them into the side padding of the header, the main area, and the footer. The token `--t-h1` (40 to 64 pixels) sets the page heading size. The token `--t-h2` (24 pixels) sets the panel heading size. Enforced by `theme.css` and `public.css`. No test. Checked in Blink at 360 and 1280 pixels, in both themes.
- A page has a canonical link and `og:url` only when the base URL is set. Both hold the base URL joined with the release path of the page, and the home page is the base URL with a slash. Search names the bare `search.html`, never a query, and a paged page names its own page. Enforced by `PublicPage`, `PublicBaseUrl.Join`, and the `CanonicalPath` that every page passes. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryPageTypeNamesItsOwnAddressAsCanonicalAndOpenGraphUrlSoSearchEnginesKeepOneAddressForEachPage`, `ReleaseRendererTests.TheSearchPageNamesTheBarePageAsCanonicalSoAQueryInTheAddressNeverBecomesAnotherPage`, `ReleaseRendererTests.APagePastTheFirstNamesItsOwnPageAsCanonicalSoPagesAreNotMergedIntoTheFirst`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.WithoutABaseUrlNoPageHasACanonicalOrOpenGraphUrlSoNoPageNamesAnAddressThatMayBeWrong`.
- The base URL is a bare origin: https, or http only for `localhost` and `127.0.0.1`, with a host and no user, path, query, or fragment. A blank value means unset. An invalid value stops the publish. Enforced by `PublicBaseUrl.Parse`, which `ReleaseRenderer.FromEnvironment` calls. `ReviewWorkspace.RunAsync` also calls it at start, with no test for that start path. `PublicBaseUrl` stores the normalized origin. The scheme and host are in lower case, and an IDN host is in punycode. The port stays only when it is not the default. The form `/.` gives the bare origin. A trailing backslash, a host that ends in a dot, and an IPv6 address are refused. Tests: `PublicBaseUrlTests.ABareOriginIsAcceptedAndStoredAsTheNormalOriginWithoutATrailingSlashSoEveryJoinHasOneSlash`, `PublicBaseUrlTests.AnythingBeyondAnHttpsOriginIsRefusedAndTheMessageNamesTheVariableSoTheOwnerCanFixIt`, and `ReleaseCommandTests.PublishingRefusesAnInvalidPublicBaseUrlBeforeAnyWorkSoNoReleaseCarriesAWrongCanonicalAddress`.
- The sitemap lists exactly the `.html` pages that the release writes or links, without `search.html`, `report.html`, `404.html`, and the withdrawn notices. It has no query address and no `lastmod`. It fails the publish over 50,000 addresses or 50 MB. Enforced by `ReleaseSite`, which records every path that staging receives. Tests: `PublishSiteFilesTests.TheSitemapListsEveryPageOfTheReleaseSoASearchEngineFindsPagesNoLinkReaches`, `PublishSiteFilesTests.TheSitemapLeavesOutSearchReportTheNotFoundPageAndWithdrawnNoticesBecauseReadersShouldNotLandOnThem`, `PublishSiteFilesTests.TheSitemapAddressesAreSortedSoTheSameReleaseAlwaysGivesTheSameFile`, `PublishSiteFilesTests.TheSitemapListsAFacetPageBeyondTheFirstAndAPastCongressPageOfAnOfficialSoSearchEnginesFindDeepPages`, and `PublishSiteFilesTests.ThePublishFailsInsteadOfWritingASitemapThatSearchEnginesRejectForHavingTooManyAddresses`.
- `robots.txt` allows every crawler and names the sitemap. Without the base URL a release holds no `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, or `404.html`. Enforced by `ReleaseSite.WriteSiteFilesAsync`, written before the manifest. Tests: `PublishSiteFilesTests.ARobotsFileAllowsEveryoneAndPointsToTheSitemap` and `PublishSiteFilesTests.WithoutABaseUrlTheReleaseHasNoSitemapRobotsFileOrNotFoundPageSoItStaysAsBefore`.
- `404.html` links every asset and page by absolute address, because Caddy serves it at any missing path. It has `noindex` and no canonical link. Enforced by `PublishedNotFoundPage`, whose `RootPath` is the base URL, and by `PublicPage`, whose `CanonicalPath` is null for this page. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryAssetNavigationAndFooterLinkOfTheNotFoundPageIsAbsoluteBecauseTheCdnServesItAtAnyMissingPath` and `ReleaseRendererTests.OnlyPagesThatAReaderShouldNotLandOnFromASearchEngineAskToBeSkipped`.
- Every kind link on the all-records pages holds a kind value that the search service accepts. The row has a link only for a kind that the release holds. Enforced by `PublishedAllRecordsPage` and `PublicationProtocol.KindText`. No test covers the script `public.js`, which reads `kind` from the address. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.AllRecordsPageLinksEachKindToASearchAddressSoTheServiceAcceptsTheLink`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AllRecordsPageOffersNoLinkForAKindWithoutRecordsSoNoLinkLeadsToAnEmptySearch`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.AllRecordsPageOfAnEmptyReleaseHasNoKindRowSoNoEmptyNavigationShows`.
- Only `404.html`, the withdrawal notices, `search.html`, and `report.html` have `noindex`. Enforced by the `NoIndex` parameter of `PublicPage`, which these four pages set. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.OnlyPagesThatAReaderShouldNotLandOnFromASearchEngineAskToBeSkipped`. The sitemap leaves out the same pages through its own list in `ReleaseSite.Sitemap`; see the sitemap invariant.
- The pages of one paged list have different titles, and the page number survives a long name. Two withdrawal notices have different titles. Record titles come from the headline and have no disambiguator. Enforced by `PublicHead.DocumentTitle` and `PublishedWithdrawalPage`. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.PagesOfOneListHaveDifferentTitlesSoSearchResultsAndTabsCanTellThemApart`, `ReleaseRendererTests.APagedTitleKeepsItsPageNumberWhenTheNameIsLongAndOnePageHasNoPageNumber`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.TwoWithdrawalsHaveDifferentTitlesSoTabsAndSearchResultsCanTellThemApart`.
- A page in a subfolder links to assets, records, and pager targets through a `../` prefix. Enforced by `PublishedFacetPage`. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.FacetPageLinksResolveFromItsSubfolderAndPageThroughItsRecordsNewestEventDateFirst`.
- The code subject may publish records of any kind, and it withdraws nothing. It returns a withdrawn record only for a person approval of a higher revision. The model author never publishes. Enforced by `PublishedRecordLoader.RequirePublishable`, `PublishRelease` through `ReviewAuthorization.RequireOwner`, `PublicationWithdrawals`, and `PostgresPublicationStore.CommitAsync` for the model author. Tests: `PublishWithdrawalsTests.TheWorkerReturnsAWithdrawnRecordWhenAPersonApprovedAHigherRevisionWithANoteAndKeepsTheHistory`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.TheCodeSubjectCanPublishAnyKindBecauseTheWorkerPublishesEveryApprovedRecord`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.TheAnalysisSubjectStillCannotPublish`, `PublishWithdrawalsTests.TheCodeSubjectCannotWithdrawBecauseOnlyAPersonJudgesThatARecordShouldGo`, and `PostgresPublicationStoreTests.TheModelAuthorCannotCommitARelease`.
- An automatic release holds approved records of every kind, whoever approved them. A person approval needs no owner step. A person replacement without a note, or over the correction cap, is left out and logged, and the other records of the batch publish. A brief whose cited records changed does not list. Enforced by `PublishedRecordLoader.ListReadyAsync`, `PublishedRecordRules.IsApprovedByCode`, and `PublicationCorrections.For`, which throws `MissingCorrectionNoteException` or `CorrectionLimitException`. `UnpublishableRecord` tags both, and `PublishApprovedRecords` leaves the record out. Tests: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AStatementThatAPersonApprovedPublishesWithNoOwnerStepBecauseEveryApprovalPublishes`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.APersonApprovalThatReplacesAPublishedRecordIsLeftOutWithALogUntilAPersonApprovesARevisionWithANoteWhileTheOthersPublish`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.APersonReplacementAtTheNotedCorrectionCapIsLeftOutWithALogWhileTheOthersPublish`, and `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.ABriefWhoseCitedRecordsChangedDoesNotPublishButAStatementBesideItDoes`.
- A ready list of one kind alternates published replacements and new records, a replacement first, each oldest first by creation time and ID. While new records of the kind wait, replacements take at most `limit / 2`, which rounds down at the store. Limit 65 gives 32 replacements and 33 new records. Limit 1 gives only the new record. With no new record, replacements may use the whole list. The bound holds for each kind. A whole batch can hold more than half replacements when other kinds have no new records. The share cut takes the front of each list, so it keeps the alternation. A correction reaches readers soon, and a flow of corrections cannot hold back new records. Enforced by `ReviewRecords.ReadUnpublishedApprovedAsync` and `PublishApprovedRecords.ShareLimitBetweenKinds`. Tests: `PostgresExecutiveActionReviewStoreTests.TheReadyListShowsAReplacementFirstThenTheOldestBecauseACorrectionMustReachReadersSoonest`, `PostgresExecutiveActionReviewStoreTests.ReplacementsTakeAtMostHalfTheBatchWhileNewRecordsWaitBecauseCorrectionsMustNotHoldBackNewRecords`, `PostgresExecutiveActionReviewStoreTests.ReplacementsAlternateWithNewRecordsUpToHalfTheLimitSoACutFromTheFrontKeepsTheBound`, and `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.TheShareCutKeepsTheFrontOfAKindInTheOrderTheStoreListedItSoTheStoreAlternationSurvives`.
- Each kind keeps an equal share of a batch, so a large backlog of one kind cannot crowd out the others. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords.ShareLimitBetweenKinds`. Test: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.EveryKindKeepsItsShareOfABatchSoOneLargeBacklogDoesNotCrowdOutTheOthers`.
- A ready list reads the published revisions of its kind as one map. The cost grows linearly with the drafts of the kind and the entries of the active release. Enforced by `ReviewQueries.UnpublishedApproved`, which every kind uses. Test: `PostgresBillStreamTests.TheReadyListStaysFastWhenTheActiveReleaseHoldsThousandsOfActionsBecauseItLooksUpOneMapOfPublishedRevisions`.
- A wake makes at most one release of up to 64 records, and no release when nothing is ready. A backlog needs one wake for each 64 records. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords`. Tests: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AWakeMakesOneReleaseOfAtMostTheReleaseLimitAndTheNextWakeTakesTheRest` and `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.NothingReadyMakesNoRelease`.
- The idempotency key names each record and revision of the release. A retry after a crash replays the stored release. A replay returns a release number that is not new, and the tick counts nothing. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords`. Test: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.ARetryOfTheSameReadyRecordsReplaysTheStoredReleaseInsteadOfMakingAnother`.
- After a rollback the stage makes no release. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords`. Test: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AfterARollbackATickMakesNoReleaseSoTheOwnerChoiceStands`.
- A record that cannot build is left out and reported, and the others publish in one release. Enforced by `PublishedRecordLoader.LoadAsync`, which tags the validation exception with the record through `UnpublishableRecord`, and by `PublishApprovedRecords`. Test: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.ARecordThatCannotBuildIsLeftOutAndReportedWhileTheOthersPublishInOneRelease`.
- A conflict ends the tick and is not a failure. Any other failure reaches the caller and counts as a failure. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords` and `StageWorker`. Tests: `PublishingWorkerTests.AConflictIsNotAFailureBecauseTheNextTickBuildsAgain` and `PublishingWorkerTests.AFailureThatIsNotAConflictIsReportedAndCountedSoTheOwnerSeesIt`.
- Without a notification, the deadline of the stage is the next full hour of the database clock. Enforced by `PostgresIntervalWakeup`. Tests: `PostgresIntervalWakeupTests.TheHourlyWakeupDoesNotReturnBeforeItsDeadlineBecauseNothingNotifiesIt` and `PostgresIntervalWakeupTests.ALostNotificationStillWakesAtTheIntervalDeadlineBecauseTheTimerIsTheFallback`.
- A draft sends one notification when it is inserted as `Approved`, becomes `Approved`, or gets a new revision while `Approved`. Code approval needs the first and last case. A new fact record is inserted already approved. A code-approved new revision keeps the status. Any other change, and a repeat of `Approved` with the same revision, sends none. All seven draft tables follow this rule. Enforced by the function `civic_lens_notify_publishing_approval` and its two triggers on each table, in the migration `PublishOnApprovalNotify`. Tests: `PublishingApprovalNotifyTests.ApprovingARecordSendsTheGroupingDeadlineBecauseTheWorkerPublishesOnlyAfterTheGroupingWindow`, `PublishingApprovalNotifyTests.ANewRecordThatIsApprovedOnInsertSendsTheDeadlineBecauseCodeApprovesAFactRecordInItsFirstWrite`, `PublishingApprovalNotifyTests.ANewRevisionOfAnApprovedRecordSendsTheDeadlineBecauseTheStatusStaysApprovedWhenCodeApprovesIt`, `PublishingApprovalNotifyTests.OnlyAnApprovedRevisionOrStatusNotifiesBecauseOtherChangesLeaveNothingToPublish`, `PostgresBillStreamTests.ACodeApprovedFirstRevisionNotifiesPublishingBecauseTheDraftIsInsertedAlreadyApproved`, and `PostgresBillStreamTests.ACodeApprovedNewRevisionNotifiesPublishingBecauseTheStatusStaysApprovedAndOnlyTheRevisionNumberChanges`. The wakeup listens on the same channel as the trigger. Enforced by the channel constant in `PostgresPublishingWakeup`. Test: `PublishingApprovalNotifyTests.ApprovingADraftWakesThePublishingWakeupBecauseTheTriggerAndTheWakeupMustShareOneChannel`.
- A wake makes no release while the newest release is younger than 10 minutes. The database clock measures the age against the stored time of the release with the newest time. The CLI publish and the status-only release count as releases. The CLI publish itself has no limit. The gap is the constant `PublishApprovedRecords.ReleaseGap`. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords` and `PostgresPublicationStore.ReadAgeOfNewestReleaseAsync`. Tests: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AWakeInsideTheGapAfterTheNewestReleaseMakesNoReleaseBecausePublishesCostDisk`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.ApprovalsDuringAPublishDoNotCauseAReleaseInsideTheGapBecauseTheNextWakeSeesTheNewRelease`, and `PostgresPublishingWakeupTests.TheAgeOfTheNewestReleaseComesFromTheNewestStoredTimeNotTheHighestNumberBecauseTheGapFollowsTheLastPublish`.
- The stage wakes when the gap after the newest release ends, also after a worker restart. A backlog therefore drains with no new approval while each wake succeeds and the release has room. A failure, a conflict, or the record cap stops the drain. Enforced by `PostgresPublishingWakeup`, which reads `publication_releases`. Test: `PostgresPublishingWakeupTests.TheWakeupFiresWhenTheGapAfterTheNewestReleaseEndsAndAFreshWakeupAfterARestartStillFiresThere`.
- A tick that fails or ends on a conflict starts the same gap on the clock of the process. Wakes inside that gap make no attempt and schedule nothing. A restart clears it. A conflict on a status-only release starts the gap too. The gap-end wake of the newest release often falls inside the failure gap and does nothing. A retry can then wait for the next full hour, up to about 70 minutes. Enforced by `PublishApprovedRecords`. Tests: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AFailedTickIsNotRetriedInsideTheGapBecauseAFailingPublishMustNotRunOnEveryWake`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AConflictStartsTheGapToo`, and `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AConflictOnAStatusOnlyReleaseStartsTheGapBecauseTheNextWakeMustNotRetryAtOnce`.
- A status-only release runs only on a wake that passed a full hour of the database clock, and on the first pass after a start. The wake for the end of the gap and the wake for an approval make none, unless it is the first wake past the hour. A wake reports this through `IIntervalWakeup.IntervalElapsed`. Enforced by `PostgresDeadlineWakeup` and `PublishApprovedRecords`. Tests: `PostgresIntervalWakeupTests.AWakeAtTheIntervalReportsItAndAWakeFromANotificationDoesNotBecauseHourlyWorkMustWaitForTheHour`, `PostgresIntervalWakeupTests.ANotificationWakeAfterACrossedBoundaryReportsItBecauseNoIntervalWakeMayBeLost`, and `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AStatusOnlyReleaseRunsOnlyOnTheHourlyWakeBecauseTheGapEndWakeIsForTheBacklog`.
- A release that would pass the record cap (`PublicationProtocol.MaximumRecordsPerRelease`) is not a failure of the pass. A replacement or a return adds no entry, so it always fits. The new records share the free slots, as the kinds share a batch, and the rest wait. The stage logs one error that counts the waiting records of that batch, and the pass publishes the fitting records. With no free slot, the pass can still make a status-only release, but only on the wake of a full hour. `worker health` also counts the cap from the real backlog. It counts the records and withdrawals of the active release, and the approved records that no active release holds. It prints the cap state and exits 3 when the worker is healthy and new records wait for room. A dead worker exits 1. It needs `CIVIC_LENS_RELEASE_DIRECTORY` for the withdrawal count. Enforced by `ReadReleaseCapacity` and `WorkerHealthCommand`. Tests: `WorkerHealthReleaseCapacityTests.AHealthyWorkerWithAFullReleaseExitsWithTheReleaseFullCodeBecauseTheCapNeedsAnOwnerAndNotARestart` and `ReadReleaseCapacityTests.TheHeldCountAddsWithdrawalsToRecordsBecauseTheyShareTheCap`. The CLI publish still refuses an over-cap request with `ReleaseFullException`. Enforced by `PublishRelease.RequireRoom` and `PublishApprovedRecords.LeaveOutWhatDoesNotFit`. Tests: `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AtTheRecordCapAReplacementAndAsManyNewRecordsAsFitPublishAndTheRestWaitWithAnError`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AtTheRecordCapWithNoFreeSlotAReplacementStillPublishesAndNewRecordsWait`, `PublishApprovedRecordsTests.AtTheRecordCapAStatusOnlyReleaseStillPublishesWhileNewRecordsWaitWithAnError`, `PublishWithdrawalsTests.AReturnTakesTheSlotOfItsWithdrawalSoOnlyAnotherNewRecordIsTurnedAwayAtTheCap`, and `PublishReleaseTests.RejectsMoreRecordsThanTheCapBeforeAnythingIsBuilt`.
- A burst of approval notifications wakes the stage once, at the earliest deadline. A later approval never moves the deadline. Enforced by `PostgresDeadlineWakeup`. Test: `PostgresIntervalWakeupTests.ABurstOfApprovalNotificationsWakesOnceAtTheEarliestDeadlineBecauseTheDeadlineMustNotSlide`. The stage ticks again after each deadline. Enforced by `StageWorker`. Test: `StageWorkerTests.ARunThatIsNotOnceSleepsUntilTheWakeupAndThenPassesAgain`.
- A statement record holds `textOrigin` (`model` or `person`) and `approvedBy` (`code` or `person`). `PublishedStatementBuilder` sets `textOrigin` from the author of the published revision: the analysis author gives `model`, a person gives `person`. `PublishedRecordRules.ApproverOf` sets `approvedBy` from the actor of the latest approval of that revision. It shares `LatestApproval` with `GetApprovalTime`, so one rule picks the decision. `PublishedStatement.Validate` refuses `code` with `person` text, because code never judges words that a person wrote. A file without these fields reads as `model` and `person`. Only files from before code approval of statements omit them, and each of those holds a model revision that a person approved. Tests: `PublishStatementTests.AStatementPublishesItsTextOriginAndApproverSeparatelyBecauseAPersonCanApproveAiText`, `PublishedRecordRulesTests.ApproverAndTimeComeFromTheSameDecisionWhenCodeApprovedThenAPersonApprovedAfterAReopen`, `PublishedStatementTests.RejectsCodeApprovalOfPersonTextBecauseCodeNeverJudgesWordsThatAPersonWrote`, and `PublishedStatementTests.AFileWithoutOriginAndApproverReadsAsModelTextThatAPersonApprovedBecauseThoseFilesPredateCodeApproval`.
- A statement page that code approved says "Checked by code on" with the time, and its timeline says "Checked by code". When the text is `model`, the page adds the wording "An AI summary of this release." and labels the policy areas "chosen by AI". The wording comes from `PublishedRecordLabels.Wording`, as for actions and votes, and only the text differs. A statement that a person approved keeps "Approved", with no AI label, even when the text is `model`. `PublishedRecordLabels` owns the wording and `PublishedStatementView` and `PublishedTimelineView` use it. The statement date of a code-approved statement carries this label beside it: "Date found by an AI model and checked by code against the quote". No person confirmed the date. The page derives this from `approvedBy` and `statementDateEvidence`. `PublishedRecordLabels.StatementDate` owns the text. A person-approved statement shows its date with no label. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.ACodeApprovedStatementWithADateSaysAModelFoundItAndCodeCheckedItBecauseAReaderMustKnowNoPersonConfirmedTheDate`, `ReleaseRendererTests.APersonApprovedStatementShowsItsDateWithoutTheCodeReadLabel`, `ReleaseRendererTests.ACodeApprovedAiStatementIsLabelledAnAiSummaryCheckedByCodeBecauseReadersMustNotThinkAPersonApprovedIt`, `ReleaseRendererTests.APersonApprovedStatementKeepsItsApprovedWordingAndShowsNoAiLabel`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.AnAiStatementThatAPersonApprovedShowsApprovedAndNoAiLabelBecauseAPersonApprovedIt`.
- The search service reads a statement record with `textOrigin` and `approvedBy`, and it indexes neither. Enforced by `RecordReader`. Test: `RecordReaderTests.ACodeApprovedAiStatementIsIndexedBecauseTheSearchServiceMustAcceptTheNewFieldsInTheSameDeploy`.
- Every date that a public page writes uses one form, "27 Mar 2026". A date with a time adds ", 14:05 UTC". Three owners write the form. `Moment.Day` and `Moment.Full` own it in the Host. `formatDay` in `public.js` owns it for search results. `ActionCodeText` owns it for the introduced date of the code text of an action, because Application cannot call `Moment`.
