A stored revision keeps the wording it was written with. Machine values stay as they are: `datetime` attributes, `data-sort-value`, sort keys, and JSON. Source text, quotes, and model text keep their own form. The tenure text of an official ("In the House since January 2025") and the glossary are outside the rule. Enforced by those owners. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryPublicPageShowsDatesInTheOneDayMonthYearForm` for the Host pages and `ActionCodeTextTests` for the code text. No test for `formatDay`.
- The role `civic_lens_public` reads only the `public_api` views. It holds no privilege on any relation outside `public_api`, it is a member of no role, and it executes no function outside `pg_catalog` and `public_api`. It can write no row, create no temporary table, and take no advisory lock. The grants step revokes `TEMPORARY` on the database and `EXECUTE` on the `pg_advisory_*` and `pg_try_advisory_*` functions from `PUBLIC`. The app role is the database owner and a superuser, so it needs no grant. Enforced by `PublicApiGrants`, which `db migrate` runs after the migrations. Tests: `PublicApiViewsTests.TheGrantsStepRestoresWhatARestoreWithNoPrivilegesDropsBecauseDbMigrateMustCloseTheGapAfterARestore` checks the grants after a restore, `PublicApiViewsTests.TheRoleHoldsNoPrivilegeOutsideThePublicApiSchemaBecauseNothingElseMayBeReachable` checks the whole catalog, `PublicApiViewsTests.TheRoleReadsNoBaseTableAndWritesNothingBecauseItHoldsOnlyTheViewGrants` tries the denied statements, and `PublicApiViewsTests.EveryObjectOfThePublicApiSchemaIsAViewOfTheOwnerWithOneSelectGrantToTheRoleAndNoOtherGrantee` checks the schema.
- A view column appears only when the file `public-api-columns.txt` lists it. Enforced by the test. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.EveryViewColumnIsInTheCheckedInListBecauseANewColumnNeedsAVisibleEdit`.
- A `public_api` view shows only records of the active release, and it follows a change of the active release at once. Enforced by the view `public_api.active_records`, which every record view joins. The six name views and `zip_districts` do not join it. Each name view joins the active row of `publication_releases` directly. An empty active release has no row in `active_records`. Its name views still show its names. `zip_districts` joins neither. The migration `ReleaseNames` owns the name view joins. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.OnlyRecordsOfTheActiveReleaseAppearAndARollbackMovesTheViewsAtOnce`.
- The view `active_records` shows the publication time of the active release, and the role reads no other column of `publication_releases`. Enforced by the view and by `PublicApiGrants`. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.TheActiveReleaseViewShowsThePublicationTimeOfTheActiveReleaseAndTheRoleReadsNoOtherReleaseColumn`.
- The vote counts equal the member rows, and each Model or Code text carries its public label. The wording equals `PublishedRecordLabels.ModelWording` and `PublishedRecordLabels.CodeWording`, because the SQL repeats the text. Enforced by the view `public_api.votes`. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.VoteCountsEqualTheMemberRowsAndEveryAiOrCodeRowHasItsLabel`.
- The counts of the view follow `VoteTally`. A member list with any other cast gives null counts. A candidate name in the vote for Speaker is such a cast. `VoteTally` gives no totals for it. Enforced by the view `public_api.votes`. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.VoteCountsEqualTheTotalsOfTheVoteTallyAndAreNullWhenTheCastsAreCandidateNamesBecauseOneRuleMustGiveBothAnswers`.
- The columns `question` and `result` of `public_api.votes` equal the columns of the same names in `vote_revisions`. The view does not read the fact rows. Enforced by the view `public_api.votes` and by the owner grants of `PublicApiGrants`, which give no access to `vote_revision_facts`. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.QuestionAndResultComeFromTheRevisionColumnsAndNotFromTheFactRowsBecauseTheColumnsOwnTheTwoValues`. The columns equal `PublishedVote.Result` and the question fact, because one owner writes them; see [votes](votes.md#invariants).
- The view `public_api.vote_members` keys a member by the configured official ID that the release files use, and the role cannot read the bioguide ID. Enforced by the column list of the view and by the owner column grants of `PublicApiGrants`. Tests: `PublicApiViewsTests.AMemberRowCarriesTheOfficialIdAndHidesTheBioguideIdBecausePublicContractsKeyOfficialsByOurId` and `PublicApiViewsTests.TheOwnerHoldsGrantsOnlyOnColumnsThatAViewReadsBecauseASpareColumnGrantWouldWidenWhatAViewCanShow`.
- The view `public_api.vote_issues` shows tracked issues only. Enforced by the view. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.AnUntrackedIssueNeverAppearsInTheIssueViewBecauseNoPublicViewMayShowIt`.
- The bill views show only bills of the active release, and a withdrawn, future, or non-action record never appears. Enforced by the joins to `public_api.active_records` with `kind = 'action'`. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.OnlyBillsOfTheActiveReleaseAppearAndAWithdrawnOrFutureOrNonActionRecordNeverDoes`.
- A revision with no written columns, no derived columns, or no sponsorship appears in no bill view. Enforced by the filter of each bill view. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.ARevisionWithNoWrittenColumnsOrNoDerivedColumnsOrNoSponsorshipAppearsInNoBillViewBecauseItHoldsNothingToShow`.
- Each bill child view shows only records that `bills` shows. A bill with no tracked issue appears in `bills` and `bill_sponsorships` and not in `bill_issues`. Enforced by the same filter in each view. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.NoBillChildViewShowsARecordThatBillsHidesBecauseARouteJoinsThemByRecordId`.
- Each Model or Code bill carries its public label, and a Person bill has none. Enforced by the view `public_api.bills`. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.EveryAiOrCodeBillHasItsLabelAndAPersonBillHasNone`.
- The event date and the cosponsor count equal the stored columns. Enforced by the view, which reads them and has no second rule. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.EventDateAndCosponsorCountEqualTheStoredColumnsBecauseCSharpWritesThemOnceAndTheViewMustNotComputeThem`.
- A sponsorship row shows the official ID, the role, and the original flag, and the role cannot read the bioguide ID. Enforced by the column list of the view and the owner column grants. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.ASponsorshipRowKeepsItsOrderOfficialIdRoleAndOriginalFlagAndHidesTheBioguideId`.
- The view `public_api.bill_issues` shows tracked issues only. Enforced by the view. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.AnUntrackedBillIssueNeverAppearsInTheIssueViewBecauseNoPublicViewMayShowIt`.
- A release holds one set of name rows: its officials with their terms, its issues with their definition, includes, and excludes, and its policy areas. The rows equal `officials.json` and `catalog.json` of the release, and the issue text comes from `PublicationCatalog`. Enforced by `PublishRelease`, which builds the names once from the release data and passes them in the commit. Test: `PublishReleaseNamesTests.TheNamesOfTheCommitAreTheOfficialsAndTheCatalogOfTheReleaseFilesBecauseTheApiMustShowWhatTheReleaseShowed`.
- The commit writes the name rows in the transaction of the release, and no later release changes them. The primary keys start with the release number, and code only inserts. Enforced by `ReleaseNameRows.Add`, which `PostgresPublicationStore.CommitAsync` calls before the one save of the release. Tests: `PostgresReleaseNamesTests.ACommitWritesTheOfficialsTermsIssuesAndPolicyAreasOfItsReleaseWithTheDefinitionInColumns`, `PostgresReleaseNamesTests.ALaterReleaseWritesItsOwnRowsAndLeavesTheRowsOfTheEarlierReleaseAsTheyWereBecauseTheApiMustShowWhatEachReleaseShowed`, and `PostgresReleaseNamesTests.ARefusedCommitLeavesNoNameRowBecauseTheRowsShareTheTransactionOfTheRelease`.
- The six name views show only rows of the active release and follow a rollback at once. Each view joins the active row of `publication_releases`. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.TheNameViewsShowOnlyTheRowsOfTheActiveReleaseAndFollowARollbackBecauseTheApiMustShowWhatTheActiveReleaseShowed`.
- The role reads no suggestion, author, run, candidate, fingerprint, citation, or JSON column of a bill, and no base table of actions. Enforced by the column list of the views and the owner column grants. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.TheRoleReadsNoNeverPublicBillFieldBecauseNoViewShowsOneAndNoBaseTableGrantExists`.
- The role cannot write. It has a 2 second statement limit and a small connection limit. It closes an idle session after 60 seconds. The pool of the API closes its idle connections after 30 seconds, so the role never ends a session that the pool still holds. A deploy leaves it without LOGIN and without a password. Enforced by `PublicApiGrants`. Tests: `PublicApiViewsTests.TheGrantsStepRunTwiceChangesNothingBecauseEveryDbMigrateRunsIt` and `PublicApiViewsTests.TheRoleIsReadOnlyShortLivedAndLimitedAndTheMigrationLeavesItWithoutLoginOrPassword`. The test reads the role state before it sets a login for its own connection. A role that exists keeps its state. So the test shows the state after the migration and the cleanup of earlier tests. It does not show a new create.
- The base grants of the view owner list only the columns that the views read, and no table grant. Enforced by `PublicApiGrants`. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.TheOwnerHoldsGrantsOnlyOnColumnsThatAViewReadsBecauseASpareColumnGrantWouldWidenWhatAViewCanShow`.

## Behavior

### Public reader experience

A record explains what happened, who was involved, and when. Where evidence supports it, it explains why the event matters. It states what the evidence can and cannot establish. Readers move from the account into retained passages and exact citations. They do not need to read collection jobs or extraction metadata first.

A document-change record shows both versions, and observation history where needed to explain gaps. A current source link supplements the retained evidence; it does not replace it. Public records show their review and version context and published corrections.

### Release content

- An executive action record has the official title as its headline. It carries the facts with their citations, and the saved Federal Register record as its one source. It has no summary. Its page says that the title is the title from the Federal Register. It also says that code checked the facts. It links to the Federal Register page. Each fact row shows its value once, with a "Source line" link to the marked line in the Sources panel. The page has no Evidence panel, because that panel only repeated the rows. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.AnExecutiveActionShowsEachFactOnceWithALinkToItsSourceLineAndNoEvidencePanel`. See [executive actions](executive-actions.md).
- Each document-change record carries the full extracted text of both versions, their source URLs, and their observation times. Changed regions are offsets into those texts.
- A citation holds an extraction ID, UTF-16 offsets, and the quoted text. The contract rejects a quote that does not match its offsets.
- Records show the approval date and the first-publication date. The all-records pages show the release date.
- `index.html` is the home page. Its page title is "Civic Lens". A navy hero follows the header. It holds the heading "Who represents you?" as the only h1, the lead "See how your representative and senators voted and which bills they introduced. Each fact links to its official source.", the line "Or browse all N officials" with the count of published officials, and the break card. The hero has two columns at 1280 px and one column at 360 px. The page has no ZIP form yet and no release line. The link goes to the officials list. If a brief exists, the page shows the latest brief next. The brief panel has the small label "Weekly brief" above it. With no brief, the panel and the label are hidden. Tests protect this: `ReleaseRendererTests.HomeLabelsTheBriefPanelAsAWeeklyBriefOnlyWhenABriefExists` and `ReleaseRendererTests.HomeHidesTheBriefingPanelWhenNoBriefExistsInsteadOfShowingAnEmptyPanel`. The section "Start with an issue" (`#issues`) is a grid of tiles, one for each issue with a page. A tile shows the icon of the issue and its name and links to the issue page. `IssueIcons` owns the fixed icon set, and an issue with no entry in `config/issue-icons.json` gets the neutral default. The section "Latest from the record" (`#latest`) has two columns, "Votes" and "Executive actions". The invariant on home items describes them. The home page does not list records, issues, or policy areas in tables. The issue and policy area tables are on `issues.html`. The latest brief is the one with the newest week. It shows the headline and the paragraphs with their citation links, which lead to the brief page. When a brief exists, it also links to `briefs.html` with the text "All weekly briefs". The home page has no search panel.
- `all.html`, then `all-2.html` and so on, list every record 50 to a page, by event day with newest first, with relative previous and next links. They hold no search panel; `search.html` has the search.
- A table can show a fixed number of rows a page in the browser. `DataTable` takes `PagedRows`, the plural noun of its rows, and then sets `data-paged` to `DataTable.PagedRowCount` (15) and `data-rows-noun`. A script in `public.js` shows 15 matching rows, adds Previous and Next buttons, and writes "Showing 1 to 15 of 603 records". A page turn scrolls the table to its top and moves focus to the table. Printing shows every matching row. `DataTable` throws when `PagedRows` has no `Caption`, because the pager takes its name from the caption. Without the script every row stays in the HTML and shows. The filter, the sort, and the paging share one function that owns `row.hidden`, so a filtered or sorted table starts again at the first page. A table with 15 matching rows or fewer shows no pager. The official page pages its records, links, and breaks. The board pages page their lists, and the policy areas page pages its table. The tables of the all-records, brief, and facet pages keep their server pages. Enforced by `DataTable` and `public.js`. Test: `PublicFoundationTests.APagedTableKeepsEveryRowInItsHtmlSoNoRowNeedsJavaScriptToBeReached`. No test for the script, because the repository has no JavaScript test harness. Every table is the shared `DataTable`. Test: `PresentationArchitectureTests.EveryTableIsTheSharedDataTableSoPagingAndTheCardLayoutHaveOneOwner`.
- A table can sort in the browser. `DataTable` and `TableCell` have sort parameters that are off by default, so the review tables have no sort. A header with `Sort` carries `data-sort` (`text`, `number`, or `date`). A cell with `SortValue` carries `data-sort-value`, and `public.js` sorts by that value, never by the displayed text. Without scripts the headers stay plain and the rows keep the server order. With scripts, `public.js` adds a "Sort by" select, buttons in the headers, `aria-sort`, and a status line. The select shows on phones, where the header row is hidden. A third press on a header, or the first option of the select, restores the default order. A row with an empty sort value stays last in both directions. Text sorts by the lower-case text in code order, as the search service does. So "H.R. 10" comes before "H.R. 2". Accented letters sort after z. The caption names the default order: "Newest first", or "Default order" for the issues and offices tables. A header with `SortLabel` gives a short name to the sort menu and the status line. A header with `DefaultSort` marks its first direction as the default order, so the menu names that order once.
- The official page sorts its whole tables in the browser: issues, links, and offices. The board pages sort their whole lists. The paged tables of `all.html` and the issue and policy area pages sort only when the page has the API address. A sort then calls `/v1/search` with the `issue` or `policyArea` filter and the `sort` key. It replaces the rows and hides the static pager. A "More results" button follows the cursor. The status line gives the shown count, the total, and the order. The total comes from the page, and the shown count never exceeds it. Each call asks for the page size, 50. The calls name no release. A release newer than the page can change the total. The reset restores the static rows. `PublishedRecordTable` writes `data-sort-source`. It holds the address, the total, the page size, the filter, the columns, and the kind labels.
- `briefs.html`, then `briefs-2.html` and so on, list the weekly briefs 50 to a page, newest week first. A row shows the headline as a link to the brief page, the Monday of the week, and the first publication day. The list uses only the manifest entries of kind brief and reads no record file. `ReleaseIndexPages` sorts the briefs once with the order of `ReleaseRecordOrder`. It gives the list to the renderer, and the home page uses its first entry as the latest brief. With no brief, the page says "No weekly briefs yet" and links to all records. Every publish writes these pages. Enforced by `ReleaseIndexPages` and `PublishedBriefsPage`. Tests: `PublishReleaseTests.EveryIndexPageIsWrittenBeforeTheManifestSoALongReleaseIsNeverTruncated`, `PublishIndexPagesTests.TheBriefsListKeepsEveryWeekOnLaterPublishesSoAnOlderBriefStaysReachable`, `PublishIndexPagesTests.TheBriefsListOrdersTheNewestWeekFirstEvenWhenAnOlderWeekIsPublishedLater`, `PublishIndexPagesTests.TheBriefsListGetsOnePagePerFiftyBriefsSoThePageCountIsBounded`, `ReleaseRendererTests.BriefsPageListsTheBriefsInTheGivenOrderWithTheWeekAndALinkToEachBriefPage`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.BriefsPagePagesPastFiftyBriefsWithPagerLinksWhereTheyApply`.
- `about.html` states the scope, the sources, and who approves each kind of record. It says that code means software that follows fixed rules, and that no AI decides an approval. It states that code approves action, vote, and presidential document records and the statements that pass `StatementCodeApproval`, and that a person approves all other records. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.AboutNamesStatementsAmongTheRecordsThatCodeApprovesBecauseStatementCodeApprovalDoesApproveThem`. It states the label for model text, what a link is, and that a brief is a sample. It also states how corrections and withdrawals show. It links to `method.html` and `report.html`. It also states that Civic Lens is "not part of any government office, party, campaign or company". It gives the contact email and the report page. It holds a privacy table with one row for each reader action. It states how long a closed report is kept. It holds a reuse table. The table gives the CC BY 4.0 terms for records and data files. It also covers quoted words, the Senate vote data of Voteview with the citation that Voteview asks for, portraits, and the AGPL-3.0 code license. It names Voteview as the source of Senate votes and says that Voteview is not an official source. It names no operator and no funding. `PublishedAboutPage` owns the text. Every publish writes it. The privacy claims rest on code. The report stores nothing about the reader. Only the report page loads the Turnstile script. The search service logs no query. No response sets a cookie. Enforced by `ReleaseIndexPages`, `PublishedAboutPage`, and `PublicApiApp`. Tests: `PublishIndexPagesTests.EveryPublishWritesTheBriefsListAndTheAboutPageSoNeitherLinkInTheNavigationEverBreaks` and `ReleaseRendererTests.AboutPageLinksToTheMethodAndReportPagesAndNamesTheWordingLabelItPromises`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AboutPageNamesVoteviewAsASenateVoteSourceAndCarriesTheCitationThatVoteviewAsksFor`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AboutPageGivesAContactAddressSoAnOfficialOrReaderCanReachTheOwner`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AboutPagePrivacyTableNamesWhatIsKeptForEachActionSoNoReaderIsLeftToGuess`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AboutPageStatesTheClosedReportRetentionAndFailsWhenTheRuleNoLongerSaysOneYear`, `ReleaseRendererTests.AboutPageStatesTheDataAndRecordLicenseWithALinkAndOwnsTheCodeLicenseInTheTable`, `ReportEndpointsTests.AValidReportIsStoredOnceWithTheKindAndTheActiveReleaseAndNothingAboutTheReader`, `ReleaseRendererTests.OnlyTheReportPageLoadsTheTurnstileScript`, `SearchEndpointsTests.ASearchWritesNoQueryTextToTheLogsBecauseThePrivacyTableOfTheAboutPagePromisesNothingIsSaved`, `SearchEndpointsTests.EveryResponseAllowsAnyOriginAndSetsNoCookie`, and `ReportEndpointsTests.AReportAnswerSetsNoCookieBecauseTheAboutPageToldReadersThatNoCookieIsSet`.
- `issues.html` lists each issue and each policy area with its name and record count.
- `board-house-democrat.html`, `board-house-republican.html`, `board-senate-democrat.html`, and `board-senate-republican.html` list the officials of one party in one chamber. Each publish writes all four from the summary rows, so the links never break. A page has three lists. They show the share of split votes against most of the party, the share with most of the party, and the missed votes. Each count shows its total. A page names the officials that it leaves out. The officials page and the method page link to them. A row with no vote figures in this release is on no board. A release with no summary rows has empty boards until `--rewrite-officials` runs. Enforced by `ReleaseOfficialPages`. Test: `PublishReleaseTests.EveryIndexPageIsWrittenBeforeTheManifestSoALongReleaseIsNeverTruncated`.
- With `CIVIC_LENS_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` set, a release also holds `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, and `404.html`; see [public base URL](#public-base-url).
- Each issue has `issues/{id}.html`, then `issues/{id}.page-2.html` and so on. Each policy area has `policy-areas/{id}.html` with the same page suffix. The first page opens with the summary panel; see the invariant on it. A page lists the manifest entries that hold the ID in `issueIds` or `policyAreaIds`, 50 to a page, in the order of `ReleaseRecordOrder`. The search service uses the same rule when a search has no text. Each row shows the kind, the headline, the officials, and the event day, else the first publication day. A facet has pages only when its ID is a valid path segment. See Limits. `PublicationProtocol` owns every page path.
- A page outside the root folder builds each link from a `../` prefix, as a record page does.
- Officials, issues, and policy areas appear by their configured name. An ID with no configured name appears as the ID.
- Only the release manifest shows the schema version.
- `officials.json` lists each official that a published record names. A record names an official when its manifest entry names the official. A record also names an official when a vote of the release holds the official as a member. A vote entry names no official. Publish therefore reads the members of the vote revisions of the release in one database query. The query takes the record IDs and revision numbers of the manifest, so it covers carried votes and new ones. It returns a record ID and a person ID for each member. It sends two array parameters, so the list size does not matter. It reads no vote file for a configured person. A person who left the configuration takes the name of an entry that names the person. A person named only by votes takes the name from one vote record, read from this publish or from the previous release. Each profile holds the ID, the name, the Wikidata ID, the website, and the dated terms. A term holds the office, its `kind`, and the dates. The `kind` is `senator`, `representative`, `executive`, `judicial`, or `other`. It holds the party, the caucus, the state, and the district when the source gives them. The caucus is the party whose caucus the official joins, when it differs from the party. A term with no end is ongoing.
- `PublicationOfficials` joins a person to terms. It matches the bioguide ID to the latest member snapshot first. When no term matches, it matches the Wikidata ID to the latest executive snapshot. A person with no match is listed by name with no terms. A Wikidata ID with no executive official is not an error. The website is the valid roster URL of the member, else the configured website of the person. A former member who is not in the current roster takes the configured website. A member who leaves the roster loses the roster URL, as the terms do. A website must pass `PublishedWebsite`. A roster URL that fails the rule becomes no website and never fails a publish.
- A vote file holds the votes of one official in one Congress. A row holds the record ID, the vote day, and the roll call (Congress, session, number). It also holds the bill type and number when the vote has a bill. It holds the cast, the result, the totals, the party of the official, and the majority cast of that party. It holds the headline, the vote kind, and the tracked issue IDs. Rows are newest first. A publish takes the new rows from the vote records that it already holds. It reads a vote file only for a touched official, and only the files that its pages need. It finds the files of an official by a listing of one folder of the previous release. The members of the replaced revisions of all Congresses come from one database query.
- `officials/{id}.html` is the current page. It shows a header band, key figures, a row of links to its sections, a vote strip, and the sections. Then it shows a panel of links and a table of the offices. The full list of records is on the record page; see the invariants. The lede reads the party, the role, and the seat. For example: "Democratic representative for WA, district 9". The header band holds the portrait beside the name, then the lede, then a line with the tenure and the website link. The tenure reads, for example, "In the House since January 1997." The name uses the same heading font as every other page title. The role and seat use the wording of the officials card. The lede of a former member reads "Former Democratic representative for WA, district 9". The tenure is then closed, with a start and an end. A caucus that differs from the party shows after the seat, such as "caucuses with Democrats". The tenure starts at the first term of the unbroken stretch in one chamber. A term that starts after the release date is not the latest term. An official outside Congress has no tenure sentence. The offices table lists only terms that started by the release date. It has one row for each unbroken stretch of terms in the same office, state, district, and party. A term continues a stretch when it starts no more than 7 days after the last end. A note below the table says so, and it names the source congress-legislators only when every term is a Senate or House term. The list of records is newest first. A count line above the list counts the records by kind and the votes with their Congress. The rows are the entries that name the official and the votes of the current Congress. A profile with a website adds a link, in the tenure line of the header, labeled "Official website". The host of the link follows it in small muted text. Tabs below the lede lead to `officials/{id}/{congress}.html`, one page of votes for each earlier Congress; see the invariants. A past page shows only the name, the tabs, the vote strip, and its votes, so a roster change does not rewrite it. The date of a row is the event date of the entry, else its first publication day, and for a vote the vote day. The roll call reads such as "House roll call 40, Congress 119, session 1, HR 139". A vote row shows the cast, the roll call, and the line "Result of the vote" with the raw result wording. The offices table has no Party column when no term has a party. The record list has no cast pill when no row has a cast. An official outside Congress, such as the President, has no votes, so the list has no cast pill. An official with a vote file also has the tab "Issues they work on"; see [Issue panel](#issue-panel). `officials.html` lists every official as a card; see the invariants. A card shows the count of the records other than votes that name the official. The page has no vote count, because a publish does not read the vote files of an untouched official. An official who left the configuration is always touched. The home page and the search page link to it. `PublicationProtocol` owns the paths.
- The current page also shows the portrait of the official in the header, beside the name; see the invariants. The photo files are `photos/{hash}.jpg` in the root of the release, and `PublicationProtocol` owns the path. A publish writes the file of a new photo from the capture, and links the file of a photo that the previous release holds. The setting of the artifact directory is in [operations](../operations.md#publish-a-static-release).
- `catalog.json` lists the issues and policy areas that the records of the release use, each as an ID and a name. It uses the names of the facet pages, so an ID with no configured name appears as the ID.

A release holds six kinds of record. Each manifest entry has a `kind`: `documentChange`, `statement`, `action`, `vote`, `brief`, or `relationship`, a lowercase camel string. All kinds share the `records/` folder, the record data path, and the record page path.

| Kind | Record ID |
|---|---|
| `documentChange` | The draft ID: 32 lowercase hex characters |
| `statement` | The candidate ID: 64 characters |
| `action` | The action record ID: 64 lowercase hex characters |
| `vote` | The vote record ID: 64 lowercase hex characters |
| `brief` | The brief record ID: 64 lowercase hex characters; the [briefs](briefs.md#decisions) page defines it |
| `relationship` | The pair key: 64 lowercase hex characters; the [statements](statements.md#decisions) page defines it |

A statement record carries one source version with the full extracted text, the source URL, and the observation time. It also carries:

- speakers by configured name, and other speakers
- issues, policy areas, and bill numbers
- the optional statement date with its evidence
- 1 to 32 quotations, each with its kind: `directQuotation` or `releaseText`
- `textOrigin` and `approvedBy`, both required; see the invariants for their rules
- an optional `articleUrl`, the link of the article, when the source is a feed that lists the article. It is null when the source is the article page. A null value keeps old files valid, so the schema version stays 1.

Every quotation must equal the source text at its offsets.

The source URL of a statement is the URL that the collector requested for the cited capture. For a feed item, the source is the feed and `articleUrl` is the item link. The statement page then links the article and says that the source is the feed that lists it. `PublishedStatementBuilder` sets both URLs. `PublishedStatement.Validate` requires a web URL for the link. Tests: `PublishStatementTests.AStatementFromAFeedCitesTheFeedCaptureAndLinksTheArticleBecauseTheReaderNeedsBoth`, `PublishStatementTests.AStatementFromTheArticlePageHasNoSeparateArticleLinkBecauseItsSourceIsTheArticle`, `PublishedStatementTests.RejectsAnArticleLinkThatIsNotAWebUrlBecauseTheReaderFollowsIt`, `PublishedStatementTests.ARecordWrittenBeforeTheArticleLinkExistedStillReadsWithNoLink`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.StatementPageLinksTheArticleAndSaysThatTheSourceIsTheFeedWhenTheStatementCameFromAFeed`.

An action record carries:

- the bill identity (congress, type, and number) and the bill title
- the policy area with its citation, the policy area ID stored at draft time, and the tracked issue IDs. The model chose the tracked issues from the policy area candidates
- the CRS summary citation, when the revision has one
- 1 to 600 sponsorships. Each has the official by configured name, the role (`sponsor` or `cosponsor`), and the original flag. It also has the sponsored date, the optional withdrawn date, and one or two citations.
- 1 to 8 sources, each with the full extracted text of one cited Congress.gov API page, its URL, and its observation time

The sources are the distinct cited extractions in citation order, so the first one is the page of the first sponsorship. Every citation must name one of the sources and quote its text exactly. A source that no citation names is rejected.

A brief record carries:

- the headline and 1 to 12 paragraphs, each with the citation IDs that it uses
- the week start (a Monday), the cutoff release number, and the coverage counts: considered, sent to the model, and cited
- 1 to 256 citations. Each has its ID, kind, record ID, revision number, and a copy of the cited headline

A relationship record carries:

- the headline, which joins the headline of the statement and the headline of the target
- a citation of the statement and a citation of the target. Each has the kind, the record ID, the revision number, and a copy of the headline
- the type, one of `describesOwnBill`, `statesPositionOn`, `claimsCreditFor`, or `statementAboutVote`, and the reason in one sentence
- the quote of the statement and the text of the fact of the target. Both are text only. The builder takes the quote from the article text. Its span must lie on the extraction of the statement and inside one passage of the statement
- the optional statement date, the speakers by configured name, and the issue and policy area IDs of both sides

The approval time of a record is the latest approval of its current revision, for every kind. One Application rule owns that time.

### Publish

One publish request names up to 64 records to publish or withdraw, each as a kind and an ID. It can name none; see the empty publish invariant. For each kind, publication loads all selected drafts and their verified evidence in one repeatable-read transaction. Each read uses a fixed number of queries for any selection of 1 to 64 drafts. The publisher builds records from this evidence and does not read it again. Before it builds, it reads the stored first publication time of the named records in one query. A record with no row, which the active release does not hold, is new and takes the clock time.

1. Application builds the release contract. A page step then renders every page that the publish writes, and Host renders the static HTML. See [related records](#related-records).
2. The owner action builds the release in a staging directory, verifies it, and moves it to a uniquely named directory under `releases/`.
3. Under all seven review locks, the commit checks each added record's revision and review-state version again, in the draft table of its kind. For each added brief or relationship it also checks that every citation is current in the records of the new release. For each withdrawn record it checks that review blocked the current revision. It also checks the next release number and the active release it built on. It then requeues the brief of the week of each record that the release changes, drops, or adds again; see [briefs](briefs.md#staleness).
4. In the same transaction, it records a sequentially numbered release and marks it as active. It stamps the release time from the database clock after it holds all review locks. The commit does not take a time from the caller. It also inserts the name rows of the release. It also inserts the first publication time of each added record into `published_records`, and it keeps any row that exists. It then checks that each stored time equals the time in the release. If one differs, the commit throws `PublicationConflictException`. The worker builds again at its next tick. A CLI publish exits with the error. The commit then inserts the entry rows of the added records, and it keeps any row that exists. If a kept row differs from the release entry, the commit throws the same exception. It writes the rows of the withdrawal tables.
5. After the commit, it takes all seven review locks again, reads the active release, and atomically replaces the relative `current` symbolic link.

Publish, replay, and activate all end with step 5. An older command therefore cannot move the link back after a newer one.

The review state is checked at commit, so a concurrent blocking decision cannot be ignored. A commit that fails on a conflict or invalid input deletes its directory. If that cleanup fails, or an unknown failure occurs, the directory stays. Only committed releases can be activated.

Each publication builds on the recorded active release, not on the link. A replayed publish request and the activate command both point the link at the recorded active release. This repairs a link that is out of date. Publish requests reuse the review replay receipts.

### Automatic publication

The worker runs the stage `publishing` when `CIVIC_LENS_PUBLISHING=1`; see [operations](../operations.md#automatic-publication). It acts as the code subject `civic-lens:code` with the owner role. The stage follows the weekly brief stage. It has its own wakeup on the database clock, and the worker runs it beside the other stages.

Each wake runs one pass. The pass first checks the gap; see the invariants. It then checks the release state, and it stops when the active release is not the latest release. It lists the ready records of all seven kinds with the loader ready reads, up to 64 of each kind. It keeps 64 in all, with an equal share for each kind. The pass publishes them with `PublishRelease`. A newer approved revision of a published record is a normal addition, and `RequirePublishable` accepts it. The pass makes one release. Records left over wait for the next wake.

When no record is ready, the tick checks `PublishRelease.HasBillStatusChangeAsync`. It reads the previous status file and one status query, and it stages nothing. When a status changed, the tick publishes one status-only release with the code subject. The key is `status-after-` and the number of the release that it builds on. A [bill status](#bill-status-line) never needs a record.

An approval wakes the stage. A trigger on each of the seven `*_drafts` tables sends `pg_notify` on the channel `civic_lens_publishing`. It fires when a draft is inserted as `Approved`. It fires when `review_status` becomes `Approved`. It also fires when an `Approved` draft gets a new revision number. The payload is a deadline in ticks: the database clock plus 60 seconds. The migration `PublishOnApprovalNotify` holds the 60 seconds as a named constant. The trigger does not read the releases.

The wakeup keeps the earliest deadline it has heard, so a burst of approvals gives one wake and the deadline never slides. The wake clears the deadline. A later approval starts a new window. The wakeup `PostgresPublishingWakeup` adds two more wake times to the approval deadline. They are the next full hour and the end of the gap after the newest release. The newest release is the row of `publication_releases` with the highest `published_at_utc_ticks`. The wakeup is the `PostgresDeadlineWakeup` that the analysis queues use, with an extra deadline query like the brief wakeup.

The gap is 10 minutes. The database clock measures it, against `published_at_utc_ticks`. The commit writes that time from the database clock, so the gap starts at the commit and a long build does not shorten it. A wake inside the gap makes no release, and the wakeup then waits for the end of the gap. At that wake the pass publishes the next 64 ready records. A backlog therefore drains at 64 records for each 10 minutes plus the build time, which is at most 9216 records a day. The 30 kept releases then cover at least 5 hours. The limit of 64 is the size of one publish request. It stays, because the memory and commit time of a larger release are not measured. The worker start wake follows the same gap. The hourly wake is a fallback for a lost notification. It is also the only wake that can make a status-only release.

The command `releases activate` of the latest release sends no wake. A stage that waits after a rollback resumes at its next wake: an approval, the end of the gap, or the next full hour.

A tick that fails or ends on a conflict leaves its records ready. It starts a gap of 10 minutes on the clock of the process. Wakes in that gap do nothing and schedule nothing. The first wake after the gap tries again. That wake is an approval or the next full hour. A restart of the worker clears this gap. The release gap from the stored releases still holds.

The tick stops early in two cases. A `PublicationConflictException` ends it, and the next tick builds again. A replay ends it too. A replay happens after a rollback followed by a CLI publish: the rolled-back records list again, and their key finds the stored release.

A person approval that replaces a published record needs the correction note of its approval. Without it, `PublicationCorrections` raises `MissingCorrectionNoteException` in the build. A record with 20 corrections with a note raises `CorrectionLimitException` for a new note. Both exceptions are tagged like a validation failure, but the tick logs one line at information level and leaves the record out. The other records publish. The record waits. To get out of the missing note state, withdraw the approval, then press Save and approve with no edit and with a note. That approves the same revision again. Under the cap this publishes the record. At the cap, the blocked list shows the record.

A record can fail validation while `PublishedRecordLoader.LoadAsync` builds it, for example when an official has no configured public name. The loader tags the exception with the record. The tick leaves that record out, reports it, and builds the batch again with the others. The next tick tries the record again.

### Rollback

Rollback first checks that the manifest of the earlier release exists. A missing manifest refuses the command with exit code 2 and changes nothing. Rollback then records the release as active and points the link at it. It also requeues the brief of the week of each record that differs between the two releases. The next publication builds on the rolled-back release, so it does not restore the records that were rolled back. A publication built before a rollback fails on commit.

The activate command reads the manifest of the replaced release and the manifest of the release that the link serves after the switch. It reports three lists next to the release summary. `restoredWithdrawals` holds the records that the replaced release withdrew and the activated release holds as records. `withdrawnAgain` holds the records that the replaced release held and the activated release withdraws. `blocked` holds the records of the activated release that review now blocks. The reads are advice and take no lock. If a read fails, the command still returns the summary and logs a warning, because the switch is done. A failure of the served manifest empties all three lists. A failure of the replaced manifest empties the first two lists. A failure of the review read empties `blocked`.

### Release retention

After a publish serves the active release, `PruneReleases` removes old release folders. It is best effort. A failure or a shutdown is logged as a warning and never fails the publish. The next publish tries again.

Step 1 runs under the lock. `PostgresPublicationStore.PruneAsync` takes the seven review locks in the order of `ReviewKeysInOrder`, as `ServeActiveAsync` does. It reads the database clock. It reads the 30 highest release rows. The query groups the rows by UTC day since the start of the day 6 days back. It returns the highest number of each day, so at most 7 rows load. It also finds the folder of the active row. `ReleaseRetention.Plan` turns the rows and the time into a `ReleaseRetentionPlan`. The plan keeps the 30 highest numbers and the highest number of each UTC day in the last 7 days. The callback reads the folder that the `current` link serves. `FileReleaseDirectory.RetireAsync` then lists the folders in `releases/` from the disk. It moves each folder that the plan retires into `deleting/`, except the active folder and the served folder. The plan retires a number only when it is at or below the highest committed number and not kept. Each move is one rename, so a half-deleted folder never holds a `release.json` under a release name. A folder with no database row is retired too, when its number is at or below the highest committed number. A folder above that number may belong to a release in progress, so it stays. The step runs without the shutdown token, because a rename is fast. A release in progress with a number below the highest committed number can no longer commit, so retiring its folder is safe.

Step 2 runs after the lock ends. `DeleteRetiredAsync` deletes every folder in `deleting/`, including folders that a crash left. It may stop on shutdown, and the next prune finishes the work. A discard of a failed publish deletes its own folder the same way. Hard links make each newer release keep its own copy of every carried file.

The database rows stay. After a prune, `releases activate` for a pruned release is refused with "has no files on this machine". The release list still shows it.

### Blocked published records

`ListBlockedPublished` serves the owner list of published records that review blocks. It reads the active manifest and gives the entries to `BlockedPublishedRecords`. That class reads the current review state of each entry with `IPublicationStore.ReadReviewStatusesAsync`. It lists the entries that `PublishedRecordRules.IsWithdrawable` accepts with the reason `ReviewBlocksIt`. A published record whose newer revision a person rejected is one of them. The rejection does not remove the live text, so the owner takes the record off with Take off site. The store method uses the same read as the commit check for a withdrawal, so the list and the commit agree. The store also names the actor of the latest approval, but only for an approved revision that the release does not hold. It reads one decision row for each such record. For each of those records that a person approved, the class reads the correction list from the record file of the active release. It lists the record with the reason `CorrectionLimit` when the list holds 20 notes. It also checks each withdrawn notice of the manifest, with the withdrawn revision as the published revision. It lists the notice with the reason `WithdrawnAtCorrectionLimit` when a person approved a higher revision and the notice holds 20 notes. The notice already holds its correction list, so this check reads no file. The notice holds no headline, so the row reads "Withdrawn record" and the record ID. `BlockedPublishedRecord.StuckText` gives the text of each reason. Each row holds the kind, the record ID, the headline, the published revision, the review status, and the reason. The list is empty when no release is active.

The CLI command `releases blocked` shows the list. The CLI JSON holds the reason of each row. The Review home page shows only the stuck rows, in the Needs a person queue. A stuck row has the badge "Cannot publish" and the reason text. A withdrawal does not free it. A row with the reason `ReviewBlocksIt` is in the queue by its review status, except a rejected record. A rejected record leaves the queue. A published record whose newer revision is rejected shows as a "Cannot publish" row. Its text says the live text stays until the owner takes it off. The owner withdraws it with Take off site on the record editor, or with the CLI command `releases publish <key> --withdraw <kind>:<id> "note"`; see [review](review.md#take-off-site). The home page reads one record file for each person-approved candidate on each owner page load. The home page reads the active manifest a second time for the stuck rows. The stuck rows are for the owner only. The Review home page also lists recent releases, read-only. It has no publish control, because the stage `publishing` publishes every approval.

### Corrections and withdrawal

A correction replaces a record. A withdrawal removes it. Withdrawing approval in review does not change published artifacts. Only a publish with a withdrawal does. The CLI option `--withdraw` and Take off site on the record editor both run that publish.

Each of the seven record files holds a `corrections` list of `PublishedCorrection`. An entry holds the replaced revision number, the publication time, an optional note, and an optional `factsUnchanged` flag. A note means a person correction. No note means that code updated the record from the source. The flag is absent unless true, so older files read as before. It marks a vote update where only the headline or the summary changed. The page then reads "Summary rewritten by the AI model". It ends with "The vote facts did not change." Code compares the two vote files. It never trusts a stored reason. The list runs newest first. It holds at most 20 entries with a note. A code update replaces the code entry of its kind in the newest run, so a run holds at most two entries. A record that no release replaced has an empty list.

`PublicationCorrections` owns the list and the note rule. A publish that replaces a record of the active release reads the earlier list from the record file of that release. It puts the new entry first. A person must give a note for each replacement. The editor writes the note at approve time, and `ReviewDecision.CorrectionNote` keeps it in the approval decision. `PublishedRecordRules.CorrectionNoteOf` reads the note of the latest approval of the published revision. The code subject gives no note. A note on a first publication is dropped. The note rules are in `CorrectionNoteText`, which the approval and the withdrawal note share.

The record page shows each entry above the other panels. The Timeline panel adds an event for each entry. A page that a later publish renders again keeps the entries, because the page reads them from the record file.

A withdrawal needs the owner and a note. `PublishReleaseRequest.Withdrawals` carries the notes, and the idempotency hash covers them. A request can have withdrawals and no records. The record must be in the active release. At commit, review must have blocked the record.

`PublicationWithdrawals` builds one `PublishedWithdrawal` for each withdrawn record. It holds the kind, the record ID, the withdrawn revision, both times, the note, and the correction list of the record. The manifest keeps the entry in `withdrawals`. The release holds no `records/{id}.json` for the record. The page `records/{id}.html` is a notice. Each publish renders every notice again, so the notices always have the current page head.

`WithdrawalCascade` runs after the publish loads its additions. It refuses the withdrawal while a relationship or a brief that stays in the release cites a withdrawn record. It finds relationships with `IRelationshipReviewStore.FindCitingAsync` over the carried relationship IDs. It reads each carried brief file for its citations. The error lists the citing records. A record that the same request adds meets the same rule earlier. `PublicationBuild.WithRemoved` takes the withdrawn records out of the release records that the loaders check. Take off site calls the read-only `WithdrawalCascade.FindCitersAsync` before it writes a decision, so a predictable refusal writes nothing. The commit-time check stays.

A withdrawn relationship leaves the related panels of the statement and of the action or vote that it cited. The publish writes those pages again. A withdrawn or returned action changes the bill link of the carried votes on its bill. `PublicationBuild.VotesOnBillsOf` finds those votes in the bill action map of the publish, and the publish writes their pages again. An action that gets another bill line changes those pages in the same way. A withdrawn vote leaves the vote files of its members. It reads only the Congress of the vote file, and the members come from the database as for a replaced vote. A Congress with no row left disappears. A withdrawn record counts as a change for each official it named, so the publish writes their pages again. A withdrawal requeues the brief of the week of the record; see [briefs](briefs.md#staleness).

The worker returns a withdrawn record once a person has approved a higher revision with a correction note. The owner can also return it with `releases publish <key> <kind>:<id>`. Every returned record has a person approval and a note. `PublicationCorrections` continues the corrections from the withdrawal and adds one entry for the withdrawn revision. The publish drops the returned ID from `withdrawals`, so the manifest lists the record again. The addition page replaces the notice page, because the notice loop reads only the remaining withdrawals. A code approval keeps the record withdrawn on every path. A revision that is not higher keeps it withdrawn too. `WithdrawnRecordException` tags the record, so the stage `publishing` logs one line and leaves the record out. A person approval without a note raises `MissingCorrectionNoteException`, and the stage skips it the same way.

A relationship or a brief that cited the record stays withdrawn. A person publishes it again, in the same request or later. The pages of officials, the vote files, the search rows, and the brief requeue follow the added record, as for any other addition.

### Vote record

`PublishedVote` is the contract of a vote record. `PublishedVoteBuilder` builds it from an approved `VoteDraftRevision`, the review decisions, and the cited extractions. It maps each member official through `PublishedRecordRules.MapOfficial` and copies each citation through `PublishedRecordRules.Cite`. The vote kind enum mirrors the Core `VoteKind`. The source enum `PublishedVoteSource` mirrors the Core `VoteSource` the same way: `CongressGov` or `Voteview`. `PublishedVoteSources` maps it, and `PublishedVote.source` is required. `PublishVoteTests` tests the builder through `PublishRelease`, as for the other kinds.

The builder reads the result from the saved result line. `VoteTally` reads the totals from the saved total lines. It reads the member lines of the first cited member extraction, which holds every member of the roll call. It groups the casts by the party that `VoteParties` gives for the vote day. `PublishRelease` reads the latest member snapshot once for each publish. `PublicationBuild` carries it to `VoteRecordLoader`, and `ReleaseDataFiles` passes it to `PublicationOfficials.ResolveAsync`. The source party letter of a member line serves the House cross-check and the party of a member who left the roster. The Senate has no party total lines, so its cross-check uses the count line. The official pages and the search service list votes. `VoteRecordLoader` owns the vote kind for publish and the ready list. `PostgresVoteReviewStore.GetForPublicationAsync` reads the selected drafts, their review cycles, and the cited extractions. It also reads the IDs of the action records with the same congress, bill type, and bill number. Several bill sources can draft one bill, so there can be more than one ID. The loader passes the first ID whose action record is in the release to `RenderVoteAsync`. The release holds an action record when the base release has it or the same publish adds it. Loaders run in list order and action comes before vote, so a same-release action counts. The page links to it. Otherwise the page shows the bill as text.

### Relationship record

`PublishedRelationship` is the contract of a relationship record. `PublishedRelationshipBuilder` builds it from an approved `RelationshipRevision`, the cited statement revision with its article text, and the cited target. `RelationshipRecordLoader` owns the kind for publish and the ready list, as `BriefRecordLoader` does for briefs. `PostgresRelationshipReviewStore.GetForPublicationAsync` reads the selected drafts and their cited revisions in one repeatable-read transaction. It flags each relationship whose cited revisions are not current in the release that the loader passes. The ready-list read uses the active release.

The review flag "cited records changed" keeps the approved-revision rule of [statements](statements.md#relationship-review). Publication uses the release rule above, as briefs do. The two flags can differ, because a cited record can be approved at a newer revision that the release does not hold yet.

A relationship page names the cited revision number of each side. It links to the current page of each cited record, which can show a newer revision. The page shows the reason, the quote of the statement, and the fact of the target. Its caveat says that the link states no verdict.

### Related records

A statement, action, or vote page lists the relationships of the new release that cite its record. The page step builds the list at render time. `ReleaseLinks` holds the relationships of the new release: the ones that the publish adds, and the carried ones that it reads from the previous release. One database read names the carried relationships that cite a record this publish renders. `PostgresRelationshipReviewStore.FindCitingAsync` does it from the pair rows. The publish then reads only those files. It reads none when it adds no statement, action, vote, or relationship and renders no carried page. `PublishedRecordFiles.MaximumCitedRecordBytes` caps the read of a cited record. It derives from the source limits, so it holds the largest record, which is an executive action with its nine sources. `ReleaseLinks.Citing` orders a list by the statement date, with an undated statement last, and then by record ID.

`ReleasePages` renders every page that the publish writes. A loader gives each added record a page renderer that takes its related list. A record that the publish adds or replaces therefore lists its links, including the carried ones. A statement, action, or vote that an added link cites, and that the release carries, gets its page written again. `PublishedRecordLoader.RenderCarriedAsync` renders it from the carried record file with the related list. Every record kind has this method, so each kind with a page can be rendered again. `PublishRelease` finds the action records of all votes of the release with one `FindBillActionRecordIdsOfVotesAsync` query. The result serves the carried vote pages, the votes on the bill of a changed action, and the bill lines of the list pages. The query reads the bill of the published revision that the release names, and not the bill of the current candidate. `ReleasePages` returns a `StagedPage` for each page and not the page text. `PublishRelease` calls it when it writes the page, so one page text is in memory at a time. `ReleaseLinks.all` still holds the carried relationships. `PublishRelease` links the record file of such a record and writes the new page.

A record page that no new link cites, and that the publish does not add or replace, stays a hard link. A later publish that replaces a cited record lists its carried links.

### Bill status line

An action page shows where the bill stands on Congress.gov. The status is not part of the record. A [bill gate](bills.md) stores the `latestAction` of each bill version that Civic Lens fetched. Each publish reads the status of every action record of the release in one query, `IActionReviewStore.ReadBillStatusesAsync`. The query joins the record to its bill and takes the newest version. It takes the read time from the creation time of the gate row. `ReleaseBillStatuses` writes the rows to `bill-status.json`, sorted by record ID.

The renderer receives the row of each action record. A carried action page is written again in three cases. Its text or date differs from the row of the previous release. The previous release has no row. Or the row is gone. The record file stays the same hard link. The store also returns the bill type of each action, as `ActionBillStatus`, because the meaning of "Received in the Senate" depends on it. The published row holds no bill type. The status refreshes in the database on the normal bill update path. It reaches the site in the next publish that rewrites the page. The worker publishes a status-only release when no record is ready; see [automatic publication](#automatic-publication).

The page shows three facts: "Latest action on Congress.gov", "Status read on", and "Official bill page". The first fact holds the plain meaning from `BillStatusMeaning` and the action date. The rules are in the table below. The read time is the time of the first read of that text and date. A later read of the same action keeps it, so the page and `bill-status.json` show one time. The `latestAction` of the bill detail has no action code, so the meaning comes from the text.

| Text has this whole shape | Meaning |
|---|---|
| "Referred to the Subcommittee on X" | "In committee: sent to the Subcommittee on X." |
| "Referred to the Committee on X", "Referred to the House Committee on X", "Referred to the Senate Committee on X", or "Read twice and referred to the Committee on X" | "In committee: sent to the Committee on X." The chamber shows only when the text states it. |
| "Received in the Senate", alone, or followed by " and [Read twice and ]referred to the ...", or by ". Read twice. Placed on ...", for a House bill type | "Passed the House. Now in the Senate." |
| "Received in the House", with the same shapes, for a Senate bill type | "Passed the Senate. Now in the House." |
| "Placed on Senate Legislative Calendar" | "Waiting for a possible Senate floor vote." |
| "Placed on the Union Calendar" or "Placed on the House Calendar" | "Waiting for a possible House floor vote." |
| "Ordered to be Reported", with only an optional "in the Nature of a Substitute", an optional "(Amended)", and an optional "by Voice Vote", "by Unanimous Consent", or "by the Yeas and Nays: N - N" | "A committee voted to send this bill to the full chamber." It reads "this measure" for a resolution type: hres, sres, hconres, sconres, hjres, and sjres. |
| "Became Public Law No: 119-21." | "Became law: Public Law 119-21." |
| Any other text, such as "Motion to reconsider laid on the table." | "Latest official action: " and the raw text |

The rules run in the order of the table. A rule matches the whole text from its start. Any other ending, such as "Unfavorably", "Adversely", or "read the third time, and passed", shows the raw text.

The committee name stops at the first ". " or " (". A committee rule accepts only these tails. One is the final period. One is a citation in the form ". (text: ...)", ". (consideration: ...)", or ". (Sponsor introductory remarks on measure: ...)". The last is the House form with a second committee, and its fixed Speaker clause, shown below.

```text
Referred to the Committee on X, and in addition to the Committee on Y, for a period to be subsequently determined by the Speaker, in each case for consideration of such provisions as fall within the jurisdiction of the committee concerned.
```

That form reads "In committee: sent to the Committee on X, and in addition to the Committee on Y." The citation never shows. A name with " (", such as "Intelligence (Permanent Select)", shows the raw text. A raw text with no final period gets one, so the action date stays its own sentence. In 16,808 dev bill details, the rules cover 91.0% (15,289). A change of a rule changes a page only when the page is written again.

### Rendering

The release builder renders public components to static HTML through `PublicPage`. The pages are the home, all-records, weekly briefs, about, search, facet list, and facet pages, and one record page for each kind. `PublishedHomePage` shows the brief through `PublishedBriefText`. The brief page uses the same component. `PublishedAllRecordsPage`, `PublishedBriefsPage`, `PublishedFacetPage` list records through `PublishedRecordTable`. The `Columns` parameter of the table picks the columns. The briefs page uses `RecordTableColumns.Week`, so each row shows the week and no kind icon or kind line. Facet pages use `RecordTableColumns.Facet`. `PublishedSearchPage` renders `search.html`. `PublishedReportPage` renders `report.html`. `PublishedRecordFrame` links every record page to it. `PublishedFacetsPage` lists facets through `PublishedFacetBrowser`. `PublishedHomePage` puts the break card (`PublishedBreakCard`) in the hero through the `Hero` slot of `PublicPage`. It then shows the issue tiles (`PublishedHomeIssues`) and the latest lists (`PublishedHomeLatest`). Every record page shows a "Timeline" panel from `PublishedTimeline`. `PublishedTimelineView` builds its events from the record file only. The related panel keeps the dated links, so the timeline does not list them. The record pages are `PublishedRecordPage`, `PublishedStatementPage`, `PublishedActionPage`, `PublishedVotePage`, `PublishedBriefPage`, and `PublishedRelationshipPage`.

Public pages have no breadcrumb. A past Congress page reaches the official page through its Congress tabs.

A statement, action, or vote page shows the "Related records" panel of `PublishedRelatedPanel` when its list is not empty. Each row links to the other record and to the relationship page, with the link type and the statement date.

An action page shows the headline, the summary, the bill, the title, the policy area, and the tracked issues. It has a sponsorship table with each official's configured name, role, original flag, dates, and a "Source line" link for each cited line. The policy area fact has the same link. The "Bill evidence" panel shows the CRS summary quote. The Sources panel opens with a note that says the marked lines are exact saved text. Each source shows its URL and observation time as plain text, with no link, and its full text with the quotes marked. Its caveat says that a sponsorship is a proposal, not a law, and that the record lists tracked officials only.

A vote page shows the headline, the summary, the vote day, the kind, the result, the totals, the roll call, and the tracked issues. It also names the data source and links the official roll call page. The totals read, for example, "Casts of all House members: Yea 218, Nay 210, Present 1, Not voting 5". The Senate text says "Casts of all senators". A vote with candidate totals shows one row for each candidate, such as "Votes for Emmer, all House members" with the value "1". The totals for Present and Not Voting read "Present, all House members" and "Not voting, all House members". It has no Yea and Nay line. The page shows no party color. A vote with a badge shows it in a "How the parties voted" panel, first on the page. A split vote also shows the panel "Members who voted against most of their party" before the panel of all members. See the invariant on vote badges. A "Bill" panel shows the bill identity as plain text. It shows a link to `records/{id}.html` instead when the caller passes the ID of an action record in the same release. Each fact row of the vote shows its value once, with a "Source line" link to the marked line in the Sources panel. The rows with a link are the vote day, the question, the result, and each other result fact. The kind row has no link, because the question row cites the same line and keeps words such as "as Amended". An Other vote reads "Other question" in the kind row, and the question row shows its text. The start time shows only in the Sources panel. The Voteview "Count" line is not a row of its own. The Totals row holds its link, because the totals come from that line. The chamber and the roll call have no link, because they come from the record and not from a line. There is no Result panel. No quote box repeats a row. A party total row shows the label "Democratic party total", "Republican party total", or "Independent members' total". `PublishedRecordLabels.VoteResultLabel` owns this label and the candidate label, and `PartyLetters` owns the letter to party name rule. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.TheVoteFactsNameEachPartyTotalInWordsAndKeepTheSavedValueExact`, `ReleaseRendererTests.EachVoteFactRowLinksToItsOwnLineInTheSourceAndRowsThatAreNotInTheSourceHaveNoLink`, and `ReleaseRendererTests.TheCountLineOfAVoteIsCitedOnTheTotalsRowAndNotShownAsASecondRow`. The panel "How each tracked member voted" groups the tracked members by cast. Two examples are "Voted Yea (208 tracked House members)" and "Did not vote (27 tracked House members)". A Senate vote says "tracked senators". One member reads "tracked House member" or "tracked senator". `PublishedVoteView.MemberNoun` names the chamber. The totals line uses it too. The groups follow this order: the Yea side, the Nay side, candidates, Present, and Not Voting. In a Speaker election the casts are candidate names. Those groups read "Voted for Jeffries (215 tracked House members)" and go by count, largest first, then by name. Names in a group go by name, then by ID. `PublishedVoteView.CastGroups` owns the order, and `ReleaseRendererTests.TheVotePageGroupsMembersByCastWithTheCountSoAReaderSeesWhoVotedEachWay` tests it. Each name links to the official page. A second link, "Source line", goes to the marked member line in the source text. Its hidden target reads "{name}, member line". The panel shows no quote, because the Sources panel shows every quote. `ReleaseRendererTests.AHouseVotePageWithEveryMemberStaysWithinItsSizeBudget` keeps a split vote page with 435 members and 216 breaks under 150 KB. The page shows the badge panel and the break panel. Each source shows as on an action page. The caveat says that the record lists tracked officials only. It also says that each cast is the wording of the roll call of the House Clerk or of the Senate, by chamber. For a Voteview vote it says that Voteview stores each vote as a number code. It says that Civic Lens shows the code as a word. It says that the Senate roll call may use other words. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.TheVoteCaveatNamesTheSourceOfTheChamberThatHeldTheVote`, `TheVoteTotalsNameTheWholeBodyThatTheyCount`, and `ASpeakerElectionShowsItsCandidateTotalsAndNoYeaAndNayLine`.

A brief page shows the headline and the paragraphs, each with links to its citations. A "Cited records" panel lists each citation. Each row shows the ID, the kind, and the revision number. A link to `records/{id}.html` shows the copied headline. The facts name the week, the cutoff release, and the coverage counts. The caveat is the sample note. The brief cites some of the new records that Civic Lens considered for the week. It is not a full account.

The header of every page is navy. It holds the logo, links to Officials, Issues, and Votes and bills, and the theme control. Only the home page adds a search box and a navy hero under the header. The link of the section of the page has `aria-current="page"` and a marigold underline. Record pages and withdrawal notices belong to Votes and bills, except brief pages and the briefs list, which belong to no section. Official pages belong to Officials. The issues page, the issue pages, and the policy area pages belong to Issues. The about page belongs to About, and the glossary page belongs to Glossary. The home page, the method page, the report page, the briefs pages, and the search page belong to no section. On a narrow screen the header has up to three rows. They hold the logo and theme control, the three links, and on the home page the search box. The footer shows the name Civic Lens and links to the method page, the glossary page, the about page, and the report page. The report page without a record link shows a notice and a link to all records.

Every page head comes from `PublicPage` and `PublicHead`. The title has the form "page name | Civic Lens". The home page title is "Civic Lens". A paged page adds "page N of M" to its name. A record page uses its headline, cut at a word to 70 characters. The description is plain text of at most 160 characters, cut at a word. It comes from data the page already has. That is the record summary or headline, the official's name and latest office, the issue or policy area name, or a fixed sentence. The head also has `og:title`, `og:description`, `og:type` (`article` for a record page, else `website`), and `og:site_name`. A page with a public base URL also has `<link rel="canonical">` and `og:url` with the same address, after `og:site_name`. Without the base URL it has neither. See [public base URL](#public-base-url). Every public page preloads Schibsted Grotesk. A page that shows record text also preloads Newsreader. These are the record pages of every kind, the brief page, the withdrawal notice, and the home page when it shows a brief. Each preload is `rel=preload as=font type=font/woff2 crossorigin`, with the page root prefix, before the stylesheets. `PublicAssets` owns the font paths, and `PublicPage` has one parameter, `UsesRecordFont`, for Newsreader. Rule: a page with Newsreader text above the fold preloads Newsreader, and other pages do not. Enforced by `PublicPage`, with `PublishedRecordFrame`, `PublishedWithdrawalPage`, and `PublishedHomePage` setting the parameter. Tests: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryPageTypePreloadsSchibstedGroteskAndOnlyPagesWithNewsreaderTextAboveTheFoldPreloadNewsreader` and `ReleaseRendererTests.TheHomePagePreloadsNewsreaderWhenItShowsABriefBecauseTheBriefTextIsSetInNewsreader`. Each preload href must name a release asset. Both tests check this.

A release copies `assets/css/theme.css`, `assets/css/app.css`, `assets/css/public.css`, `assets/js/theme.js`, `assets/js/app.js`, `assets/js/public.js`, `assets/favicon.svg`, the fonts `schibsted-grotesk.woff2` and `newsreader.woff2`, and the two OFL license files of these fonts. The release never holds `review.css` or `review.js`. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.ReviewStylesAndScriptsStayOutOfTheRelease`. A page never uses a class that only `review.css` defines. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.APublicPageNeverUsesAClassOnlyTheReviewStylesheetDefinesBecauseTheReleaseDoesNotShipIt`. Every page links `theme.css`, `app.css`, `public.css`, `theme.js`, `app.js`, and `public.js`, with `app.js` before `public.js`. Order test: `PresentationArchitectureTests.EachShellLoadsOnlyItsOwnStylesheetAndScriptAfterTheSharedOnes`. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryStylesheetAndScriptAPageLinksIsAReleaseAssetWhetherThePageIsAtTheRootOrInAFolder`. `PublicAssets` and the Host project file name the same list. Pages also work without JavaScript. On a screen 760 pixels wide or less, each row of a public table becomes a block. The main cell comes first. Each other cell shows its column name before its value. The name comes from `PublicColumn`, which also writes the column header, and the `data-label` attribute of the cell carries it. The header of the main column stays literal text outside `PublicColumn`, and the main cell has no label. An empty cell stays in the accessibility tree on a phone. It takes no space and shows no column name. So the next cell keeps its own column header. The CSS does not use `display: none` for it. No test for this CSS rule. The column names are CSS generated content with empty alt text, so a screen reader does not read them. The headers stay in the page, hidden from sight. WebKit drops table meaning when CSS changes the display of table parts. On the phone each row is a card with a border and a radius. So every public table sets `Roles` on `DataTable`. Then the table has `role="table"`, its groups have `rowgroup`, and its rows have `row`. `TableCell` gives each head cell `scope="col"` and `columnheader`. It gives each body cell `cell`, except the main cell of the row, which has `rowheader`. Each body row has exactly one main cell. Page markup sets `Main` once for each row, and the roles test below protects this. The main cell is the cell that names the row, for example the record, the official, or the event. `TableCell` also owns the `cell-main` class and the `data-label` attribute. The review UI does not set `Roles` and its markup has no roles. Test: `SourcesPageTests.ReviewTablesCarryNoTableRolesBecauseOnlyPublicTablesOptIn`. Limit: `.cell-main` moves a cell first only on screen. The reading order of a screen reader stays the order of the markup. Checked: the Blink accessibility tree at 375 pixels width, on pages from the renderer test fixtures, not from a release. Not checked: WebKit and VoiceOver, because this machine cannot run them. Roles invariant: enforced by `DataTable` and `TableCell`, protected by `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryPublicTableKeepsItsTableRolesSoWebKitReadersKeepRowsAndHeadersWhenCssStacksTheCells`. Label test: `ReleaseRendererTests.EveryCellOfAPublicTableCarriesItsColumnHeaderAsLabelSoAPhoneReaderSeesWhatEachValueIs`. Shared components are described in [host](host.md#presentation).

No sideways scroll at 360 pixels width: `.public-main` sets `overflow-wrap: break-word`, and all public content inherits it. `.panel-padded` has one grid track, `minmax(0, 1fr)`. Together they make a long unbroken string, such as a source URL, break inside its panel. The track rule is needed because `break-word` does not lower the minimum width of a grid track. `overflow-wrap: anywhere` would, but it also breaks words inside narrow table cells at 761 to 1100 pixels. No test for these CSS rules. Checked: Blink, not WebKit, on the pages from the renderer test fixtures. At 360 pixels, `scrollWidth <= clientWidth` on every page type, with the fixture URLs replaced by a 600 character URL. Before the fix, the document change, statement, and vote pages measured 1153 pixels. After it, all measure the screen width. At 900 and 1000 pixels, no word breaks inside a table cell. The check used long sponsorship and office values.

The public header is sticky above 760 pixels width. `html` sets `scroll-padding-top` to the topbar token plus `--s3`, which is 72 pixels. The 760 pixel block resets it to 0, because the header is static there. So every jump to an anchor stops below the header: the skip link to `#main`, the citation links, and the section links. The review top bar uses the same token and gets the same rule. No test for this CSS rule. Checked: Blink at 1280 pixels. The target top was 72 pixels and the header bottom was 65 pixels, so the real gap is 7 pixels.

The method page and the about page use `Term` for the first use of the terms they name. A record page does the same for these words. They are the vote kind, the executive action type, and the cast words Yea, Nay, Present, and Not voting. They also include the word correction when the correction has a note, and the word statement in the related panel. The executive action page also names the Federal Register as a term in its evidence paragraph. The Federal Register label in the facts stays plain text. A presidential notice shows the label and the term "Presidential notice", not the agency term "Notice". The official page does it for the casts in the table of votes against the party. A `Fact` with a `TermKey` shows its value as a `Term`. `CastWord` shows a cast word as a `Term`. `PublishedRecordLabels` owns the map from a kind or a cast to a term key. A popover is a `button` with `popovertarget`, so keyboard and touch readers open it without a script. Without popover support the page hides the popover, and the word looks like plain text and does nothing when pressed. The glossary page holds every definition. No test for this CSS rule. The glossary page shows the group headings, the entry labels "Means", "Why it matters", and "Source", and the rules for how party votes are counted.

### Public base URL

`CIVIC_LENS_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` is the public origin of the site, for example `https://civic-lens.info`. `PublicBaseUrl` parses it once per process and joins it with a release path. `ReleaseRenderer` holds it and gives it to `PublicPage` through the page services. Each page passes only its own release path as `CanonicalPath`. The `404.html` page passes none, so it has no canonical link.

When the variable is set, `ReleaseSite` wraps the staging directory and records each page path. Before the manifest, it writes `404.html`, `sitemap.xml`, and `robots.txt`.
