The sitemap lists the recorded pages in ordinal order. When the variable is unset, the release is the same as before this setting.

### Report a problem

Every release holds `report.html`, rendered by `PublishedReportPage`. Every record page links to it once from `PublishedRecordFrame`, with `recordId` and `revision` in the query. The names come from `ReaderReportProtocol`. The page holds a category list, a text field, and the Turnstile widget. The text is required for the category "other". The form stays hidden until `public.js` runs. Without JavaScript the page says that a report needs it. A page opened with no valid record shows the panel "Choose the record first". It names the link "Report a problem with this record". A separate panel, "A problem with the site", gives the contact email address. It shows in every state, also when reporting is not available or JavaScript is off. `PublishedRecordLabels.ContactEmail` owns the address. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.TheReportPageWithNoRecordSaysWhichLinkToUseAndGivesTheSiteContactAddressInEveryState`. It checks the panel title, the link text, and the address. It also checks that the panel of the address has no `hidden` attribute and no `data-report-missing`, with and without a service address. `public.js` shows the form only for a valid record ID and revision. It posts to the submit path of `ReaderReportProtocol` at `CIVIC_LENS_PUBLIC_API_URL` and shows a message for 202, 400, 404, 413, 429, 503, and a failed request. For a valid record, it links back to the record page. Publish reads the public site key from `CIVIC_LENS_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`. Without that variable or the service address, the page says that reporting is not available. The Turnstile script loads on this page only, through the `ExternalScript` parameter of `PublicPage`. Public pages set no content security policy.

### Issue panel

Each action and each vote chooses its issues separately. One bill can show different issues on its action and its vote, or on two roll calls. The sponsored and voted counts can then disagree for that bill.

The current page of an official with a vote file shows the issue chips in its section "Issues they work on". The tab "Issues they work on" of its record page opens a pane. The pane has a row for each tracked issue and three plain counts. The counts are "Their statements", "Bills they sponsored", and "Votes they cast". The row lists the official title of each sponsored bill. The window is the current Congress of the official. The key figures state the voted share and the share with the party of each chamber. The panel "What they said and what they did" follows the sections. It lists the approved links from the statements of the official to bills and votes. The issue pane links to `method.html`. The record section has a tab that hides every row that is not a vote against most of the party of the official. Each nonzero count of the issue table is a button that filters the record list to the records of that count. Code builds the profile from the manifest entries of the official and the vote rows of the current Congress. It uses no model.

Every release holds `method.html`, rendered by `PublishedMethodPage`. Its title is "How the counts work", and the footer link uses the same words. It states where Senate votes come from, including Voteview and its missing Vice President tie-break vote. It states what the three issue counts mean, the window, the substantive vote kinds, and the source of the issues. It also states how the votes with their party are counted and defines a split vote. It states what a link is and the four link words, and that a link is not a verdict. It also states that a reviewer approves the issue tags of a statement and that some tags can be wrong. For a sponsored bill or a vote on a bill, the policy area in Congress.gov gives the possible issues. An AI model chooses the issues of a bill or vote. It reads the title, the policy area, and the subjects that Congress.gov lists for the bill, when there are any. It says that for a statement that code approves, an AI model finds the issues and quotes the article, and code checks only the quote. Test: `ReleaseRendererTests.TheMethodPageSaysThatNoPersonChecksTheIssuesOfACodeApprovedStatementAndThatCodeChecksOnlyTheQuote`. A bill with no fitting issue shows no issue. Subjects cover only some bills. No person checks that pick. Older records list every possible issue.

### Search

Every release holds `search.html`, rendered by `PublishedSearchPage`. Every public page links to it. The page fills the kind, official, issue, policy area, and source filters from the manifest and the catalog names. A script on the page calls the [search](search.md) service at `CIVIC_LENS_PUBLIC_API_URL`, which publish reads. The form stays hidden until `public.js` runs, because search needs JavaScript. The page address holds `q` and the seven filters, never a cursor or a release, so a reader can share a search. A submit writes browser history; typing does not. See [search](search.md#search-page). Without the variable, the page says that search is not available. A method note on the page states the source text limit.

The all-records pages open with one line. The line starts with the words "Search by kind". Then it holds one link for each record kind that the release holds, and a link that says "Filter by issue or date". A release without records has no line. A kind link goes to `search.html?kind=<kind>`. No link is current, because the row is not a filter of the list. `PublishedRecordLabels.KindPlural` owns the plural kind labels, and `PublicationProtocol.KindText` owns the query value. The search page form and the search service use the same value. Without a search service, the linked page says that search is not available.

### Public API views

R-68 plans to serve public data from tables. The schema `public_api` holds readable views for that path. The views cover votes, bills, ZIP districts, and release names. `PublicApi` reads `active_records`, `votes`, `vote_issues`, `vote_members`, `bills`, `bill_sponsorships`, and `bill_issues`. It serves `/v1/release`, `/v1/votes`, `/v1/votes/{recordId}`, `/v1/votes/{recordId}/members`, `/v1/bills`, `/v1/bills/{recordId}`, and `/v1/bills/{recordId}/sponsorships`. See [search](search.md#api).

- The view `public_api.active_records` joins the active row of `publication_releases` with `publication_record_states`. A record belongs to release N when `first_release <= N` and `last_release` is null or at least N. The view shows kind, record ID, revision number, release number, and the publication time of the release as `published_at_utc_ticks`. `/v1/release` reads the number and the time from it. The migration `PublicApiReleaseTime` adds the time and grants the owner that one column of `publication_releases`. A release with no record shows no row, so it reads as no active release. Every record view joins it. The six name views join the active row of `publication_releases` directly, and `zip_districts` joins neither. For an empty active release, `active_records` has no row but the name views show its names. The migration `ReleaseNames` owns this join. No function and no stored copy exist.
- The view `public_api.votes` has one row for each active vote. It shows the roll call, the bill, the headline, the summary, `text_origin`, `label`, the four counts, `question`, and `result`. The counts come from the member rows by cast. `Yea` and `Aye` count as yea. `Nay` and `No` count as nay. A vote revision with no member list appears in no vote view.
- The columns `question` and `result` read the same columns of `vote_revisions`. `question` is null when the source gives no question. `result` is never null. `VoteFacts` owns the rule that takes both from the facts, and `VoteRevisionRows` writes them once. The migration `VoteQuestionResultColumns` filled existing rows from the fact rows and replaced the view. The SQL of the view holds no prefix.
- The column `label` holds the public wording of each origin. A model text gets "An AI summary of the official record". A code text gets "Written automatically from Congress.gov data, with no AI". A person text has no label, as on the pages. `PublishedRecordLabels` owns the wording.
- The view `public_api.vote_members` shows position, official ID, and cast. The official ID is our configured official ID, the same ID as `PublishedOfficial.Id`. No view shows a bioguide ID. The view `public_api.vote_issues` shows the issue ID of tracked issues only. Neither view shows a citation offset.
- The view `public_api.bills` has one row for each active bill record. It shows the bill identity (`congress`, `bill_type`, `bill_number`), the title, the headline, and the summary. It also shows `text_origin`, `label`, the policy area name and ID, `event_date`, `sponsor_count`, and `cosponsor_count`. Routes key a bill by record ID and filter by the bill identity. The label wording is the same as in `votes`.
- The columns `event_date` and `cosponsor_count` read `action_revisions.event_on` and `action_revisions.cosponsor_count`. `ActionRevisionRows` writes both once, from `ActionDraftRevision.EventOn` and `ActionDraftRevision.CosponsorCount`. The view computes neither, so the index `ix_action_revisions_event_on` can serve an order by date. Only `sponsor_count` comes from the sponsorship rows, as a count of the role `Sponsor`.
- The view `public_api.bill_sponsorships` shows one row for each sponsorship: position, official ID, role, `is_original`, `sponsored_on`, and `withdrawn_on`. The official ID is the configured ID, as in `vote_members`. No view shows a bioguide ID. The view `public_api.bill_issues` shows the issue ID and position of tracked issues only.
- The three bill views show a revision only when the revision has a headline, an `event_on` value, and a sponsorship row. An older revision holds `revision_json` and no columns. A revision with columns and no derived columns waits for the backfill. All three views use the same filter, so a child view never shows a record that `bills` hides.
- The six views `release_officials`, `release_official_terms`, `release_issues`, `release_issue_includes`, `release_issue_excludes`, and `release_policy_areas` show the names of the active release. Each row carries the `release_number`. The issue view shows the `definition` as verbatim text. The includes and excludes are child rows with a `position`. No route reads them yet. The tables are in [Storage and contracts](#storage-and-contracts).
- The view `public_api.zip_districts` has one row for each ZCTA, state, and district of the newest district snapshot. It shows `land_area_square_meters` and `land_share`. It reads the district tables and the attempt rows, not the publication tables. See [districts](districts.md#decisions).
- The view owner has no grant on `vote_revision_facts`. The migration `VoteQuestionResultColumns` revoked the old grant. No view shows a citation offset or a citation extraction ID.
- No view shows a suggestion column, a note, a reviewer, an actor, or a JSON column. The base grants of the view owner list only the columns that the views read.
- The migration `PublicApiViews` creates the schema and the four record views in one transaction. The migration `PublicApiZipDistricts` adds `zip_districts`. A NOLOGIN role `civic_lens_public_owner` owns the views, and they use `security_barrier`. A failed migration creates no view. A later migration that drops a column a view reads fails.
- `PublicApiGrants` owns every role and privilege statement. It creates the role `civic_lens_public` without LOGIN and without a password, and it sets the role limits. It grants CONNECT, USAGE on the schema, SELECT on the views, and the column grants of the view owner. It revokes `TEMPORARY` and the advisory lock functions from `PUBLIC`. `db migrate` runs it after the migrations, every time, and every statement is idempotent. A restore with `--no-privileges`, a rollback, or a new migration cannot leave the grants behind, because the next `db migrate` sets them again. The step holds no secret, and a role belongs to the cluster, so the operator enables the login. See [public role login](../operations.md#public-role-login).
- The migration creates only the owner role, because the views need it when they are created. `readonly-grants.sql` does not revoke `TEMPORARY`. `PublicApiGrants` owns that revoke for the whole database, so run `db migrate` before the installer.
- Tradeoff: the views live in the operational database. An outage of that database stops the live API, while the cached pages stay up.

## Storage and contracts

- The generated [publication schema](schema/publication.md) lists every table, column, key, check, index, and trigger.
- The trigger `trg_<table>_publishing_notify` on each of the seven `*_drafts` tables calls `civic_lens_notify_publishing_approval`. The generated schema docs of the draft tables list it.
- The generated schema docs do not list views. [public-api-columns.txt](public-api-columns.txt) lists every column of every `public_api` view.
- The state table lists the records of each release as kind, record ID, and revision. It does not list withdrawn records.
- The table `published_records` holds one row for each record that was ever published: `kind`, `record_id`, and `first_published_at_utc_ticks`. The key is `kind` and `record_id`. `kind` uses the same spelling as `publication_record_states`. Code inserts rows and never changes them. A rollback keeps them. The migration `PublishedRecords` creates the table empty. `releases first-published backfill` fills it from the active manifest; see [operations](../operations.md#first-publication-backfill).
- Six tables hold the names of each release: `release_officials`, `release_official_terms`, `release_issues`, `release_issue_includes`, `release_issue_excludes`, and `release_policy_areas`. Each key starts with `release_number`, which has a foreign key to `publication_releases`. An official row holds the ID, the name, the Wikidata ID, and the website. A term row holds the office, kind, state, district, party, caucus, and the start and end dates. An issue row holds the name and the definition. The definition text is the only verbatim text. The migration `ReleaseNames` creates the tables and the views. Publish writes the rows in the commit transaction, so a release with no records still writes them. A release that an older image published has no name rows, and its views stay empty.
- The table `published_entries` holds one row for each record revision that a release added. The key is `kind`, `record_id`, and `revision_number`. The row holds the scalar facts of the revision: headline, source host, event date, bill title, document type, and the link columns. It also holds seven pairs of nullable columns, one pair for each revision table. The pair of the entry kind is set, equals the key, and has a foreign key to the revision table. The entry has no first publication time, because `published_records` owns it. The link columns `statement_record_id`, `target_kind`, and `target_record_id` have foreign keys to `published_records`. The column `statement_kind` is generated. The matched official of a relationship entry is the row of `published_entry_officials` with `is_matched` set. The arrays are in `published_entry_officials` (ID only), `published_entry_issues`, and `published_entry_policy_areas`. Each has the entry key and `position`. The tables hold no official name, no vote count, no alignment, no chamber, and no cosponsor. The migration `PublishedEntries` creates the tables empty. Nothing reads them yet. `releases entries backfill` fills the rows of record revisions from earlier releases. A revision that no kept folder lists gets no row.
- The table `publication_record_states` holds one row for each range of releases in which a record has one revision: `kind`, `record_id`, `revision_number`, `first_release`, and `last_release`. A null `last_release` means the range is open, so the record is still in the highest release. A withdrawn record has no open range, and the manifest holds the withdrawal details. `kind` uses the wire spelling of the manifest, which `PublishedEnumJsonConverter` owns. `PublishedRecordReference.NameOf` must match it. A record that leaves and returns has two rows. The state at release R is the rows with `first_release <= R` and a null `last_release` or `last_release >= R`. A commit reads the open rows, which are the highest release, and writes only the records that entered, left, or changed revision. Activate and prune write nothing to the table. Review queries and the brief queries read this table. The migration `DropPublicationRecordsJson` removed the column `records_json`.
- The table `publication_withdrawals` holds one row for each range of releases that list one withdrawal of a record. The columns are `kind`, `record_id`, `withdrawn_revision_number`, `withdrawn_at_utc_ticks`, `note`, `first_release`, and `last_release`. The key is `kind`, `record_id`, and `first_release`. One foreign key points to `published_records`. Two foreign keys point to `publication_releases`. A null `last_release` means the range is open. The range rule is the same as in `publication_record_states`. The table `publication_withdrawal_corrections` holds the corrections of a withdrawal. A row holds the key of the withdrawal, `position`, `replaced_revision_number`, `published_at_utc_ticks`, `note`, and `facts_unchanged`. The migration `PublicationWithdrawals` creates both tables empty.
- The manifest `release.json` holds `records` and `withdrawals`. A withdrawal holds the kind, the record ID, the withdrawn revision, the first publication time, the withdrawal time, the note, and the corrections. The field `withdrawals` is required. A returned record leaves `withdrawals`.
- The ready lists read the active row and match by kind, so a statement ID cannot hide a document change.
- `bill-status.json` is part of each release and is optional for a reader. It holds `records`, a list of `recordId`, `text`, `date`, and `observedAtUtc`. The search service never reads it.
- The `photo` of an official profile is null when the official has no photo. It is a fact about the official, not a field for an older version. The manifest and the record files carry no optional field that exists for an older version. The exceptions are `source` of a vote record and the entry fields `yeaCount`, `nayCount`, `alignment`, `chamber`, and `cosponsorIds`. Each manifest entry requires `kind`, `officials`, `issueIds`, and `policyAreaIds`. Each vote record and each row of the official vote files holds `chamber` (`house` or `senate`). Each vote record holds `source` (`congressGov` or `voteview`). A vote file with no `source` reads as `congressGov`. Each row of the official vote files holds `kind` and `issueIds`. Each of the seven record files holds `corrections`, and the manifest holds `withdrawals`; see [Corrections and withdrawal](#corrections-and-withdrawal). The files `officials.json`, `officials-summary.json`, `catalog.json`, and `officials/{id}/votes-{congress}.json` are part of each release. Action, vote, and statement records require `textOrigin` (`model`, `person`, or `code`). Only action records hold `code`. An action record may hold an optional `billCitation` for the title and introduced lines of the bill page. It counts as a citation, so every source stays cited. Statement records also require `approvedBy` (`code` or `person`). The version stays 1, because production holds live releases and every new field is optional. `PublishedOfficeTerm` allows a null party and a null end, and it requires `kind`. Readers accept version 1 only. An older release, or one with a term without `kind`, is not readable; see [Dev database reset](../operations.md#dev-database-reset).
- `PublishedOfficials` and `PublishedCatalog` each have a `Validate` method. `IssueConfiguration` refuses an issue or policy area name over 256 characters, so a catalog entry never fails the publish. Officials are capped at `PublishedOfficials.MaximumOfficials`, each official at `PublishedOfficials.MaximumTerms` terms, the catalog at `PublishedCatalog.MaximumEntries` entries in each list, and every text value at 256 characters. The search service ignores both files. `PublishedOfficialVotes` is capped at `PublishedOfficialVotes.MaximumVotes` rows and `PublishedOfficialVotes.MaximumBytes` bytes, and its rows hold one Congress. A row allows 64 issue IDs of 256 characters, so `PublishedOfficialVotes.MaximumBytesPerVote` is 52 KB, which allows 3 bytes for each character. `PublishedOfficialSummaries` is capped at `PublishedOfficials.MaximumOfficials` rows and `PublishedOfficialSummaries.MaximumBytes` bytes. A row holds at most `PublishedOfficialSummary.MaximumRows` rows of each kind.

| Filter | Document change | Statement | Action | Brief | Vote | Relationship |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `officials` (ID and configured name) | Officials | Speakers | The sponsor, when tracked | Empty | Empty | The speakers of the statement |
| `issueIds` | Issue IDs | Issue IDs | Tracked issue IDs | Empty | Tracked issue IDs | The issue IDs of both sides |
| `policyAreaIds` | Empty | Policy area IDs | The policy area ID stored with the approved revision, or empty | Empty | Empty | The policy area IDs of both sides |
| `sourceHost` | Host of the after version URL | Host of the source URL | Host of the first source URL | None | Host of the first source URL | None |
| `eventDate` | Evidenced change date, or empty | Evidenced statement date, or empty | Latest sponsored or withdrawn date | The Monday that starts the week | The vote day | The statement date, or empty |

- `src/CivicLens.Publication.Contracts/PublicationProtocol.cs` owns the version; see [Behavior versions](../architecture.md#behavior-versions).
- Each release also holds `release.json.gz` and `release.json.br`, at `PublicationProtocol.ManifestGzipPath` and `ManifestBrotliPath`. They compress the exact manifest bytes, so a static server can send them without compressing on each request.
- A separate static file server serves the release root read-only. It has no access to operational storage.

Release and rollback commands are in [operations](../operations.md#publish-a-static-release). Backup, retention, and monitoring rules are in [Limits and planned work](#limits-and-planned-work).

## Limits and planned work

- The entry tables hold the revision facts of the manifest entries. No reader uses them. `releases entries backfill` fills the revisions of earlier releases from the kept folders, but a revision that only a retired folder held has no row. The backfill reads the whole state table in one query, so its memory use grows with the number of revision ranges. The limit bounds only the inserts. Each run reads every kept manifest that covers a range without a row. It also reads the first record file of each such range. `releases entries verify` proves that the tables rebuild the kept manifests. It does not compute the left-out fields from their owners. So it cannot show how often a view would differ from the published name, vote count, alignment, or cosponsor list. A later `officials` view supplies the names, and the public views compute the vote counts, the alignment, and the cosponsors. Then the change adds the foreign key from `publication_record_states`. A relationship entry needs `published_records` rows for its statement and target. Until `releases first-published backfill` has run, the commit stops with `FirstPublishedMissingException` for a target of an earlier release.
- `releases entries verify` writes each stored manifest again with the current serializer. An older image can write a release before a manifest field exists, for example the entry `chamber` field. That release fails with `manifestNotReproducible`, although its entries are correct. Only the newest kept release proves the current tables, so check that release. Old releases that fail this way need no repair. No test.
- An entry field that a later contract change adds needs a new column. Existing rows keep it null until a one-time fill.
- The vote views `votes`, `vote_members`, and `vote_issues` leave out a vote revision that has no member list. A revision from before the columns were written has none, and the views cannot show it. Test: `PublicApiViewsTests.AVoteRevisionWithNoWrittenColumnsAppearsInNoVoteViewBecauseItHoldsNothingToShow`.
- `PublicApiGrants` revokes privileges on system functions and on the database, so `db migrate` needs a superuser. R-24 revisits this for a managed database, where the owner may not be a superuser.
- The withdrawal tables are written beside the manifest and no reader uses them. Withdrawals made before the migration have no rows. The first commit after the migration opens a row for each carried withdrawal at its own release number, so `first_release` is wrong for them. The backfill change must fix those rows and fill the earlier ranges.
- The withdrawal tables have no foreign key to the entry table. A later change adds the key from `kind`, `record_id`, and `withdrawn_revision_number`.
- The weekly brief of a record still comes from `BriefFirstRelease`, which reads the release rows. It does not read `published_records`. A later change can move it to the table.
- Until `releases first-published backfill` runs, a publish that adds a record of the active release stops. A publish whose release lists any withdrawal, carried or new, stops the same way. `PublishRelease.RequireFirstPublished` owns this check. On a deploy that adds the migration `PublicationWithdrawals`, the operator stops the worker, migrates, runs the backfill, and starts the worker. A record that no active entry or withdrawal names has no row until a publish adds it.
- When every row of a chamber holds it only in `previous`, `ShownCongress` can pick an older Congress than the figures allow. This edge case is not fixed.
- A page that a release carries by hard link keeps the date form that it was written with until a publish writes it again.
- The code text of an action is stored in its revision. A revision written before this form keeps "Introduced May 21, 2025" until a change to the bill writes a new revision.
- New vote kinds and treaty votes wait for a contract choice.
- The status of a bill reaches the site only in a publish. The worker checks for a status change once for each publish tick. The first publish after the backfill finds no earlier row, so it writes every action page that has a status. That is one page render for each such action.
- The status backfill reads saved captures, so a backfilled status is as old as the capture. A bill that no later update touched keeps that status and its read time.
- A record page that a release carries by hard link, and an official page that a publish does not touch, keep their old markup. They have no glossary footer link and no terms until the page is rewritten.
- The first use of a term follows the render order of the page, not the reading order.
- The lede of an official page depends on the release date. A page that a release carries by hard link keeps its lede until a publish writes it again. So a term that ends later can leave "Representative" in the lede until the profile or its records change. The officials list is written in every publish and has no such delay.
- A layout change reaches a page only when a publish writes the page. A plain publish writes the assets, the home page, the all-records pages, the issue pages, and the other index pages. It does not write carried record pages and untouched official pages. A carried page keeps its old markup. It has no `class="public"` on `body`, so it has no new palette and no navy header. Its font request is for Instrument Sans, which the release no longer holds. The request returns 404, so the page uses the system font. Paging does not run, because the old table has no `data-paged`. The new styles still reach it, because the asset names are fixed. After the deploy that ships the new header, footer, icons, and paging, run `releases publish <idempotency-key> --rewrite-pages --rewrite-officials` once.
- The Congress tabs, the profile header, and the bill status lines reach an official only when a publish writes the page of that official. After the deploy that ships them, the owner runs `releases publish <idempotency-key> --rewrite-officials` once. An untouched official otherwise keeps the old footer panel and the old header and has no bill status lines. After `releases activate` of a release older than the rewrite, the next publish carries the old layout pages again, so the owner must rewrite again. The same holds for `--rewrite-entries` and `--rewrite-pages`: after `releases activate` of a release older than that rewrite, run the rewrite options again. Use a new idempotency key each time, because the same key and payload replay the old result with no rewrite.
- A release carries unchanged pages from an earlier release by hard link. Such a page keeps the head it was written with until a publish writes it again. A carried page therefore keeps its canonical address. Set `CIVIC_LENS_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` before the first production publish. A change of domain needs `releases publish <idempotency-key> --rewrite-officials` to write the official pages again. The carried record pages stay linked from the earlier release with the old head. `releases publish <idempotency-key> --rewrite-pages` writes them again with the new head. The record data files stay linked. Every other page gets the new head at the next publish.
- An image from before the vote and cosponsor entry fields and `billLine` cannot read a release that a newer image made. `UnmappedMemberHandling.Disallow` refuses the new members, and a new publish writes them in every entry, as null when empty. A new manifest writes `billLine` as null in an entry with no line. It also refuses the `previous` member of a summary row. To go back to the older image, first activate a release that it made.
- `cosponsorIds` and the cosponsor count leave out a withdrawn cosponsorship, and leave out an official who is also a sponsor of the same action. A sponsor list still includes a withdrawn sponsorship. The count covers every Congress that the release holds. No page uses either yet.
- Vote pills and counts show in rows that the page holds in its HTML. The rows that the browser builds after a sort, and the search page, come from the search index, which does not hold the vote fields. Those rows show no pill until the index gets the fields. Rows of actions keep their bill line, because the index holds it. Vote rows show none. A carried vote entry shows a pill only after `releases publish --rewrite-entries` fills its fields. The sort status line says "Sorted rows leave out the kind icon, the party badge, the vote counts, and the bill line of a vote."
- The bill line comes only from the CRS summary of the action. The first-sentence rule of `BillLine` is a stopgap. Planned work: a later PR replaces it with a model line that carries a quote. Code checks the quote against the CRS summary. The line has the label "AI summary of the Congressional Research Service summary." and a link to the full summary. An action with no CRS summary has no line until then.
- The bill line shows in the record table, which lists actions and votes. It also shows in the official record rows of actions, the home latest votes, the closest vote card, and the vote page. Vote rows of the official pages wait for a measure of the re-render cost. The related panel is planned. The official issue pane shows no line, by decision.
- The break card reads the summary rows. An untouched official keeps the row of the previous release, so a figure can be older than the newest roll call. An official with no vote figures is not counted, so the card cannot tell no breaks from no figures. The card names the Congress and not the day of the newest roll call. An official with no summary row is never named.
- An official with no vote file has no current Congress, so the page shows no issue panel. A statement with no day counts in the Congress of its first publication day.
- A House resolution roll call whose bill title has not arrived is classed as Passage, so it counts as substantive; see the [votes limit](votes.md#limits-and-planned-work).
- Missed votes count only the roll calls on this site. A roll call whose vote did not publish, such as a blocked or refused vote, is in neither the missed count nor the held count.
- The profile counts a roll call once. Any cast other than "Not Voting" counts, so a House free-text cast counts. A sponsorship counts in the Congress of its latest sponsorship day, which can be a withdrawal day.
- Real bill titles have at most 544 characters in development data. A release where every action entry has a 4,000 character title is over 64 MiB. The publish then refuses it with a clear error.
- A row of an official vote file can hold 64 issue IDs of 256 characters. The read cap of a file is therefore about 266 MB. Real issue IDs have at most 60 characters.
- Each release links its photo files, so a photo file stays on disk while any kept release uses it. A prune removes it with the last release that links it. A takedown of one photo sets `withholdPhoto` and needs a new publish. Kept releases keep the file until a prune removes them; see [operations](../operations.md#take-down-a-member-photo). A withheld photo and withdrawn content can stay in a kept release folder with no time limit. A prune removes a folder only when four things are true. At least 30 higher release numbers exist. The release is not the highest of a UTC day in the last 7 days. The folder is not the active or served folder. A later publish runs the prune. Seven days is the minimum for the highest release of a day. It is not a maximum for any folder. Restic backups can keep `releases/` for up to 6 months (see [operations](../operations.md#backups)). The search index keeps withdrawn rows (see [search](search.md#invariants)). A rollback to such a release shows them again.
- A change only to the tracked state of a person or to an issue name is not a profile change. A publish with no record refuses it.
- The host keeps the 30 highest release numbers and the newest release of each of the last 7 UTC days. It prunes the other release directories. It keeps the active release and the release that `current` serves. A rollback can reach only a kept release. During a burst of publishes, the 30 newest releases can span a few minutes. A rollback to a state before the burst then reaches only the last release of each earlier day, up to 6 days back. A drain publishes about one release every 7 seconds. Each release is about 29 MB in development. It is not measured whether that figure is the total size or the size that carried files do not share. The rule keeps at most 36 folders, plus the active and served folders when they are older. At 29 MB without sharing, 36 folders are about 1.0 GB, and 38 folders are about 1.1 GB. The daily releases need 7 days to build after the first deploy, so full rollback reach takes 7 days to appear. Hard links share carried files, so the real size is lower. A backlog makes several releases in one tick. A prune does not remove orphan folders in `staging/`, which a crash leaves.
- The loader drops relationships and briefs that are not ready after the store has alternated replacements and new records. After a mass change of citations, many drops can break the alternation. Replacements can then fill more than half of a batch. The next tick reads again without the dropped rows.
- A record that cannot build still takes a place in the 64 records that the tick lists. More than 63 such records in one kind can hide other records until a person fixes them.
- A ready record that cannot build is logged at error level at every tick until a person fixes it. A store read that fails, for example on a changed citation, fails the tick.
- After a rollback and a CLI publish, the rolled-back records list again with the same key. The stage replays the stored release each tick and publishes nothing until new records change the batch. Publish those records with the CLI.
- The stage has no status row. Its loop writes the `publishing` heartbeat; see [collection](collection.md#autonomous-collection-engine).
- A vote record embeds at most `PublishedVote.MaximumSources` sources: the detail, the members, and the bill detail.
- An action record embeds at most `PublishedAction.MaximumSources` sources. The worst case is the detail page, 3 cosponsor pages (250 rows), and the summaries page, which is 5 sources. A record over the cap fails publish loudly.
- The stage `publishing` leaves out a withdrawn record that is approved again at the withdrawn revision, by code, or by a person without a note. It logs one line for each skipped record at every tick, until a person approves a higher revision with a note.
- A returned record counts against the cap of 20 notes. A record with 20 notes cannot return yet.
- The worker or the CLI returns a record. The CLI and Take off site on the record editor withdraw.
- A relationship or a brief that cited a withdrawn record stays withdrawn when the record returns. A person must publish it again.
- A withdrawn record still answers with its notice page and status 200. A static 410 needs a Cloudflare rule, which comes with [R-24](../roadmap.md#later). The search service has the same limit; see [search](search.md). The notice page has `noindex`, so search engines drop it.
- The withdrawal check of citations reads every carried brief file when the request withdraws a statement, a document change, or an action. The cost grows with the number of briefs.
- Kept release directories keep the withdrawn content until a prune removes them. A pinned search release can still find the record; see [search](search.md).
- The privacy table of `about.html` says "Nothing" for a page read. Its search row says "Nothing" too. Both stay true only while the Caddy deploy keeps no access logs for page reads or search requests. The Caddyfile sets that; see [Web server](../operations.md#web-server).
- `about.html` says a backup can keep a closed report for up to 6 months after the review system deletes it. That stays true only while the nightly backups run and `backup.sh` keeps 6 monthly snapshots; see [Backups](../operations.md#backups).
- A replacement reads the record file of each replaced record in the active release, to copy its corrections. The cost grows with the number of replaced records, at most 64.
- A record keeps at most 20 corrections with a note. A person cannot publish a 21st correction of one record yet. Code updates still publish.
- A rollback followed by a publish drops the rolled-back revision and its note from the history. The list copies forward from the active release. A rollback to a release before a withdrawal restores the record. A publish from there drops the withdrawal history.
- Only the statement page labels a statement date that code read. The link page, the paired day on an official page, and the link editor show that date with no label. `PublishedRelationship` carries no `approvedBy` for its statements yet.
- The record page of an official lists every entry that names the official, 15 rows a page in the browser. A relationship or a document change that names the official appears with the other entries. The official page shows only the newest five items of each section.
- A replaced vote touches its old members in every Congress of the replaced votes of that publish. The result is correct, but a publish may rewrite identical files.
- An official who is configured again keeps the "no longer tracked" notice until a publish touches the official. The profile does not record the notice.
- Votes of one day on an official page follow the record ID, not the roll call number.
- Record pages stay in one file. Add year pages only if a measured record page grows too large.
- A related list orders links by the statement date. On a statement page every link has the same date, so the order there falls back to the link ID. Dates of the target records are not in the relationship file.
- A publish that adds or replaces a statement, action, vote, or relationship reads the carried relationship files that cite those records. It also sends the IDs of all relationships in the release to one database query. The cost of the query grows with the number of published relationships.
- A carried vote page lists a bill action link only when the release holds an action of that bill. A later action does not write the vote page again.
- A relationship flagged "no longer matches" still publishes. Only a person withdraws it. The owner sees an approved link with the flag as a row in the Needs a person queue; see [review](review.md#needs-a-person-queue). The review editor shows the flag. The `releases publish` command shows no flag, because it names records by ID and prints only the release summary.
- A relationship waits until both cited records are in the active release. This follows the rule for briefs. A later wake publishes the relationship after the cited records.
- A record with 20 corrections that have a note cannot take another person revision. For an action, a vote, a statement, or an executive action, only a code-approved revision can publish. For other kinds, no new revision can publish. The worker logs the record and skips it at every tick. The Review home page and `releases blocked` list it.
- A withdrawn record with 20 corrections that have a note cannot return. A person approval with a note raises `CorrectionLimitException`, one without a note raises `MissingCorrectionNoteException`, and a code approval raises `WithdrawnRecordException`. The blocked list shows it when a person approved a higher revision. No cap-free way out exists yet.
- The approve page shows the correction note box for every approval, also for a first publication, because the page does not read the active release. A note on a first publication is dropped.
- Expandable evidence and shareable records are planned against published releases.
- A facet page lists the officials and the event day of each record. It does not list the issues of a record. The all-records page follows the same rule and shows the event day under "Date". A row in the latest lists of the home page shows the event day under its title.
- R-68 plans to replace static public serving. `PublicApi` will read `public_api` views through a role that can read nothing else. The release manifest will then stop being a data path. The views for votes and bills exist, and `PublicApi` serves the vote list, one vote, and the bill routes from them.
- The manifest is one file, and each publish reads the previous one. R-63 moves its entries to tables, so a publish no longer reads it. Release 910 held 18,281 records in 14.9 MB, about 815 bytes for each record.
- At 40,000 action entries with 15 cosponsor IDs each, the manifest is 52,280,111 bytes (about 49.9 MiB), about 78% of the 64 MiB cap. That leaves about 370 bytes of headroom for each entry. The 15 IDs and the 114-character title are assumed inputs, not measured from data. `PublicationReleaseTests.AManifestOfTheMaximumNumberOfRealisticActionEntriesWithCosponsorsStaysUnderTheManifestByteCap` measures it. `PublishRelease` refuses a manifest over the cap.
- A facet ID that the catalog does not name still gets pages, and they show the ID. This keeps a carried record from blocking a later publish after an issue leaves the configuration. `PublicationProtocol.MaximumFacetIdLength` is the one length limit. `IssueConfiguration` applies it to configured issue and policy area IDs, so the configuration fails to load on a longer ID. A carried manifest ID can still be longer or can hold other characters. Such a facet ID gets no pages. The facet list shows its ID with no link.
- The manifest filter fields stay, because the facet pages and the search service use them.
- A deploy rollback to an image from before the migration `DropPublicationRecordsJson` cannot run until an explicit EF rollback of that migration happens. That image reads and writes `records_json`, and the column does not exist. The `Down` of the migration adds the column and rebuilds it from `publication_record_states` for every release, in the shape `kind`, `recordId`, and `revisionNumber`. The order of the entries in a release is by kind and then by record ID, because the table does not keep the original order. `db migrate` only moves forward.
- `deploy/scripts/update.sh` stops the worker and the review host before `db migrate`, so no old image commits a release during the migration. A manual migration needs the same stops. Do not run `releases publish` during `update.sh`, because that one-off container uses the old image.
- A future live public Blazor deployment must read published releases only and stay separate from the operational application.
- Each publish rebuilds the home, all-records, search, and facet pages from the manifest in memory. The measured case has 20,000 records, 100 issues, 20 policy areas, 2 issues and 1 policy area for each record, and a local disk. It writes 1,603 page files, which are 400 all-records pages, 1,200 facet pages, the home page, `issues.html`, and `search.html`. They total 33 MB, and rendering and writing them takes about 1.0 second. The file count is one page for each 50 records of each facet, so it grows with the number of facet memberships, not with releases. A record holds up to 64 issue IDs and 64 policy area IDs, so the facet page count has no fixed cap. Only the configured issues keep it low.
- A future mobile client would read public release contracts through an API, not operational storage.
- Public MCP will expose `search_records`, `get_record`, `get_official_timeline`, and `compare_document_versions`. It returns published data with IDs, citations, dates, review status, coverage caveats, and the release ID.
- Public MCP cannot start collection, inference, or edits. It bounds and pages responses, limits requests, and accepts record IDs, not arbitrary fetch URLs. It treats source passages as untrusted attributed content.
- Public MCP will search through the search service API, over the same entries as the website. The website and public MCP will share one release, and clients can pin it across calls.
- Public MCP needs request compute, but never the local database or AI inference.
- Postgres and capture files need coordinated backups and a tested restore. Production backup rules are in [host](host.md#limits-and-planned-work).
- Retention must keep the evidence that published releases reference. Disposable captures follow an explicit policy, which is not agreed yet.
- Monitoring must cover freshness, skipped and deferred work, source failures, storage, and spend.
- See [roadmap](../roadmap.md) for order.

Public MCP acceptance (after the first deploy, R-25):

- [ ] Public tools never access operational data, change records, crawl, or call models.
- [ ] The website and MCP use the same release.

Operations acceptance (part of R-24):

- [ ] Show restore, rollback, release-pinned reads, pagination, bounded traffic behavior, and visible coverage gaps.
